Just a heads-up: this document is a summary of Horikita in the COTE series. Therefore, not only her greatest feats will be reviewed, but all of them good, bad, and even useless ones. The best feats will be in the final summary.

My native language isn't English, it is Spanish, so to convey my ideas I used AI translation because I'm not confident in my English writing. I apologize for that and hope you don't mind.

Summary
________________
FSIQ Categories:
VCI (Verbal Comprehension Index) -

An academically outstanding girl, ranked among the top students in language. Suzune has excellent communication skills and can interact with others effortlessly, demonstrating fluency in both Japanese and English, improving in this last language constantly. Finally, Suzune has the ability to understand exams with just Sae's explanation, identify the legal loopholes in each test, and notice the unmentioned rules.

* Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune confronts Kushida directly, fully aware that an emotional confrontation would be futile and counterproductive. Instead of appealing to feelings, she constructs a closed logical framework that deliberately strips Kushida of maneuvering room, leaving her with only one viable option: accepting the bet.

Suzune frames the conflict as a strictly binary decision, with no gray areas or implicit alternatives:
   * “If you win, I leave.”
   * “If I win, you stop sabotaging the class.”
The purpose of this discourse is not persuasion, but entrapment within a forced choice, where any attempt to evade the decision would function as an implicit admission of moral or strategic defeat.

To finalize the agreement, Suzune introduces a third party with recognized verbal and institutional authority (Manabu) as a guarantor. His presence eliminates the possibility of later reinterpretation, increases the emotional cost of withdrawal, and reinforces the binding weight of the pact—turning the confrontation into an irreversible commitment for Kushida.

* Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): During the Class Poll Exam, Suzune deliberately places herself in the most dangerous possible position: she speaks first, publicly names an expulsion candidate, and sustains her accusation before the entire class—including allies, neutrals, and enemies.

She avoids exaggeration and maintains a cold, clinical, and controlled tone, which enhances her credibility. She tightly regulates the pace of the discourse, does not allow premature interruptions, and ensures that each point is fully closed before moving on, preventing the discussion from becoming chaotic or emotionally driven.

Her argument follows a strict logical structure: she introduces the problem → establishes objective criteria → compares cases → presents evidence → dismantles counterarguments. She also adapts her approach to different individuals:
   * She refutes Hirata without antagonizing him or undermining his moral authority.
   * She neutralizes Sudō by using his own reasoning framework.
   * She weaponizes Kushida’s verbal reputation as indirect evidence, turning her public image against her without making explicit accusations.

* Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11): During the Event Selection Exam, Suzune needs to:
   * Protect Kiyotaka.
   * Avoid suspicion.
   * Force Kiyotaka's appointment as commander without appearing to coerce him.
   * Keep a fragmented class united.
And she accomplishes all of this primarily through persuasive speeches that help her achieve all her objectives.

Personal ranking for the best VCI feats:
Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10) > Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11) > Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > …

VSI (Visual Spatial Index) -
* Club's ghost member Tactic. (Y1V1): At the club fair, Suzune carefully scanned the entire gym. She identified clubs such as the Judo club and, among the plus 100 first-year students and senpais, she spotted her brother before he introduced himself as the student council president.

* Counter Operation friendship. (Y1V1): Suzune visualized all the students inside Café Palette, a place approximately 120-140 square meters in size. She recognized the faces of her classmates sitting at adjacent tables and immediately noticed when one of the tables became available.

* Sudou Trial (Y1V2): When Sae asked for a witness in Sudou's case, Suzune carefully analyzed the reactions of the 39 students in the class. Thanks to her keen observation, she identified Sakura as a witness based on her unusual behavior. Additionally, she noticed that Kiyotaka had reached the same conclusion.

* Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Despite a fever exceeding 38°C that had weakened her for seven days, Suzune follows Ibuki's trail through the forest of an uninhabited island in the dead of night without problems, with no light and her mind and vision clouded.

* Bottle girl (Y1V4.5): When her hand was trapped in a bottle, Suzune visualized every camera in the hallway of her floor to effectively hide her trapped hand. She sensed when someone was approaching and perfectly concealed her hand when entering the elevator, making sure neither the cameras saw her.

* 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): During her practice for the sports festival, Suzune noticed spies from other classes watching them from the windows of their respective classrooms and from a distance. Among all the faces, she realized that none belonged to class C.

* 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): During the sports festival, Suzune realized that she was the target and faced all the riders from class C, dodging their attacks coming from every direction until she managed to grab a ribbon.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): She is able to visualize the entire class and analyze the reactions of each of its members. (63 to 65 square meters, typically with dimensions of 7 x 9 meters)

* Chess Feat (Y1V11): Suzune plays chess for the first time in her real life, with only a week of study against Hashimoto (experienced player) and then Sakayanagi (grandmaster), managing to recognize Hashimoto's spatial offensive style:
   * Territorial expansion
   * Progressive enclosure
Avoiding typical reflex reactions (unnecessary defensive sacrifices) by maintaining positional consistency, even under verbal pressure, and identifying when their spatial advantage begins to consolidate.

Personal ranking for the best VSI feats:
Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3) > Chess Feat (Y1V11) > Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > Sudou Trial (Y1V2) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > Counter Operation friendship. (Y1V1) > Club's ghost member Tactic. (Y1V1) > ….

CPI (Cognitive Proficiency Index) -
* Suzune is able to take exams quickly and efficiently, understanding the questions and finding the answers with ease.

* Counter Operation friendship. (Y1V1): Suzune was able to notice the "friendship operation" and deduce the entire plan just by observing Kushida arrive.

* Fake study group. (Y1V1): Solving three equations in her mind in less than a second:
1) A+B+C = 2150
2) A = B + 120
3) C×2/5 + B = A + 220

By putting A = B + 120 in equation 3, the two B's cancel out, and we get C = 850
Then Putting this value of C in Equation 1, we get A + B = 1300
Then by Substituting A = B + 1300 in Equation 2 we get, B = 590
then Putting this value of B in Equation 2, we get A = 710

The answer is 710 yen. Suzune was able to calculate the answer to Sudou's problem by merely looking at it.

* Sudou Trial (Y1V2): When Sae asked for a witness in Sudou's case, Suzune carefully analyzed the reactions of the 39 students in the class. Thanks to her keen observation, she identified Sakura as a witness based on her unusual behavior and noticed that Kiyotaka had reached the same conclusion.

* She instantly deduced Kiyotaka's intention to go to Sakura's place.

* In Sudou's case, Suzune was momentarily paralyzed, but upon recovering, she analyzed the entire situation and turned it to her advantage. When it seemed like an agreement was about to be reached, she connected all the dots and devised a plan that forced the members of Class C to withdraw their proposal. She also devised a whole strategy to save Sudou in that moment

* Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Suzune fights with Ibuki while she is sick (38°C). Ibuki has slightly superior abilities and Suzune was able to keep up with her in a weak state.

* 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Suzune mentally kept track of the complete matrix of the 40 students in Class D (their physical metrics, ideal positions, pairing lists, and rival interactions) while debating and adjusting the distribution of speaking turns during class discussion periods.

* D class upgrade (Y1V2-now): Suzune has the ability to analyze special exams with just Sae's explanation, identify the legal loopholes in each test, and notice the unmentioned rules in seconds while the explanation is given. Not to mention that right after she is able to analyze what the line she will follow for the exam will be. An example is the Event Selection Exam. Suzune is confronted with an exam defined by extreme cognitive load:
   * Extensive and dense rules.
   * Incomplete and ambiguous information.
   * Hidden variables and undeclared conditions.
   * Simultaneous interaction between multiple classes.
   * The risk of immediate expulsion.
All of this is delivered in a single briefing session, with no prior materials or preparation time. Despite this, Suzune is able to retain and process simultaneously:
   * Ten different events
   * The distinction between five genuine events and five decoys.
   * Participant limits for each event.
   * The strategic role of the commander.
   * The random selection system.
She keeps all of these variables active while questioning Sae, dynamically refining her mental model, and identifying key patterns—bluffs, decoy events, and structural inconsistencies—in real time. She reacts instantly to:
   * Photograph the board.
   * Transmit critical information to Kiyotaka.
   * Reassert control of the classroom when Sudō derails the discussion.
As a result, Suzune:
   * Reconstructs the exam’s hidden system
   * Distinguishes explicit rules from implicit ones.
   * Anticipates how other classes will exploit the format.
Besides, Suzune does not become cognitively overwhelmed. She requires no breaks, does not collapse under complexity, and can think, speak, lead, and make a strategy simultaneously as demonstrated throughout the document

Summary
________________
FSEQ Categories:
EM (Emotional Management) -

Suzune doesn't show many of her emotions throughout any situation she faces; she doesn't stand out for "feeling less," but for not allowing her emotions to alter her performance, even when the cost is personal, social, or irreversible.

* Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Kushida harbors active hatred toward Suzune, resulting in continuous betrayal, espionage, and sabotage. At the same time, Suzune is forced to confront Ryūen—an opponent who understands her deeply—where any single mistake could cost her everything. Despite this, Suzune does not respond to provocations, does not lose focus, consciously accepts becoming the target of Kushida’s hatred, being with Manabu, dealing with Sae's emotions and control her enemies in a scenario where the risk falls almost entirely on her and Kushida holds a clear advantage (allied with Ryūen and competing in her strongest field),

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune voluntarily assumes the role of moral executioner within an emotionally unstable class, fully aware that:
   * She will earn Yamauchi’s open hatred.
   * She may become the next target if she fails.
   * Hirata, Sudō, and part of the class will oppose her emotionally.
   * The exam entails irreversible expulsion.
   * She experiences guilt when sacrificing a classmate.
Despite these factors, Suzune remains unwavering. She does not respond with anger to Yamauchi’s hostility, supports her stance with rational arguments, redirects collective blame toward Kushida and Yamauchi, and neutralizes Hirata’s compassion through logic rather than emotional confrontation. Even by presenting herself as a potential expulsion target, accepting Hirata’s vote against her, and exposing Kushida before the class, Suzune succeeds in altering the emotional stance of 36 students in Class D.

* Chess Feat (Y1V11): In the final special exam of the year, Suzune endures days of pressure from the entire class without Hirata—her primary leadership support—standing by her side. By the time the exam begins, Class A has already secured victories in three events, losing one only because a member refused to participate. In the final event, Suzune must compete in a complex game whose rules she had learned barely a week earlier. For Kiyotaka to have a real chance against the strongest class leader, Suzune had to defeat an opponent with months of experience: an elite student trained by a grandmaster such as Sakayanagi, who also provoked and verbally attacked her throughout. A single misstep would have erased an entire year of effort to reach Class C.

Even under these conditions, Suzune maintains complete emotional control. After the defeats in previous events, frustration does not dominate her, nor does she change her treatment of her teammates. During the chess match, she remains composed against an objectively superior opponent, clearly recognizes when the situation exceeds her capabilities, and does not panic or freeze. Finally, after the ultimate loss, she does not collapse emotionally, does not seek excuses, and integrates the experience as actionable information for the future.

Personal ranking for the best EM feats:
Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > Chess Feat (Y1V11) >...

EQE/EE (Emotional Engagement) -
* D class upgrade (Y1V1-now): Suzune’s primary goal is to reach Class A. This goal is neither superficial nor merely instrumental. For Suzune, reaching Class A represents:
   * Personal validation in the face of her past isolation.
   * Surpassing her brother's (Manabu's) example.
   * Proof that her growth was not accidental, but deserved.
   * Justification for the emotional sacrifice she imposes upon herself.
The objective manages to affect her emotionally on many occasions, for example in the "Class Poll", where Suzune explicitly recognizes that expelling someone goes against her recent emotional development where she has learned to value the group, but the objective requires her to act against that empathy, which generates conscious and undenied emotional dissonance. These are not isolated incidents. Over the course of her first year, Suzune carries persistent emotional burdens, including:
* Long-term guilt over the Yamauchi case.
* Kushida’s sustained and active hatred.
* Acceptance of the role of moral antagonist within her own class.
* The deliberate abandonment of easy or emotionally comfortable relationships.
* Direct personal risk of expulsion.
* The constant pressure of having 38 people dependent on her decisions.
None of this stops her. On the contrary, Suzune actively uses her emotions as an analytical engine: they heighten her attention to detail, strengthen her preventive planning as exams progress, and become conscious criteria in her decision-making. This is reflected in key choices, for example:
* She does not expel Kushida, even though it would be the “easier” option, because she understands the emotional impact on the class and the long-term strategic consequences.
* She repeatedly chooses to protect Kiyotaka, not out of cold logic alone, but because she recognizes his strategic value, the emotional trust built with him, and his human importance within the class system.
Even her central objective—reaching Class A—is emotionally internalized. Suzune is neither emotionally anesthetized nor obsessed. She demonstrates flexible emotional commitment rather than blind attachment, with the capacity to re-engage emotionally after conflict. This is evident when she reflects on past mistakes, doubts herself, seeks moral validation from others (Manabu, Ichinose, Ryuuen), or acknowledges when she is not the appropriate person to provide emotional support (the Hirata case). Not only that, but after Yamauchi's expulsion, the class was fragmented and tense. Suzune channeled the general coldness and discomfort to guide the classroom conversations, keeping everyone focused solely on the cognitive goals of the special exam without anyone's help.

Suzune doesn't seek emotional comfort, she doesn't seek social acceptance as an end in itself, she doesn't seek power for ego. Suzune doesn't repress emotions to avoid feeling, but rather activates, channels, and endures them to sustain complex cognitive tasks over the long term.

EQU/EU (Emotional Understanding) -
* Kiyotaka Analysis (Y1V1-Y1V2): Kiyotaka wishes to live a peaceful and ordinary life. To achieve this, he uses method acting and assumes the role of a “normal student”, This makes him passive, inexpressive, and supposedly mediocre. Suzune understands that:
   * His passivity is intentional.
   * He avoids the spotlight for his own benefit.
   * He doesn't act for recognition, but for something more.
   * He will only intervene when necessary.
   * Kiyotaka sees others as tools.

* Kushida Analysis (Y1V6): On a superficial level, Kushida appears kind, cheerful, cooperative, and approachable. In reality, this is a carefully maintained façade—one she can wear with complete naturalness after years of practice—so convincingly that almost no one in the series truly knows her. Suzune, however, understands her in depth.
   * Kushida doesn't act out of rational self-interest.
   * Her behavior is driven by one-sided hatred, paranoia, and fear of the past.
   * She doesn't seek to "win"; she seeks to eliminate threats to her self-image.
   * As long as Suzune exists, Kushida will never stop attacking.
From this, Suzune concludes that Kushida is not a situational adversary but a structural enemy. Consequently, during the Paper Shuffle, she anticipates that Kushida will betray the class again and seek external support—specifically Ryūen—once she feels cornered. To deceive her and lead her into accepting the bet that would ultimately become a trap, Suzune needed to grant Kushida an emotional advantage over herself (through Manabu) while simultaneously allowing Kushida to preserve her pride—most importantly, the secrecy surrounding her true nature.

* Hirata Analysis (Y1V11): After Yamauchi’s expulsion, Hirata falls into a silent emotional collapse. He neither cries nor shouts, nor does he openly confront the class, yet his breakdown is unmistakable. Suzune clearly understands that:
   * Hirata isn't suffering solely because of Yamauchi.
   * His conflict is existential and moral: he can't accept that "someone had to bear the burden of evil."
   * Hirata's identity as an empathetic mediator has shattered.
   * Any direct attempt to comfort him would only reinforce his withdrawal.
From this assessment, Suzune concludes that telling him “it’s not your fault” would be ineffective, that framing Yamauchi as a straightforward villain would push Hirata further into denial, and that she herself is not the right person to support him emotionally. As a result, she anticipates that:
* Hirata will not recover through social pressure or collective encouragement.
* Only someone external to the moral structure of the conflict—Kiyotaka—can truly reach him.
* The most rational course of action is to step back, acknowledge her own limits, and delegate the intervention.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Class C is emotionally fragmented due to several overlapping factors:
   * The formation of defensive factions.
   * A pervasive fear of being singled out.
   * Collective moral paralysis: no one is willing to name a candidate.
Suzune accurately understands that:
   * Silence is not neutrality; it is fear expressed through inaction.
   * No one wants to strike first, because doing so would immediately turn them into the next target.
   * What the class requires is not an open debate—which would only prolong avoidance—but a guided catharsis capable of breaking the emotional deadlock and forcing a resolution.

Personal ranking for the best EU feats:
Kushida Analysis (Y1V6) > Hirata Analysis (Y1V10) > Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > Kiyotaka Analysis (Y1V1-Y1V2)

EQP/EP (Emotional Perception) -
* 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She picked up on her classmates' reactions to the festival rules (such as the overestimation of skills in Ike and Yamauchi) and detected the real intentions behind Shinohara's protests and Kei's resistance during the debates on the participation system.

* Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10)/ESE Planning (Y1V11): Before, during, and after the Class Poll, Suzune demonstrates an exceptionally precise understanding of the psychological and behavioral state of the 38 students in Class C.

For example, at the start of the “Class Poll”, After Sae finishes her explanation, the classroom immediately descends into chaos. Kōenji speaks about his “hidden potential” and suggests expelling a useless student, which instantly triggers a confrontation with Sudō. Hirata attempts to mediate, but Ike and Yamauchi oppose him, further escalating the conflict. Sudō is on the verge of a verbal outburst, and before he even raises his voice, Suzune already detects:
   * The ongoing emotional escalation.
   * His aggressive posture.
   * Irregular, accelerated breathing.
   * A defensive tone preceding an outburst.
Before the situation fully explodes, Suzune intervenes with a direct order and successfully restrains him. In the days leading up to the exam, when entering the classroom, Suzune notices subtle yet consistent signals:
   * Nothing has been stated explicitly.
   * No visible arguments are occurring.
   * The atmosphere is quiet, but not calm.
   * Conversations stop when she enters.
   * A lack of direct eye contact.
   * Noticeable shifts in the overall tone of the room.
From this, she immediately concludes:
   * There is latent tension.
   * Collective discomfort is present.
   * A hostile group is forming beneath the surface.
During the interrogation, Suzune maintains complete control of the situation. The most revealing cases are Kushida and Yamauchi. Kushida maintains her mask: a constant smile, polite language, and passive denial. However, even without breaking her façade, Suzune perceives the internal fracture through:
   * Stiff body posture.
   * Minimal but telling changes in tone.
   * Avoidance of eye contact.
   * Suppressed tension beneath the friendly exterior.
In Yamauchi’s case, even as he attempts to defend himself verbally, Suzune observes:
   * Persistent hesitation.
   * Emotional contradictions.
   * A steady increase in anxiety.
   * Shifts in his defensive tone.
She clearly identifies his emotional breaking point when Kushida abandons him, and recognizes the final blow when the class completely loses trust in his words. Following Yamauchi’s expulsion, Suzune understands that the consequences continue to unfold, as seen in the questions surrounding why Kiyotaka obtained 42 praise points. Her most precise insight, however, concerns Hirata:
   * Hirata continues attending class.
   * He does not argue or rebel.
   * He maintains an apparently normal demeanor.
To most, this appears to be “contained sadness.” Suzune, however, immediately detects critical non-verbal cues indicating a silent emotional collapse before it becomes obvious:
   * He avoids eye contact.
   * He leaves the classroom without looking back.
   * His body language is rigid rather than relaxed.
   * His silence is not neutral; it is emotional withdrawal.
From this, she clearly concludes that:
   * Hirata is not processing the event.
   * He is emotionally blocked.
   * His condition is unstable and deep.
   * That he won't be useful in the last exam of the year in that state

EQF/EF (Emotional Facilitation) -
* Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Sudō is highly reactive, easily provoked, and emotionally explosive. Suzune understands this from a structural perspective and chooses to manage him over the long term, not through direct confrontation, but by leveraging the emotional bond Sudō develops toward her. He seeks her approval, and Suzune consciously uses that desire as a mechanism to shape his behavior and restrain him at critical moments. To achieve this, she applies a progressive and sustained approach:
   * She trains him consistently over several months, focusing not only on performance but on discipline.
   * She gradually exposes him to pressure, increasing his emotional tolerance.
   * She reinforces self-control as a priority, recognizing that emotional stability matters more than raw ability.
The first important moment occurred:
   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): When she goes after Sudou after his outburst of anger, Suzune abandons her distant facade for the first time in nine years. She becomes vulnerable and transparent about her own history and desires, channeling that emotional connection to motivate Sudou to return to the competition and become her strategic ally.

* Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6): At the start of the pairing exam, before enforcing any system meant to save everyone, Suzune is fully aware of her standing within the class:
   * She is not well-liked.
   * She is perceived as cold and authoritarian.
   * The class will not listen to her if she speaks from a position of superiority.
Therefore, before explaining any plan, she adopts an approach that goes against her natural disposition: she offers a public apology, acknowledges her past mistakes, and bows to the class. This action is not meant to gain sympathy, but to lower the group’s emotional defenses, create a minimal emotional opening, and temporarily shift her image from “bossy” to “responsible.” Suzune does not justify herself, does not play the victim, and deliberately endures the emotional discomfort of the act. She then chooses not to be the primary voice behind the plan. Recognizing that Hirata possesses emotional charisma—something she lacks—she avoids competing with him as she once did during the sports festival and instead employs indirect emotional facilitation:
   * She uses him as an intermediary.
   * She asks him to introduce and articulate her ideas.
   * She merges her structural logic with his emotional warmth.
As a result, she influences the class more effectively by:
   * Reducing initial resistance to her proposals.
   * Increasing overall group acceptance.
   * Preventing unnecessary conflicts that would have arisen from direct enforcement.

* Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Kushida actively seeks Suzune’s expulsion. Her hostility is not circumstantial but emotional, driven by hatred, and she is willing to betray the class to achieve her goal. Faced with this profile, Suzune understands that attempting to punish her, reason with her, or appeal to her empathy would be futile.

Instead, Suzune chooses to instrumentalize Kushida’s abilities. To do so, she constructs an emotionally binding wager, with her brother acting as guarantor, fundamentally redefining the emotional consequences of the conflict. The purpose of this wager is not reconciliation, but to reframe the confrontation within a controllable structure, directly targeting Kushida’s psychological pressure points:
   * Sustained pressure.
   * Compromised pride.
   * Fear of being exposed.
   * Fear of losing control over her image and the situation.
In this way, Suzune does not eliminate Kushida’s hatred; she redirects it into a structured framework, transforming it from a chaotic threat into a predictable and manageable factor.

* Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): The class is emotionally fragmented, simultaneously strained by multiple internal pressures:
   * Collective guilt.
   * Fear of expulsion.
   * The formation of opposing factions.
   * Hirata being emotionally blocked and unable to act.
   * Sudō’s impulsive and volatile behavior.
   * Kushida actively manipulating events from the shadows.
In response, Suzune consciously and deliberately assumes the role of the “emotional villain” within the class. She does not seek approval or understanding; she prioritizes systemic stability over her personal image:
   * She accepts being hated if necessary.
   * She adopts a cold, firm, and unambiguous tone.
   * She positions herself as a judge of the process, not as a victim or an empathetic mediator.
Suzune leverages the emotional impact of her presence to her advantage. She clearly understands that:
   * Her firmness generates psychological pressure.
   * Her cold logic provokes initial resistance.
   * That resistance can be strategically redirected.
Rather than avoiding it, she uses this rejection to concentrate the class’s diffuse anger onto a single target—Yamauchi—preventing the conflict from spreading and becoming uncontrollable. At the same time, she actively regulates the most unstable individuals in the group. The clearest examples are:
   * Sudō: direct confrontation to restrain his impulsivity.
   * Hirata: neutralization through logical exhaustion, avoiding a direct emotional clash.
   * Kushida: indirect humiliation, not to destroy her, but to reinforce her limits within the system.
Through this approach, Suzune transforms an emotionally chaotic environment into a controllable structure, even at the cost of bearing the emotional burden of antagonism herself.

Personal ranking for the best EF feats:
Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10) > Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6) > Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8) > Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > …

EQR/ER (Emotional Realization) -
* She is herself (Y1V1-Y1V11.5): Suzune begins the story with a rigid mindset of self-sufficiency. She firmly believes that individual effort alone is enough, holds an implicit disdain for less capable classmates, and assumes that bearing all responsibility by herself is not only possible but correct.

Over time, this perspective gradually begins to fracture. Suzune comes to realize that she is not an all-powerful genius capable of carrying everything alone, that her emotional coldness creates unnecessary friction within the class, and that her emotional isolation is a genuine obstacle to reaching Class A.

This change is neither sudden nor comfortable. It is not an impulsive shift in personality, but a slow, deliberate, and painful process of self-awareness. Suzune learns through failure, group resistance, and the accumulated weight of difficult decisions, ultimately internalizing that effective leadership is not about imposing oneself, but about integrating, coordinating, and sustaining others without losing coherence or authority. The importance moments:

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Suzune experiences a key personal development milestone when she realizes her tunnel vision and her lack of talent for achieving Class A on her own. Furthermore, she recognizes Sudou's emotional shortcomings, understanding that his frustrations reflect his own ego-driven flaws.

FSSQ Categories:
Social Skills -

Suzune is a strong judge and clear filters: she knows when to say what is necessary and when to remain silent. Furthermore, when she chooses to, she can hold smooth conversations with others, handle delicate situations with tact, and accurately read non-verbal cues and the overall atmosphere of a group.

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune demonstrates clear, progressive, and strategically adaptive communication across multiple interlocutors. She adjusts her discourse to the audience—whether addressing the class as a whole, key leaders, Kushida, Ryūen, or Sae—carefully selecting both tone and timing. She knows when to employ a public apology and controlled body language (such as a bow) to reduce resistance, and she can explain complex systems—pairings, scoring rules, and evaluation mechanisms—in a way that is accessible even to the least academically capable students.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune confronts Yamauchi, restrains Hirata, responds to Sudō, and even engages in dialogue with Kōenji without losing control of the discussion, all while manipulating Kushida through her words. She maintains coherence, logical structure, and command over the flow of conversation in an environment where a single verbal mistake could make her the target of expulsion by the entire class.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): She controls high-tension discussions—such as the dispute over Kiyotaka’s praise points—redirects potentially dangerous conversations through strategic deception, explains complex rules in a clear and accessible manner, and demonstrates precise command of conversational timing, knowing exactly when to speak and when to cut a discussion short before it escalates.

Personal ranking for the best Social Skills feats:
ESE Planning (Y1V11) > Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > …

Social Awareness -
* Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She demonstrated a keen understanding of the dynamic and political environment between the classes, knowing when to intervene publicly in the meeting to thwart Ryuuen's proposal.

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune consistently operates with a precise contextual reading of the social environment: identifying potential sources of resistance (the class, Kōenji, Kushida), understanding territorial dynamics in spaces such as the library, recognizing the psychological risks of forcing pairings, and anticipating the likelihood of external sabotage and information leaks.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): At the start of the exam, Suzune anticipates the formation of factions, the possibility that Kushida will exploit the situation, the exact moment to intervene, and the emotional impact of the preceding silence. By doing so, she fully understands Class C’s collective psychological state and precisely adjusts her timing to maximize the impact of her intervention.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune consistently operates one step ahead of her environment. She immediately perceiving the system’s “deception/bluff” nature by identifying collective discomfort (mental fatigue, reluctance to attend meetings), recognizing critical emotional states in key individuals (Hirata, Sudō, Ike, Shinohara), and detecting systemic anomalies, such as the early signs of Tsukishiro’s sabotage.

Personal ranking for the best Social Awareness feats:
Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > ESE Planning (Y1V11) > Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > …

Empathy -
* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune identifies potential sources of resistance (the class, Kōenji, Kushida), understands territorial dynamics within shared spaces—such as implicit control over areas like the library—recognizes the psychological risks of enforcing forced pairings, and anticipates the likelihood of external sabotage and information leaks.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Although her approach is often cold and rational, Suzune consistently demonstrates empathy. From the outset, she considers the emotional impact of expelling someone, protects Sudō in the long term by using him as a point of personal growth, and is able to understand Hirata’s moral stance—even when she chooses to oppose it.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune empathy is restrained, yet clearly present when strategically necessary. Suzune protects Kiyotaka’s reputation and emotional stability, refrains from pressuring Hirata when she understands it would be counterproductive, accepts him without reproach after his recovery, and acknowledges her own emotional limitations by admitting that she is “not the right person” for certain roles.

Personal ranking for the best Empathy feats:
Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > ESE Planning (Y1V11) > Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > …

Social Intuition -
* Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She knew how to read the moment when Sudou needed space to reflect in Volume 2 (the trial with Class C) and when he required direct guidance to prevent him from committing infractions in later exams.

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Although Suzune does not prioritize emotional empathy, she consistently demonstrates strategic empathy. She protects the lowest-performing students, recognizes the genuine fear of expulsion as a decisive psychological factor, and, after confirming Kushida’s betrayal, deliberately chooses not to expel her, instead opting to control her and restrict her future influence. In addition, Suzune seeks out Ichinose to understand an alternative moral perspective, demonstrating ethical reflection beyond purely instrumental reasoning.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Even without direct initial evidence, Suzune concluded that Yamauchi had not acted alone, as he lacked the intellectual capacity to devise such a plan. From this, she deduced the presence of external intervention—specifically Sakayanagi’s involvement—based on the prior connection she had formed with him. In addition, she identified Kushida’s weakness throughout the exam: her reliance on a carefully maintained social façade.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune displays an exceptional ability to read unexpressed intentions. She anticipates suspicions surrounding Kiyotaka, accurately interprets Kōenji’s behavior and strategically corners him using his own ego, distinguishes between genuine events and decoys orchestrated by Class A. Not to mention, she detected the group's (led by Ike) fatigue and social discontent regarding workload and free time, restructuring the explanations of the rules to maintain class support.

Personal ranking for the best Social Intuition feats:
 Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6) > ESE Planning (Y1V11) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > …

Resource Management -
* Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): Suzune brilliantly managed their most scarce resource: time. She transformed 10-minute breaks between classes into optimized micro-study sessions. Furthermore, she skillfully managed and delegated human resources, utilizing Kiyotaka and Kushida to cover teaching duties.

* Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): She efficiently managed and coordinated heterogeneous resources: Kanzaki's private points, Sotomura's technical knowledge, Ichinose's social status and charisma, and Kushida's network of contacts.

* Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She recognized Ike's practical talent for survival and arranged for Kiyotaka to persuade him to lead the group, also organizing efficient search teams to save food and points.

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune simultaneously and coherently manages human capital—tutors, academically strong and weak students, as well as external support—adapts schedules to students’ actual capabilities, strategically controls physical spaces (the library, the use of Ichinose’s group), regulates information flow by restricting access to critical questions, and knows when and how to leverage institutional authority (Sae, Manabu) as tactical support. In short, she efficiently managed the academic capital of Class D. She designed a scoring system by dividing the 40 students into specific ranges (students who had to score 0, 1, 80, or 85 points) to ensure stable pairings and prevent the worst averages from being left unprotected.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Nothing is wasted or revealed prematurely: Suzune waits for the optimal moment to act, taking into account the individual behaviors of all 39 students in Class C. At the same time, she leverages tactical support from Kōenji and even uses enemies like Kushida to deliver the final blow against Yamauchi, while positioning herself as an alternative target. Allies, neutrals, enemies, and even herself are all integrated as components of a single strategy aimed at achieving one objective.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune optimizes student deployment without prematurely exposing key talents, carefully manages time, information, and group attention, avoids unnecessary expenditures—such as unproductive surveillance or inefficient espionage—and employs Kiyotaka only when his intervention is genuinely decisive. She maximized the efficiency of her class's resources by assigning each student to exactly the task where their individual abilities surpassed or equaled Class A.

Personal ranking for the best Resource Management feats:
ESE Planning (Y1V11) > Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6) > Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > ….

Social Cognition -
* 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She identifies the group dynamics of Class D: she anticipates that less able students will seek individual points at the expense of the group and understands how Hirata's leadership or loyalties to Kei affect decision-making in the classroom.

* Kushida (Y1V1-Now): She accurately interpreted the social repercussions of exposing Kushida to the rest of the classes, negotiating the silence of her own group and understanding how Kushida's "mask" would still function as an external defense.

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune designs complete social systems and reasons metacognitively about collective and individual behavior. She analyzes how her class thinks, how other classes act, how individuals react under pressure, and how incentives, loyalties, and betrayals emerge within a competitive environment. She understood the social dynamics of the class and the influence of factions (like Karuizawa's and the girls'). She knew exactly who to approach to sway votes without creating friction.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune thinks in terms of social dynamics rather than isolated individuals, analyzing the entire class as a system. She can anticipate the roles each student will assume in a discussion: the moral leader in Hirata, the traitor in Yamauchi, the wildcard represented by Kōenji, and the pawn she can utilize in Kushida.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune thinks in terms of social systems rather than isolated actions, accurately models how Sakayanagi and her own class will think, recognizes the psychological impact of each event, and integrates academic logic with social behavior to anticipate outcomes and refine her strategies. Furthermore, She understood the social landscape and the internal dynamics of the classroom. She knew how to respond to Sudou and Shinohara's questions, anticipating how the student body would react if she proposed Kiyotaka as commander.

Personal ranking for the best Social Cognition feats:
ESE Planning (Y1V11) > Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > ….

Leadership -
* D class upgrade (Y1V1-now): Horikita’s primary goal is to reach Class A—whether to earn her brother’s recognition, as at the beginning of the story, or to prove herself, as in her current stage.

The problem is that this goal is extraordinarily difficult.

In their very first month, the class started with 0 class points, while the others held overwhelming advantages (Class C: 490, Class B: 650, Class A: 940). Their homeroom teacher not only failed to support them but actively sabotaged them, showing complete indifference. The system itself was designed to brand them as inferior.

Class points are directly tied to private points—the money students use to live each month. With very few, or none at all as in the beginning, the class was forced to rely on low-quality free products, negatively affecting their health and overall performance. Moreover, in many special exams, having private points could determine victory or defeat.

Clear examples include the Sports Festival, where changing team positions required 100,000 private points, and the Class Poll, where saving someone demanded 20 million. A system that claimed equality only widened Class D’s disadvantage.

The rival classes only compounded the difficulty. Class C, the closest threat, was ruled by a dictator, Ryuuen, turning them into a unified and aggressive force. They excelled physically, and their leader was cunning and ruthless enough to sacrifice even his own allies for a marginal gain.

Class B was more friendly but no less formidable. Under Ichinose’s charismatic leadership, they possessed absolute teamwork. While not extreme in physical or intellectual ability, every member was above average, and their cohesion made them a wall difficult to overcome.

Finally, Class A. After resolving its internal power struggle, it fell under the control of Sakayanagi, a genius among geniuses, leading students considered elite even by elite standards.

With the system stacked against her and increasingly difficult rivals at every level, Horikita’s challenge seemed impossible… and yet, none of these obstacles was her true problem.

Suzune’s Class D was considered the worst Class D in the history of ANHS. It lacked clear leadership, had poorly defined roles, and suffered from slow, chaotic, or impulsive decision-making. There was an almost complete absence of planning and concrete goals, combined with poor communication, frequent unresolved arguments, and a low level of commitment to a shared objective.

Many of its members acted purely out of obligation, displaying a significant lack of discipline and consistency, and adopting individualistic attitudes that prioritized ego over collective results. In addition, there was a notable disparity in skills and knowledge, which led to excessive reliance on one or two students, alongside a clear lack of technical and strategic preparation and an inability to adapt to problems or unexpected changes.

This was further compounded by inefficient use of available resources, a tense, toxic, and demotivating environment, and the bad habit of blaming others for mistakes instead of taking responsibility. The lack of trust among members, combined with resistance to criticism and learning, resulted in unmet objectives or mediocre outcomes, the constant repetition of the same mistakes, a failure to learn from defeats, and ultimately the gradual disintegration of the class when faced with adversity.

They were the worst of the worst, something that inspired no confidence in Suzune. However, after realizing that she could not reach Class A on her own, she understood that she had to take action to change the situation. (Go to Strategy Section)

Only in Year 1, Suzune transformed Class D from a chaotic, self-sabotaging group into a stable, organized class capable of sustained progress under clear leadership.

Relationship Management -
* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune demonstrates the ability to preserve relationships even under high tension. She maintains cooperation with Hirata despite fundamental ideological differences, leverages Kushida without completely severing the relationship—avoiding premature expulsion—deliberately strengthens ties with Class B, and manages her relationship with Kiyotaka from a position of independence rather than reliance.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune manages to create a scapegoat while simultaneously strengthening the class’s future cohesion, reinforcing boundaries around Kushida, and securing Sudō’s loyalty.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune correctly prioritizes critical relationships. She maintains a functional but non-dependent relationship with Kiyotaka, manages Sudō and Kōenji without direct confrontation, avoids breaking ties with Hirata during his personal crisis, and successfully coordinates the class despite ongoing internal tensions.

Influence -
* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune’s influence is exercised through structure and foresight rather than charisma. She secures compliance without direct coercion, forces Kōenji to take a clear stance, keeps Kushida’s behavior under control without expelling her, and indirectly constrains Ryūen’s actions.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune controls the final decision without imposing it directly or provoking active opposition. Even while making an unpopular choice, she succeeds in having the class adopt her narrative framework. She takes care Yamauchi loses all credibility, Kushida is forced to retreat, and Hirata is left without real support.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune exerts influence through logic, precise timing, and contextual pressure. She compels Kiyotaka to accept the role of commander by shaping the collective perception of the Yamauchi case, steers group decisions without devolving into chaotic voting, and minimizes resistance without direct imposition.

Self Awareness -
* 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She finally accepts that her main motivation (chasing her brother Manabu's shadow) had blinded her to the common good of Class D. Recognizing that she is not an "unbeatable genius," she radically re-evaluates her approach.

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune does not act from blind ego, but from continuous evaluation. She publicly acknowledges her past mistakes, learns from her failure at the sports festival, knows when to rely on others—such as Hirata or Kiyotaka—and accepts personal risk when directly confronting Kushida.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): She acknowledges her emotional limitations, is aware of how much she has changed compared to her past self, and understands that, at certain moments, she requires external validation—such as from Ryūen or Manabu—in order to act decisively.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune demonstrates a clear awareness of her own strengths and limitations. She recognizes her emotional boundaries, particularly regarding Hirata, understands when to withdraw from direct control, and accepts that Kiyotaka is tactically superior in execution, adjusting her role accordingly.

Personal ranking for the best Self Awareness feats:

Social Integration -
Throughout the entire first year, Suzune stops acting as an isolated observer and becomes a central figure accepted by the class. Her discourse is no longer seen as external or forced, but rather understood and followed by the majority, indicating that she has fully integrated into the class’s decision-making core. She does not seek new friendships, but operates fully within the social system. For the last special exam of the year, Suzune shows that she is already fully integrated as a strategic axis of the class, is recognized as a reference figure and does not need external validation to operate.

* Kushida (Y1V1-Now): She openly acknowledged her own flaws, her selfishness in trying to save Kushida out of personal convention, and her lack of social skills compared to Kushida's natural talent for winning over people.

Influence Building -
* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune turns strategic success into permanent legitimacy; she increases her authority after each phase of the plan, reduces the influence of Kushida and Ryūen, and consolidates her image as an indispensable leader.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune does not merely wield influence; she accumulates it systematically. After this event, her words carry greater weight, her decisions face less resistance, and her role as leader becomes firmly established. Throughout the volume, she deliberately transforms latent influence into real authority over the class, evolving from a secondary strategist into a recognized leader. Simultaneously, she undermines Kushida’s influence without expelling her, narrowing her future room for maneuver, while securing the tacit support of key figures such as Kōenji, Sudō, and part of Hirata’s group.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune constructs her influence gradually and without overt declaration. Each success reinforces her future legitimacy, neutralizes doubts about her leadership, and positions her as indispensable without ever needing to assert it explicitly.

Personal ranking for the best Influence Building feats:

Social Engineering -
* Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): She regulated the behavior and dynamics among the first-year classes. By involving Class B in the operation, she strengthened the alliance between Class D and Class B while neutralizing the hostile move by Class C (Ryuuen).

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune converts each strategic success into lasting legitimacy. With every phase of the plan, she strengthens her actual authority, diminishes the influence of destabilizing actors such as Kushida and Ryūen, and solidifies her image as an indispensable leader.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune redirects the course of the class by eliminating the corrupting agent, reordering internal hierarchies by positioning herself as the undisputed leader, setting a precedent with consequences, and reducing the influence of Class A over her class.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Redefine group behavior through implicit rules by designing events to force mass participation, trap Kōenji in a reputational snare, and turn an exam into a controllable system.

Personal ranking for the best Social Engineering feats:

Self Management -
* Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): Although Suzune is naturally antisocial and individualistic, she managed her social behavior to relate to people she intellectually detested/despised in service of her higher goal.

* False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): Despite her solitary nature, she adapted to working in a multidisciplinary team with members from different classes, managing her role as Sudo's mentor/leader.

* Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She agreed to assume the role of leader of Class D to prevent it from falling into the hands of incompetent classmates (the "three idiots"), modulating her behavior to maintain the minimum cohesion required by the exam.

* Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She suppressed her personal pride by attributing plans or lies to herself in public to act as the "perfect smokescreen," demonstrating total control over her social behavior in front of the entire school.

* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune never loses control of the public stage. Throughout the entire exam, she maintains consistent composure, adapts her behavior to the context—displaying humility, firmness, or calculated helplessness when required—and precisely controls the pace, tone, and timing of every action.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune redirects the course of the class by eliminating the corrupting agent, restructuring the internal hierarchy, and establishing herself as the undisputed leader. In doing so, she sets a clear precedent with tangible consequences and significantly reduces Class A’s influence over her class, all while offering himself as a possibility for expulsion, she expelled a colleague and risked destroying the entire reputation he had worked to build over months.

Self Regulation -
* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune demonstrates complete emotional regulation even under the direct threat of expulsion. She contains her emotions in confrontations with Kushida, displays desperation only when it serves a strategic purpose, and never reacts impulsively to provocation.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune never loses control, even when attacking or being attacked. She consciously regulates her emotions (guilt, doubt, and pressure) and maintains full command over both her verbal conduct in front of the class and her body language.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Throughout the entire operation, Suzune exhibits an exceptionally high level of emotional regulation. She does not respond to provocation, manages stress during the decisive chess match, and maintains cognitive clarity even when she temporarily loses the strategic advantage.

Personal ranking for the best Self Regulation feats:

Self Regard -
* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune maintains a precise balance between individual and collective responsibility. She knowingly stakes her continued enrollment with full awareness of the consequences, avoids unnecessary self-sacrifice, yet accepts calculated risks when circumstances demand it, all while preserving her dignity and moral coherence.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune values herself enough to put herself forward as a potential expulsion candidate, calming the class and granting them moral protection through her “sacrifice.” By assuming the role of judge and publicly defending her judgment before everyone, she becomes virtually untouchable during the exam.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): She possesses a solid, realistic, and strategic sense of self-worth. Suzune accepts calculated risks without underestimating or overestimating anyone, including herself, and safeguards her own position without sacrificing the group, thereby reinforcing her legitimacy as a leader.

Carisma -
Suzune presence commands respect rather than closeness. Her influence is not born of charisma, but of logical consistency and the authority she builds through results. Even if she doesn't stand out for her natural charm, her honesty and determination command respect, and her ability to take the blame publicly enhances her image.

Aura -
Simply by standing at the podium, Suzune brings about immediate silence. Her presence creates tension in every student and a clear sense of inevitability, as if the outcome has already been decided. Her presence generates psychological pressure on allies and rivals. Not only that, it is also capable of inspiring those who observe it, one case being Yukimura in Paper Shuffle. Yukimura understood that he couldn't continue acting selfishly towards the class, and if he wanted to reach Class A, he had to support them. It was something he understood when he saw Suzune's change at the sports festival, motivating him to help.

Cunning -
* Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Suzune simultaneously deceives Ryūen and Kushida through the use of half-truths, calculated theatrics, and deliberate staging, steering both opponents into predetermined courses of action without them realizing they are being manipulated.

* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Suzune carries out a social deception without resorting to explicit lies, using a gradual exposure of information. Through verbal traps and indirect control of the discourse, she manipulates testimonies and turns Kushida’s social prestige into a weapon against her.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune manipulates key narratives (praise, the commander role), traps Kōenji by exploiting his pride, reads and counters bluffs from Class A, and anticipates institutional traps before they are set into motion.

Personal ranking for the best Cunning feats:
Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6) > Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10) > ESE Planning (Y1V11) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > …

Summary
________________
FSAQ Categories:
Composure -
Suzune possesses a remarkable ability to maintain her composure under any circumstances, whether facing betrayal, unexpected developments, or situations of extreme stress. Her emotional control allows her to think clearly and act rationally even when pressure is at its highest.

An example was the last special exam of the year, the final event. She was competing in a game whose rules she had only learned a week ago, facing an opponent with months of experience. She needed to gain an advantage or at least keep the game balanced so that Kiyotaka had a chance. A single misstep would mean throwing away all the effort her class had put into reaching Class C. On top of that, her opponent was trying to unsettle and distract her, yet she remained steady and focused on her goal until the very end.

* Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): She remained calm and collected in the face of Yamawaki's insults and provocations in the library to prevent the incident from escalating into a physical fight that would result in Sudo's expulsion.

* Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She kept her cool and collected when confronted by Ryuuen (who tried to provoke and flirt with her), when dealing with Karuizawa's hysteria after the underwear theft, and when cornered by the internal collapse of Class D.

* Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She remained calm and collected under situations of high psychological pressure caused by Ryuuen's hostile behavior, reacting coolly and without being intimidated.

* ESE Planning (Y1V11): She remained calm and focused when Hashimoto tried to unsettle her by talking and encroaching on her territory on the board, responding calmly and maintaining the tactical advantage.

Determination -
Once Suzune sets a goal, she pushes forward until the very end regardless of the circumstances. A clear example is the sports festival, where she continued competing despite having a broken leg. However, her greatest display of determination occurs during the uninhabited island exam.

For seven days on the island, Suzune remained as the card leader, enduring a worsening fever that exceeded 38°C, clouding both her vision and thoughts. Despite her deteriorating condition, she traversed the island gathering information, helped her class by collecting berries and branches, chased Ibuki through the forest, and fought her until she was defeated. Yet, even after all that, she refused to give up. She chose to stay on the island to prevent her class from losing points due to her departure.

* Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): Her unwavering goal of achieving Class A led her to not give up on struggling students, overcoming the sudden change in the exam and exhausting every last resource (even defying the school system) to save Sudo.

* Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Despite suffering from a debilitating fever of up to 38°C for five consecutive days on a deserted island in extreme conditions, Suzune steadfastly refused to retreat or surrender. She remained focused on her goal of leading Class D to victory and protecting the leader card at all costs.

* 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She reaffirms her unwavering commitment to rise to Class A despite admitting that she lacks the natural talent of geniuses, willing to adapt all her methods in order to win.

Impulse Control/Management -
Suzune has great body control, for example when Ryuuen subjected her to constant psychological pressure in his search for the mastermind and the "VIP" of the Dragon Group. Even when he made inappropriate remarks about her, Suzune did not show the slightest opening, either verbally or through her body language.

Cognitive Discipline -
Suzune has had a well-structured daily routine since elementary school: she woke up at 6 a.m., groomed herself, had a balanced breakfast, and, precisely at 8, left for class. After class she would return home to do her homework and study and then go to sleep again and start a new day. Everything is cyclical due to its lack of interaction with the world. In the class, Suzune is not shaken by the introduction of a new special exam; instead, she analyzes the rules to find exploitable gaps and formulates strategies aimed at avoiding any losses within the class. However, when circumstances demand it and she must sacrifice someone, she is able to move forward while prioritizing the good of the majority. For example, Suzune set aside her personal feelings and, based on a logical judgment that best benefited the class, chose one of her classmates to be expelled. Even knowing she was ruining his future, she exposed him without hesitation.

* Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): She filtered out distractions from her surroundings and the frustration caused by her classmates to focus strictly on intensive academic preparation and retention of key content.

* Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Even though the fever actively clouded her thoughts and reduced her cognitive ability to less than half of her usual performance, she forced her brain to stay focused on executing her concealment plan, investigating enemies, and interrogating Ibuki.

* Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She kept her emotions, focus, and mental performance under control despite the stresses of the exam, mentally organizing the data and noting down the student lists by class upon returning to her room.
________________
* Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): She maintained impeccable composure and serenity throughout the entire confrontation. Even when Hirata slammed his fist on the table in frustration, trying to intimidate or instill fear, Suzune remained calm, smoothed her bangs, and accepted the dissenting votes without flinching.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): She maintained rigorous concentration and absolute mental control under the enormous pressure of defining 130 class points in her chess game against Hashimoto.
Perseverance -
Suzune is extremely stubborn. Once she sets her sights on something, she pursues it to the end regardless of how long it takes or who stands in her way. This is evident throughout the series: despite repeated defeats and setbacks, she never stops moving forward. Her determination to reach Class A drives her to press on without hesitation. In terms of long-term perseverance, her greatest example is spent 9 years chasing her brother’s shadow, enduring his coldness as well as his physical, emotional, and psychological mistreatment.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Despite suffering an injury deliberately caused by Kinoshita, being cornered in the cavalry battle by four Class C competitors, and finishing last in the three-legged race, she continued to compete to the limit of her physical strength.

Stress Tolerance -
Suzune is capable of effectively managing, regulating, and enduring high levels of stress under extreme pressure. The clearest example of this ability is shown during the island exam.

The Class D did not have enough points to properly compete in the class competition. Winning the first special exam or at the very least preserving as many points as possible, was essential not only to maintain a realistic chance of reaching Class A (Her main objective), but also to sustain their daily life and avoid relying solely on low-quality free goods. Despite suffering from a fever above 38°C that clouded her thoughts, she faced a critical situation: the boys and girls were divided, her card had been stolen, the manual had been burned, and the other classes were ready to take points away after discovering her identity as the leader. She had been beaten until she fell, and without a doubt, her class would blame her for everything that had gone wrong. Yet, even under these circumstances, she managed to devise a solution to safeguard the few remaining points.

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She handled multiple simultaneous pressure points: keeping her illness hidden from 39 classmates and 3 enemy classes, protecting the leader card, investigating other classes, and maintaining control over troublesome members of her group.
Mental Fortitude -
Suzune's mental strength is what a leader should have. Not only was Suzune manipulated by her first friend in the Sudou case, but she was also beaten and broken physically, mentally, and psychologically on the island and during the sports festival because of Kiyotaka's own schemes. She suffered betrayal in both the Zodiac exam and the Sport Festival itself while dealing with the emotions and traumas of her classmate. Additionally, she had to be the direct cause of a classmate’s expulsion and bear the responsibility for 38 students during the final exam, since her fellow leader was depressed. Despite falling and being defeated over and over again, and all while being rejected and ignored by the most important person in her life, Suzune always got back up and never gave up on her goal of reaching Class A.

   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): After learning that they had wasted an entire week studying the wrong material and being under overwhelming inner rage, Suzune maintained her outward composure and did not allow despair to affect the group's performance.

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She endured not only the physical pain of the disease, but also the psychological toll of knowing that her class was fractured, that Hirata was not acting efficiently, and that she herself had to bear the role of leader without showing weakness in front of rivals.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She maintained her composure under the constant pressure from Class C and Ryuuen's explicit threats without breaking down emotionally or revealing secrets.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5):  She suffers a psychological breakdown when Ayanokouji verbally shatters her pride and diminishes her vision. Far from giving up or succumbing to despair, she absorbs the blow, restructures her mindset, and acts immediately to win Sudou back.

Miscellaneous Categories:
Perception/Observation -
   * Eyes perception:
   * Suzune has the ability to instinctively sense when someone is looking at her.
   * Suzune analyzes everything she sees and wonders: why?
   * Suzune is able to decipher people's thoughts to a certain extent through their words, movements, and reactions, like an esper. This can be seen in how he is able to easily identify Kiyotaka's (false) thoughts, even when he makes no reaction, and even later with his real emotions.
   * She closely observed Ike and Yamauchi's nonverbal reactions to Suzune/Kushida to address their immediate motivations, and detected inconsistencies in Ayanokoji's attitude and excuses.
   * Suzune’s perception extends to the entire class (In Japan, the standard size of a classroom historically designed to accommodate up to 40 people is 63 to 65 square meters, typically with dimensions of 7 x 9 meters.). She is capable of observing and processing individual micro-reactions even within a group setting. A clear example occurs when Sae announces Sudou’s case: Suzune identifies Sakura’s guilt solely through her immediate reaction, before any explicit evidence is presented.
   * Suzune's perception doesn't stop even if she's doing something else or her mind is elsewhere, for example, at the sports festival where As she read through the manual, she observed her classmates' reactions and started devising a strategy to minimize point losses.
   * In the Sudou Trial, she analyzed the physical map of the campus and the conditions of the special building, noting the sunlight hours, the orientation of the building at 3:40 PM, and the lack of surveillance in that sector.
   * Suzune's abilities are vast when she's not focusing on people, for example, during the Physical Education class, students from other classes watched Class D’s practice from their windows, something Suzune quickly noticed. However, she also observed that Class C’s members weren’t analyzing their opponents at all.
   * She observed Kinoshita's body language during the Sports Festival incident to determine her intentions.
   * Suzune is capable of analyzing environments with a high concentration of people and understanding their internal dynamics. A clear example appears in Volume 5, where she naturally identifies the factional divisions among second- and third-year students inside the closed gymnasium. Another instance occurs in Volume 6, when, during the exam period, she assesses the nearly full library and concludes that only about 10% of the space remained available for use.
   * Hear perception:
   * Suzune analyzes and thinks about every word she hears, finding loopholes or strange things if there are any.
   * Suzune has the ability to hear the whispers in the classroom (63 to 65 square meters, typically with dimensions of 7 x 9 meters), especially without them speaking about her.
   * Suzune possesses a remarkable ability for voice recognition. A clear example occurs in Volume 3: despite suffering from a 38 °C fever, being semi-conscious from exhaustion after running through a dark jungle, and having lost a physical confrontation with Ibuki, she was still able to accurately recognize Ryuuen’s voice, including if he whispered and that by that point, she had only heard him voice twice: once in the rain and once on the same island.
   * Touch Perception/Smell perception:
   * Suzune couldn't help but marvel at the VR game's realism. There was a smell of trees coming from somewhere, and although her body looked the same, the only difference was the clothes she was wearing. It felt like she was in the real world.
   * Taste Perception:
   * Suzune is capable if an ingredient is of quality when eating a meal

Personal ranking for the best Perception/Observation feats:
Eyes perception > Hear perception > Touch Perception/Smell perception > Taste Perception

Knowledge/Academics -
Suzune is an exceptionally capable student, excelling both academically and physically. She is skilled in various academic subjects, individual sports, piano, and cooking. Additionally, as an avid reader, she possesses extensive knowledge in areas such as social relationships, the body language, the zodiac animals, and philosophy.

Methods -
   * Club's ghost member Tactic (Y1V1): Recruiting numerous members and keeping them as ghost members is an effective tactic to increase the budget and maximize profits in a club.

   * Food Trap 1/2 (Y1V1): Deliberately cuts options and creates an implicit debt. By doing so, she establishes a psychological obligation that she later exploits to compel him to cooperate and work for her when needed.

   * Fake study group (Y1V1): Chooses a selective investment based on an academic cost–benefit analysis, prioritizing the class’s overall outcome over indiscriminate support.

   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): Exploited the gaps within the system’s rules and optimized the process to reach her objective safely. By steering both allies and enemies toward outcomes that benefited her, she made their own decisions work in her favor.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Turns one of the class’s greatest weaknesses into one of its strongest assets, shaping it to benefit the group while ensuring its loyalty to her leadership.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V2-now): Restructured the worst Class D in ANHS history into an organized team with a clear and functional chain of command, explicitly aimed at advancing toward Class A, protecting them and making it stronger through education.

   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Uses Legal-rational pressure via information asymmetry to dismantle Class C’s narrative by systematically exploiting their weaknesses and forces a strategic break that halts their momentum and neutralizes their advantage.

   * Class B Alliance (Y1V2-Y1V11.5): Established a cooperation and non-aggression alliance with the second-strongest class.

   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): She designed a highly rigorous 4-step sequential procedure (Purchase -> Installation -> Appointment by Kushida -> Confrontation by Ichinose/Kiyotaka) that guaranteed the desired objective.

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Hiding the card in plain sight, under layers of deception and protection.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): Conceals her greatest weapon while exploiting the influence earned by her own achievements.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): Conceals the VIP to force a one-sided victory, structuring the situation so that only her class gains points while all others are effectively neutralized.

   * Sudou Carry Tactic (Y1V4.5): Use your greatest weapon to the fullest.

   * 'Skill-Based' System Strategy (Y1V5): Prioritizing the most athletic students, they needed to form the best possible combinations to ensure victory and fully utilize their potential.

   * Race with Manabu (Y1V5): Uses the sincerity of her emotions as a means of persuasion to convince both Sudou and Hirata to let her race against Manabu, appealing not to cold logic, but to the honesty of her feelings.

   * Sudou MVP (Y1V5): Places a motivated Sudou at the forefront not only to improve the class’s athletic performance, but to secure MVP points.

   * Confession recording strategy (Y1V5): Manipulated enemy into confessing and recorded that confession to use it later as leverage for blackmail and control.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Neutralizes her enemy and forces her to become her ally while eliminating an external threat and ensuring the protection of her class.

   * Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Class-level academic redistribution to control pairing outcomes.

   * MTC group girls Strategy (Y1V8): Uses Kushida as an intermediary to relay their concerns and handle group issues without exposing herself directly.

   * MTC Suzune plan (Y1V8): Forms an alliance to ensure that the weakest members of her class are paired with the strongest students from the other class.

   * Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Uses a traitor as a scapegoat, redirecting all blame onto them in order to protect a truly skilled student and prevent a strategic loss for the class.

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11): Exploits the class’s confusion to pressure the otherwise inactive weapon into nominating himself.

   * Class D Basket Strategy (Y1V11): Control tempo and morale by staging power escalation, rather than front-loading maximum strength.

   * Typing/Archery Strategy (Y1V11): Select competitive formats that isolate individual specialization while structurally suppressing external interference.

   * Anti-Class A Tests Strategy (Y1V11): Prepare multiple internally coherent response paths in advance for a luck-dependent system, allowing the commander (Kiyotaka) to select the optimal course once uncertainty resolves.

   * FMA Strategy (Y1V11): Maximize winning probability in an environment where direct control over a key asset (Kōenji) is unreliable, by pairing a high-ceiling but uncontrollable actor with a stable, controllable fallback.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): Outcome optimization through event probability analysis and role specialization.
