Deduction -
   * Kushida class (Y0): Deduced that Kushida was responsible for destroying her class based on rumors he overheard in whispers, even though the school went to great lengths to conceal it.

   * Counter Operation friendship (Y1V1): Deduce Kushida's entire plan and her possible motivations.

   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Deduce the Class C plan and intentions

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Deduce Ibuki’s true mission was not to compete or endure the exam, but to sabotage Class D while enduring a fever of 38 °C. Upon returning to the ship and seeing that the class greeted her like a hero, she instantly deduced that Kiyotaka had transferred the island exam credit to her to maintain his own peaceful life. She also deduced that the theft of the card and the burning of the manual were connected.

   * Karuizawa (Y1V4): Kiyotaka asks a lot of questions about Karuizawa, and Suzune deduces that Kiyotaka is going to do something to make her his "ally."

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): Based on concrete premises (such as the appointment time and the list of students in each group), she correctly concluded the structure of the exam and inferred that no member of Class D sent the final email by checking the lack of notifications in her group. In short:
   * P1: Every student received an official notification with a specific meeting time and assigned classroom.
   * P2: Kiyotaka's assigned meeting time was strictly different from Suzune's assigned time.
   * C1: The school is not conducting a single unified session; the exam is split either by gender or into distinct smaller groups.
   * P3: Kiyotaka's meeting confirmed the presence of students from different classes and genders in a single room.
   * C2: The exam is structured around multi-class cross-functional groups.

   * Zodiac Exam Analysis (Y1V5): Deduce the mechanics of the 'VIP' search, which had been conducted in mixed teams divided into 12 groups.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She deduces from the rule gap specified by Sae how the school would handle absences/substitutions and the point cost involved and deduce the participation chart that had been leaked by a traitor, that Sudou had been deliberately provoked by Ryuuen, and that Ryuuen had even sacrificed a capable student just to injure her.

   * Kushida intentions (Y1V5): Deduced the true reason behind Kushida’s betrayals: expelling her. She assumed that Kushida wanted to keep her secret buried in the dark so she could live her ideal school life, deceiving everyone.

   * Paper Shuffle exam (Y1V6): Concluded that the upcoming exam would determine partnerships based on students’ scores, pairing the most capable ones with those who performed the worst.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune started from concrete premises: Kushida wants her expulsion at all costs; the only way to achieve this in this exam is for Suzune to get a lower grade in a selected subject; for this, Kushida needs the answers from Class C. She will inevitably deduce that Kushida will make a deal with Ryuuen to exchange the questions.

   * X Strategy (Y1V7): Concluded that Ryuuen had completely lost interest in her and was now focusing all his attention on the mastermind and deduced that Kiyotaka already had everything planned to protect himself.

   * Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): From the proven fact that Yamauchi did not possess the ability or intelligence to organize such a large voting group on his own, she logically deduced the existence of an external agent (Sakayanagi from Class A) guiding his actions. Furthermore, deduced  Kiyotaka had influenced her brother to guide Suzune, but she realized that she wouldn’t get anything else from him

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune immediately deduced from the rules set by Sae that 5 out of the 10 recorded events were bluffs designed to deceive the opponent. Furthermore, she deduced that the Class A event structure aimed to force the competition toward average academic ability, neutralizing the commander's intervention.

   * Sakayanagi and Kiyotaka relationship (Y1V9/Y1V10/Y1V11.5): Deduced that there was at least a one-sided history between Sakayanagi and Kiyotaka that went back to their childhood, but she couldn't prove it.

Improvisation -
   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Managed to corner Class C and forced their homeroom teacher to request a settlement, one in which the consequences for Sudou would be significantly reduced. However, Suzune was not willing to settle for a partial outcome. Instead of accepting the arrangement, she deliberately steered the situation to force a one-day interim period.

That extra day was not merely a delay, but a critical window she used to develop and refine the strategy she had already envisioned—a strategy aimed not only at minimizing losses, but at securing a decisive victory.

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11): Suzune needed Kiyotaka to take on the role of class commander. When Sudou stood up demanding an explanation, Suzune seized the opportunity and steered the situation to force Kiyotaka to nominate himself as commander, framing it as a necessary step to clear his name and restore the class’s trust.

Personal ranking for the best Improvisation feats:
Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11) > Sudou Trial (Y1V2)

Induction -
   * Lost Points (Y1V1): Calculating how many points were deducted for talking or arriving late to class. As she recorded the infractions.

   * Hirata Analysis (Y1V3): Suzune explained why she couldn’t trust Hirata, she saw him as two-faced, much like Kushida, though in a slightly different way.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V2-now): Suzune suspected that at some point there would be a special exam dedicated to academics.

   * MTC Suzune plan (Y1V8): Understands even though she is respected, the girls will not approach her even if they have a problem.

   * Kei and Hirata's breakup (Y1V9): Kiyotaka's involvement in Kei and Hirata's breakup

   * Sakayanagi and Kiyotaka relationship (Y1V9/Y1V10/Y1V11.5): Suzune deduces that Sakayanagi's failure was the product of deliberate overexposure. Suzune identifies the predictable nature of the outcome, which generates a methodical doubt in her as to whether this mistake by the genius of class A was a distraction maneuver within a larger plan orchestrated by someone else.

Abduction -
   * S-System (Y1V1): Over the course of a month, Suzune managed to deduce several key aspects of the S-System based on various clues she observed:
   * They would not receive another 100,000 points the following month.
   * The number of points would decrease due to her classmates’ behavior and performance. The other classes would earn more points than them.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V2-now): Identified key issues that needed to be addressed to upgrade:
   * Reducing tardiness
   * Minimizing classroom chatter
   * Ensuring everyone passed the exams
   * The Other classes
   * Physical improvement
   * Create a class unit
   * Dealing with external and internal threats.

   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): She inferred that Kiyotaka was the true architect behind the acquisition of the past exams handed out by Kushida, formulating the most probable hypothesis about her partner's true ability.

   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Identify Sae’s true ambition to reach Class A.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): The first thing Suzune did was gather information, so she messaged Kiyotaka and found out that his assigned time was different from hers. From this, she reached two conclusions:
   * It was possible that the exam would separate boys and girls.
   * It was also possible that it was a group-based exam.
Suzune confirmed her second suspicion when Kiyotaka provided her with the information he had obtained from his meeting. She literally formulated the most probable hypotheses with incomplete information at the beginning of the exam (deducing whether it was a gender-based or group-based exam) and at the end (inferring how Ryuuen discovered the VIP mechanics) and abducted that Kushida deliberately leaked her own identity as a "VIP" after noticing the incongruity between her lack of ambition for victory and the resulting benefit to Class D.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Observing the inactivity and evident lack of training of Class C from the windows during practice, Suzune sensed that Ryuuen was not competing in traditional sporting terms, but preparing an unconventional strategy of interference or sabotage.

   * The idiot trio (Y1V6): To find out why the idiot trio abandoned Kiyotaka

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Upon noticing the complete absence of visible Class C study groups in the library during the preceding days, Suzune inferred that Class C was not studying in a conventional manner, the most likely hypothesis being that Ryuuen was fully relying on a method of cheating or leaking information through a traitor.

   * Kiyotaka Analysis (Y1V1-Y1V11.5-Now): Upon seeing Kiyotaka's grade, Suzune immediately deduced that he was holding back.

   * Invitation to the Student Council (Y1V7.5): Realizes that it is yet another of his schemes. In order to avoid getting involved, she refuses the offer, even when Kiyotaka attempts to persuade her by using her own brother.

   * Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): Upon observing the clues left by Chabashira during the explanation of the exam rules, she concluded that the school was seeking to force an intentional expulsion in that generation to test them. Furthermore, she abduce the scheme by Sakayanagi to undermine Class C.

   * Sakayanagi and Kiyotaka relationship (Y1V9/Y1V10/Y1V11.5): In Y1V11 Suzune uses deduction to rule out chance (if A is a genius and B is a valuable resource, A doesn't give B to a stranger) and then employs abduction to theorize the existence of a hidden relationship between Kiyotaka and Sakayanagi.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): With incomplete information about the events that Class A would choose, she abduced that their inclusion of physical events was a bluff and that they would concentrate their true power on tests of medium/high academic participation. Furthermore,  Hirata was in a state of depression. Suzune knew it was better to leave him alone with his thoughts, as approaching him directly could be counterproductive.

Intuition -
Nearly all of her decisions are guided by intuition, which demonstrates that she possesses an exceptionally sharp intuitive sense.
   * Kushida Analysis (Y1V1-Now): Suzune was able to intuitively interpret Kushida's intentions and plans, as well as her true nature.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She had a precise intuition that, although the plan to hide the VIP seemed to have worked on paper, something had gone wrong and they had lost the test.

   * Kiyotaka Analysis (Y1V2-Now): To sense whenever Kiyotaka is involved in a problem and his participation in its creation or solution. Example:
   * Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): To sense that everything that was happening, especially the issue with his brother, was influenced by Kiyotaka.
   * Sakayanagi and Kiyotaka relationship (Y1V9/Y1V10/Y1V11.5): To suspect that Kiyotaka was responsible for spreading the rumors to counter Sakayanagi

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune was able to sense the School (Tsukishiro's) intervention and the hole he could use to affect them from the explanation of the rules

   * VR game (Y1V11): Intuitively learned how to play a VR RPG that brought real-life skills into the game without any prior gaming experience.

Problem Solving -
   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): She resolved multiple unforeseen crises: the initial refusal to study, the conflict with Class C in the library, the drastic modification of the assessment syllabus, and Sudo's final failure by a single point in English.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Turn the student with the worst grades and the biggest behavioral issues into a model student and the strongest weapon of the class.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V2-Y1V11.5-now): Turn the worst Class D in ANHS history into a functional and competitive team.

   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Avoid demerits for Sudou

   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): Avoid demerits for Class D, resolve the core issue, prevent future repercussions, reform Sudou, and make the enemy pay the price.

   * Sudou Carry Tactic (Y1V4.5): Ensured winning the bet against Ichinose and her classmates.

   * Paper Shuffle planning (Y1V6): Secure a complete victory for Class D, prevent dual expulsions, neutralize the traitor and turn them into a usable asset, increase class points, improve relations with Class B and carry out her revenge against Ryuuen.

   * MTC Suzune plan (Y1V8): Avoid expulsions in MTC.

   * Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): To make the most useless student in the class and a traitor the scapegoat in order to avoid losing someone.

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11): Force Kiyotaka to take the role of commander, placing the most capable student, and the holder of a protection point, at the forefront to maximize the chances of victory and prevent expulsions.

Learning Ability -
   * Academics (Y1V1-now): Suzune is able to maintain her position among the students with the best academic performance.

   * Chess Feat (Y1V11): The time it takes to become good at chess depends on dedication and the skill level one wants to reach.

The usual average is to gain 100 points in a few months for players with 800-1000 elo. Players with a rating of 1600 to 2000 may take 4 years or more to gain 100 points. To master any skill, 10,000 hours of practice are needed. GMs practice 20 hours per week on average, which equals 500 weeks or 10 years.

Hashimoto had been playing chess for months. Among Class A and the school's elite, he was the best and Sakayanagi's personal student, a genius nad a great master who had been playing for over 8 years.

This would put him above 1000 elo at the very least and a maximum of 1500 elo for being an elite. However, Suzune managed to surpass him completely in a week (If we subtract her 8 hours of sleep per day, the 8 hours of classes, the time spent on daily activities such as cooking, eating, bathing, or changing clothes, and also remove her daily hour of studying with Sudou, with the class, and individually, Suzune would be left with approximately 2 free hours per day to practice with Kiyotaka. Over the course of a week, that would amount to about 14 hours.)  achieving an elo of 1100-1600 without ever touching a chess piece and have no previous experience with any similar game.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She learned to teach and tutor by adapting her pedagogy to someone with severe academic deficiencies, optimizing Sudou's study time to focus only on the topics that maximized his OAA score.

   * She is herself (Y1V1-Y1V11.5): Throughout the series, Suzune undergoes continuous growth and self-improvement. By the end of the first year, she had gained substantial experience as the leader of Class D, successfully transforming what was considered the worst class into a functional and organized unit. Along the way, she learned how to identify and deal with different types of adversaries: a two-faced manipulator like Kushida, a violent and unpredictable opponent like Ryuuen, and a genius-level like Sakayanagi or a traitor like Yamauchi.

Personal ranking for the best Learning Ability feats:

Adaptability -
   * S-System (Y1V1): When Sae revealed the truth behind the S-System, Suzune remained calm. She immediately calculated how many points the class had lost and, based on that assessment, devised a clear long-term strategy to reach Class A.

   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): Sudou had fallen asleep and failed to study English. Faced with this problem, Suzune devised an efficient method that allowed him to learn what was essential in a short amount of time and maximize the number of points he could earn on the exam. Furthermore, when she discovered in the teachers' lounge just 10 minutes before the end of the break that the school had changed the exam topics (rendering a week of study useless), Suzune immediately adapted the group's approach without collapsing.

   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Ryuuen uses Ishizaki, Komiya and Kondo to set a trap for Sudou, taking advantage of his poor self-control. This leads to the school’s intervention through a trial against Sudou, which could result in a temporary suspension and a loss of class points. Suzune manages to devise a plan to avoid a negative outcome for the class: she saves Sudou, prevents the loss of class points, solidifies an alliance with Class B, and identifies the mastermind of Class C using minimal resources.

   * Special Exams (Y1V3-Y1V11-Now): Each time a new special exam is revealed, Suzune is able to create a strategy to save class points and her classmates.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She adapted to the hostile composition of her group (filled with influential figures like Kanzaki and Katsuragi) and to Ryuuen's constant interruptions, adjusting her defensive stance in real time to protect Kushida throughout the entire course of the test.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Faced with the class's initial rejection of the 'Skill-Based' system, Suzune adapts her proposal by offering a private points compensation system to mitigate the discontent of the students with the worst athletic performance.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): Faced with Hirata's sudden and inevitable emotional decline, she readjusted the class structure so as not to depend on him as a key asset, reorganizing roles without altering the effectiveness of the overall plan.

Personal ranking for the best Adaptability feats:
Special Exams (Y1V3-Y1V11-Now) >> Sudou Trial (Y1V2) > Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1) > S-System (Y1V1)

Mind Reading -
   * Kiyotaka Analysis (Y1V1-Now): Suzune is capable of reading the thoughts of Kiyotaka’s second persona—the one that represents the ordinary student—while simultaneously identifying the emotions and feelings of his third persona, his truest self. Clear examples of this ability can be found throughout Volumes 1 and 2, where Suzune is able to discern the intentions behind Kiyotaka’s façade simply by observing him. Another notable instance appears in Volume 7, when she recognizes Kiyotaka’s genuine happiness upon finally obtaining a group of friends.

Information Gathering -
Suzune has exceptional perception, but during the first year her primary source of information was her position as class leader. This role allowed her to request and consolidate information from all 38 students, even when someone was unwilling to cooperate. In such cases, Hirata and Karuizawa—who operated under her leadership—could effectively persuade them. Her influence as a leader was not confined to her own class; it also extended to Class B through a stable, friendly relationship, as well as through the authority and presence she projected toward the other classes.

   * Kushida (Y1V1-Now): She gathered information about Kushida's social behavior in middle school solely by listening to distant conversations and hallway rumors, piecing together the hypothetical scenario of the destruction of her former class.

   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): Noticing inconsistencies in the preparation, she went directly to the teachers' office to question and verify with the tutor the exact topics that would be on the test before it was too late.

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She actively visited the Class C, B, and A camps. She extracted crucial information about Ryuuen's massive point expenditure, Ichinose's cohesion strategy, and discovered through verbal pressure the existence of the claim device in the Class A cave.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She continuously monitored and recorded Sudou's study habits, attendance, disciplinary offenses, and physical metrics to quantitatively evaluate his progress and growth.

   * Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6): She used different test banks to probe the school's limits on what kind of complex questions and interpretations were permissible on the exam, accumulating valuable data before drafting the final test.

Multi Tasking -
Suzune is capable of handling multiple tasks simultaneously. She is frequently shown managing two processes at once, typically engaging in conversation while analyzing the situation in the background. Her greatest feat in this regard takes place during the island exam: despite suffering from a 38 °C fever, she engaged in close-quarters combat with Ibuki while simultaneously analyzing how to resolve the situation and identifying the true culprit behind the fire in Class D.

Manipulation Categories:
Direct Manipulation -
   * Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1): Suzune needs a warhorse, someone she can use as both operational muscle and a connection to the rest of the class. To achieve this, she exploits her link with Kiyotaka, directing and employing him as the “ordinary guy” who can carry out her plans.

She assigns him specific tasks like gathering the three idiots for the study group, conducting investigations, providing support, and actively participating in the false camera strategy to save Sudou. Kiyotaka had to follow her around the island, visiting each class and then helping her separate the boys and girls and finally to help her escape from a bottle in her hand.

There was some resistance, naturally, but it was quickly dealt with. A few physical threats, compass included, were enough to bring him back in line.

   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): She used the truth about their imminent risk of expulsion directly and bluntly as a verbal lever to force them to submit to her study regime.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She guided Sudou's behavior through direct but formative verbal confrontations, such as when she touched his pride in the first study group or when she kept him under control in the student council room.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-now): Suzune had already helped the class improve in the past. She started with Kiyotaka immediately after the S-System was revealed. By staying constantly at his side, she allowed him to act as nothing other than a model student, correcting him in a direct and authoritarian manner to force him to behave properly.

Besides, she was the one who managed to raise the so-called “idiot trio’s” grades enough for them to pass the final exams.

Once in a leadership position, she established a clear chain of command and became a figure the class could follow with confidence.

Suzune initiated consistent study groups aimed at raising the class’s overall academic level and preventing expulsions due to midterm, final exams or an Academic Special Exam.

She focused particularly on providing personal tutoring to Sudou, one of the strongest students in terms of physical ability, but whose academic level was comparable to that of a child who had barely finished elementary school. Thanks to Suzune’s study plan, Sudou gradually improved and became more integrated into the class.

In addition, she managed potential conflicts with other classes, for example; deliberately ignoring Ryuuen’s provocations during his search for “X”.

Suzune also encouraged students who wished to join clubs to do so, allowing the class to earn additional points through club activities. She took an active interest in her classmates and learned each of their individual talents and strengths. Something that supported the class with private points and class points
She was the leader and devised a way to win the Paper Shuffle, preventing them from being eliminated. She also ensured no one was eliminated in MTC, leading the girls to victory, reducing the impact of the rumours strike, expelling Yamauchi and organizing the events of Class C.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She guided Sudou directly, motivating him the captaincy of the event and establishing clear motivational conditions (such as the goal of being the MVP) to turn his overflowing physical energy into the cornerstone of the class's athletic performance.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Through a face-to-face confrontation, Suzune cornered Kushida, openly revealing her intentions and offering her a calculated wager. She used this direct confrontation to force Kushida to accept terms that would compel her to seek Ryuuen's help.

   * Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): She confronted Kushida directly in front of the entire class, using Kushida's own "mask of kindness" against her to force her to validate Yamauchi's guilt and dismantle the voting group.

Personal ranking for the best Direct Manipulation feats:
D class upgrade (Y1V1-now) >>> Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1)

Indirect Manipulation -
   * Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): Instead of directly confronting the aggressors herself, she orchestrated the plan from the shadows using third parties (Kushida to summon them, Kanzaki/Sotomura for the camera, and Ichinose/Kiyotaka for the direct confrontation).

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune used Kushida to control and manipulate Ryuuen's movements in the "Paper Shuffle"

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal Strategy (Y1V11): Sudou stood up and took the floor. He wanted to know why Kiyotaka had received 42 praise points in the previous exam. His question sparked curiosity among the students, who cast doubtful glances at Kiyotaka.

Suzune reduced the entire situation to a simple conclusion: Sakayanagi had orchestrated everything to expel Yamauchi for personal reasons, and Kiyotaka’s involvement was nothing more than a coincidence.

This situation created an opportunity for Kiyotaka to step forward as the commander, ensuring no one else had to risk expulsion while also clearing his own name.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): She manipulated Kiyotaka through his summary notes to lead the class to victory against Class A. Manipulating his class without being in the position of commander.

Personal ranking for the best Indirect Manipulation feats:
Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > Kiyotaka's self-proposal Strategy (Y1V11) > ESE Planning (Y1V11) >> Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10)

Mass Manipulation -
   * Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): She convinced her class that Yamauchi was the best choice to vote for in the "Class Poll," even when half of them had already planned and were certain about voting for Kiyotaka as a sacrificial pawn. In short, she took complete control of the 39 students in the classroom. Through a logical and relentless presentation, she altered the voting behavior of the majority of the class, dismantling the mass voting group that Kushida and Yamauchi had built.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-now): In her pursuit of strengthening Class D, Suzune went as far as manipulating 38 students of her own class—excluding Koenji, and herself—along with a teacher and Ichinose and her class, ensuring that they followed her plans whenever necessary achieving a total account of 79 people in total under her control when the situation required it.

Personal ranking for the best Mass Manipulation feats:
D class upgrade (Y1V1-now) > Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10)

Emotional Manipulation -
   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8):  Suzune became known as the main reason Sudou avoided expulsion. She not only followed him closely to ensure he could pass the exam, but deliberately lowered her own score to reduce the class average and give him a chance to survive. Even when he failed, she firmly demanded that the decision be overturned, and her uncompromising attitude ultimately caused Sudou to fall in love with her.

Suzune was aware of Sudou’s strong feelings toward her (believing them to be gratitude rather than love) and did not hesitate to use them to achieve her goals.

She first reformed him by making him understand the damage his behavior caused to the class, instilling guilt and discipline to prevent further incidents. From then on, she was the one who restrained him whenever he was about to act recklessly. She also pushed him to train harder and improve his standing in his sports club, directly contributing to higher class points.

When Sudou tried to call her by her first name as a way to grow closer to his “savior,” Suzune, fully aware of his intentions, turned it into an incentive: he would only be allowed to do so if he earned the MVP title at the sports festival. This both discouraged improper familiarity and motivated him to fight for the class’s success.

In the end, she used his feelings as leverage to force him to study seriously and raise his academic average, turning him into a loyal and protective dog under her control.

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11): Control the emotions of Shinohara, Sudou, Ike to force Kiyotaka to self-propose him as a leader of the exam

Personal ranking for the best Emotional Manipulation feats:
Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8) > Kiyotaka's self-proposal (Y1V11) > False Horikita Manabu (Y1V1) > Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1)..

Logical Manipulation -
   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8):
   * Uses shame, comparison, and accountability without alienation.
→
   * Gradually ties Sudou’s self-worth to class contribution.
→ .
   * Result:
   * Transforms a liability into a semi-reliable class asset

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She defused Shinohara's protest by coldly demonstrating that, given her lack of athletic ability, her chances of winning rewards in a free competition were virtually nil, forcing her to accept the pairing system as her most advantageous option.

   * Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6):
   * Constructs a functional chain of command by leveraging Hirata’s moral authority and Karuizawa’s social influence.
        ->
   * Frames her leadership as necessary, not desirable.
->
   * Forces compliance by tying participation to exam survival.
   * A Result:
   * Leadership legitimacy achieved without formal election
   * Centralized decision-making accepted by the class

   * Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10):
   * Starts the exam Allows voting blocs to form, exposing traitors organically.
->
   * Delays action until information asymmetry peaks.
->
   * Maximizes impact of final speech.
->
   * She reduces the vote to innocent vs judge vs traitor, eliminating emotional neutrality.
->
   * Each counterargument is anticipated and neutralized in advance.
->
   * She weaponizes Kushida’s credibility to destroy Yamauchi’s defense.
->
   * By offering herself as a valid target, she removes resistance while knowing it will not be chosen.
->
   * The class believes it is choosing freely, but all rational paths converge on Yamauchi.
   * Result:
   * Expulsion of Class A pawn
   * Protection of Kiyotaka
   * Subjugation of Kushida
   * Behavioral correction of Sudou
   * Class unity stabilized
Also, Suzune does not remove Kushida—she logically cages her.
   * Public exposure without total destruction preserves utility.
->
   * Reminder of prior agreement + social pressure ensures compliance.
->
   * Kushida is left with no logical incentive to rebel again.
   * Result:
   * Converts an existential internal threat into a controlled asset

Personal ranking for the best Logical Manipulation feats:
Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10) > Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6) >  Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8) > Class B Alliance (Y1V2-Y1V11.5) > ….

Individual Manipulation -
   * Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1): She assigns him specific tasks like gathering the three idiots for the study group, conducting investigations, providing support, and actively participating in the false camera strategy to save Sudou. Kiyotaka had to follow her around the island, visiting each class and then helping her separate the boys and girls, help her escape from a bottle in her hand and other things.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Manipulating Sudou into a smart student with above-average grades, who contributes to the class with his club training, doesn't cause trouble, and is loyal to her and her cause.

   * Kushida control (Y1V6/Y1V8/Y1V10): Suzune and Kushida are rivals and outright enemies. This opposition forced Suzune to develop a deep understanding of how Kushida thinks, giving her the ability to anticipate her behavior and control her actions to serve her own objectives.

During the Paper Shuffle, Suzune used Kushida as a tool to manipulate Ryuuen’s movements and steer the exam toward her victory. In the Mixed Training Camp (MTC), she exploited Kushida’s social skills, using her as an intermediary to gather and relay the class’s concerns. Finally, in the Class Poll, Suzune used Kushida to further bury Yamauchi, exposing him as a traitor and securing his downfall. All when Kushida only wanted to crush her, using her own schemes to her advantage.

   * Hirata Co-Leader: When no one in the class trusted Suzune, she leveraged Hirata’s social influence to seize leadership, using him as an intermediary between herself and the rest of the class. This allowed her to enforce her decisions without directly confronting collective resistance.

Once her leadership was established, Hirata became her aide, handling the social sphere alongside Karuizawa. Together, they acted as the class’s emotional bridge, allowing Suzune to focus on strategy and decision-making without being burdened by interpersonal management.

Personal ranking for the best Individual Manipulation feats:
Kushida control (Y1V6/Y1V8/Y1V10) > Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8) >  Hirata Co-Leader > Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1)

Event Manipulation -
   * Food Trap (Y1V1): Suzune uses Operation Friendship to force Kiyotaka into working for her and acting as her intermediary with the three idiots. She does this by accusing him of owing her a debt, claiming he abused her trust in the past, and using that moral leverage as a means to bring him in line with her plans.

   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): Suzune strategically makes use of three key events. The first is the revelation of the S-System, where the school exposes only the first layer of its true mechanics. She exploits this to plant doubt and lend credibility to her deception through the use of fake cameras.
The second event is the assault on Sudou by Ishizaki, Komiya, and Kondo, which adds another layer to the deception by leveraging the context of the incident to make them hesitate and question their position.
The third is Sudou’s trial, which reinforces the notion that the school knows far more than it lets on, applying sustained psychological pressure until their resistance collapses.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Suzune leverages her victory in the island exam as a source of legitimacy. She uses that achievement to put forward her ideas, ensure the class listens to her, and solidify her influence within the group.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She used the frustration of Sudou's past defeats (such as the Sports Festival incidents and the midterms) as learning catalysts, transforming his mistakes into lessons in self-control.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune starts from a concrete premise: the Paper Shuffle. In this exam, there is only one way to win—achieving a higher academic average than the opponent. Anticipating that Ryuuen would resort to cheating to secure that result, Suzune builds her strategy around that assumption, cutting off any opportunity for manipulation while simultaneously guiding and controlling him through Kushida’s actions.

   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10): She capitalized on the chaos of the Class Poll and the crisis created by the voting groups to intervene at the moment of greatest desperation. She used Sudou and Ike's history of misconduct and Yamauchi's past actions to construct an irrefutable narrative. Furthermore, she took advantage of the accumulated tension from the special exam and Sakayanagi's plot to restructure the internal dynamics of the class, discipline Sudou about his past mistakes, and corner Kushida for future exams.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She skillfully connected past events (such as the race against Manabu at the Sports Festival or Yamauchi's expulsion) to support her present lies and build a coherent story about why Ayanokoji seemed "average" and She restructured past events by posing as the mastermind to take credit and keep Kiyotaka off the radar.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): Suzune analyzes both the 10 potential events that could be selected from Class A and those 10 belonging to her own class, then trains Class D accordingly, preparing them in advance to counter each possible scenario.

Personal ranking for the best Event Manipulation feats:
Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) >= False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2) > ESE Planning (Y1V11) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > Food Trap (Y1V1)

Suicide Inducement -
No feats

Psychological Manipulation -
   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): Suzune used a fake camera to make them believe they had been recorded and that the school already possessed evidence of their deception, implying possible expulsion if they continued. She exploited standard but unfamiliar school procedures to her advantage. Combined with the heat of the building and the pressure from Ichinose, it made them realize that their own safety was greater than their fear of Ryuuen and his plans.

Social Manipulation -
   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Suzune devises the False Cameras Strategy, but to put it into action, she needs time. She secures it by calming Sudou's fury and manipulating the situation to force a delay, buying herself enough time until the desired conclusion is reached.

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Despite being ill, Suzune pretends to be in good condition to avoid being removed from the island and losing 30 class points. She personally takes responsibility for carrying the commander card.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): Suzune deliberately deceives every member of the Dragon Group to conceal the fact that Kushida is the VIP. The plan succeeds; their eventual loss is not due to a strategic failure, but to Kushida’s betrayal.

   * Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): She manipulated the group's social dynamics by cornering Kushida through her own "good girl" facade. She forced Kushida to turn against Yamauchi so as not to lose the class's favor or trust.

   * Kiyotaka’s Self-Proposal (Y1V11): Suzune outwardly covers for Kiyotaka as the apparent leader, while her true intention is to corner him strategically and force him into becoming the commander of the Event Selection Exam.

Personal ranking for the best Social Manipulation feats:
Kiyotaka’s Self-Proposal (Y1V11) > Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4) > Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3) > Sudou Trial (Y1V2) > Race with Manabu (Y1V5)

Deception Categories:
Misdirection -
   * Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): They fabricated physical evidence by placing a fake security camera to make the aggressors believe that the entire violent incident had been recorded from the beginning, leading them to a false but believable conclusion.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): Ayanokouji Kiyotaka is a hidden powerhouse, a resource capable of completely shifting the balance of power. Suzune is fully aware of his abilities and understands that, if she wants his support, she must uphold a clear agreement. First, she must not question or actively investigate his past. Second, she must protect him.

For example; Ryuuen had lost the island exam. He, having analyzed all the key figures, found it unreal that Suzune could come up with a strategy capable of ruining his plan, which led him to believe that she was merely a front for the true mastermind.

Ryuuen launched a psychological battle to extract information about the exam and the island test. However, Suzune controlled both her words and body language, avoiding his trap. Instead, her movements and statements had an effect on Ibuki. Additionally, she planted a bait regarding Kiyotaka, directly suggesting that he was the mastermind to confuse Ryuuen when he tried to extract a name from her. The tactic worked, successfully diverting suspicions away from Kiyotaka. (Y1V4)

Or another: She concealed her true intentions and the fact that her primary objective was to protect Kiyotaka. She downplayed to the class why Yamauchi chose Ayanokoji so as not to raise suspicions about his true abilities. (Y1V10)

Or Another: Sudou stood up and took the floor. He wanted to know why Kiyotaka had received 42 praise points in the previous exam. His question sparked curiosity among the students, who cast doubtful glances at Kiyotaka.

Suzune reduced the entire situation to a simple conclusion: Sakayanagi had orchestrated everything to expel Yamauchi for personal reasons, and Kiyotaka’s involvement was nothing more than a coincidence. (Y1V11)

Or Another: Suzune met with Ichinose to discuss the results of the Events Selection Exam.

During the meeting, Kiyotaka revealed that he was the one who convinced Ichinose to fight against Class A. Suzune immediately caught his lie but chose to remain silent. Instead, she maintained her role of covering for Kiyotaka’s independent actions without explicitly calling him out. And this continues through year 2 (see the Y2 doc)

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): With the exam concluded, Suzune made sure that no one in her group could identify Kushida as the VIP. She successfully deceived nine individuals considered "elites" within her year, including key figures like Katsuragi and Kanzaki, both central figures in their respective classes. Ryuuen being the only one who saw through their lies, not because of skill, but because Kushida betrayed them.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune was fully aware that Kushida would betray them again. Therefore, she chose to act in advance, eliminating the problem before any real damage could occur.

She convinced Chabashira to accept only the exams that were submitted directly by Suzune herself. Any other student attempting to turn in exams would be “received” normally, but those exams would be discarded without being counted. In this way, Suzune cut off the avenue of betrayal without alerting the class or raising suspicion.

The maneuver was effective on two levels. First, it completely neutralized the information leak. Second, it created the illusion of success for both Kushida and Ryuuen, who believed their plan was progressing as intended. In reality, they had already been deceived from the very beginning.

Personal ranking for the best misdirections feats:
Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5) > Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2) > Zodiac Exam Strategy (Y1V4) (By no information)

Fabrication -
   * Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1): Suzune drafted a contract in one night, which she flawlessly forged both Kiyotaka’s signature and seal. The document stated that he willingly accepted becoming her "war horse," ensuring his cooperation in her plans.

   * Kiyotaka’s Self-Proposal (Y1V11): She concocted a calculated lie in front of the entire class, arguing that "Kiyotaka didn't know why he had 42 commendation points" and blamed a plot by Sakayanagi to deflect suspicion from Kiyotaka's true abilities.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): The ten events the C class had chosen were English, basketball, archery, swimming, tennis, table tennis, typing, football, piano, and rock-paper-scissors, Aware that Class D had only a limited set of strategies to confront Class A, Suzune adapted to that constraint, making it extremely difficult to predict which one she would ultimately employ.

Personal ranking for the best Fabrication feats:
ESE Planning (Y1V11) >>>Kiyotaka contract (Y1V1)

Verbal Deception -
   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): Upon arriving at Class A’s base, Kiyotaka forced Suzune to step forward and investigate more closely. At the entrance, Yahiko attempted to block their way, but Suzune, with her sharp words, slowly convinced him that since they were nothing more than "brainless defects," there was no real issue in letting them in. However, Yahiko, loyal to his orders, refused to allow them entry. Suzune had anticipated this and used his desperation to extract valuable information: inside the cave, there was a reclamation device. Just as she was about to convince him to grant them access, Katsuragi appeared, cutting off their chances entirely.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): Ayanokouji Kiyotaka is a hidden powerhouse, a resource capable of completely shifting the balance of power. Suzune is fully aware of his abilities and understands that, if she wants his support, she must uphold a clear agreement. First, she must not question or actively investigate his past. Second, she must protect him.

Suzune takes on this responsibility seriously, not only deceiving her rivals, but also manipulating the perception of her own classmates to keep Kiyotaka out of the spotlight.

   * Race with Manabu (Y1V5): The class finished organizing their participation chart. Suzune called Sudou and Hirata to discuss it, as she had a specific goal in mind: she wanted to be the anchor in the 1200-meter relay. Since the anchor position is usually reserved for the fastest runner, both were confused by her proposal.

Suzune explained her reasoning: if Sudou ran first, he could establish an early lead and maintain the advantage throughout the race. In an individual race, a starting handicap can help protect a lane, but in a relay, that doesn’t work. From the second runner onward, the starting position depends on the previous runner’s placement, and the rules allow competitors to switch lanes to overtake others.

Suzune wanted to position Sudou as the first runner to secure an early lead. However, just being at the front didn’t guarantee that he could shake off his opponents. On the contrary, if Sudou pushed too hard, the gap would gradually close, increasing the pressure on the following runners.

She justified her strategy as unpredictable, but that wasn’t her true reason. In reality, she wanted to be the anchor so she could run alongside her brother and speak with him. She knew that if she proposed this in front of the entire class, many would oppose her, assuming she just wanted attention. That’s why she pulled Sudou and Hirata aside to present her idea privately, using the "unpredictable strategy" argument to sell them on it.

Personal ranking for the best Verbal Deception feats:
Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5) > Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3) > Race with Manabu (Y1V5) > Sudou Trial (Y1V2) > Food Trap (Y1V1)

Non Verbal Deception -
   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She concealed the trembling, sweating, and feverish discomfort with an upright posture and a cold, impassive expression. She also used decoys (approaching the card machine accompanied) to visually mislead the spies about who possessed the real card.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): During the meeting with Ichinose, when Kiyotaka lied, saying that he had convinced Ichinose to fight against Class A, Suzune caught on to the lie instantly and maintained an impassive and neutral expression so as not to give him away.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): When it was time for her meeting, Suzune found Katsuragi waiting. Katsuragi spoke to her with some caution, as she now carried the reputation of having won the island exam. However, Suzune responded without intimidation. Kanzaki indirectly supported her in the verbal confrontation against Katsuragi.

Things became more tense when Ryuuen arrived, attempting to provoke those present. However, Suzune saw through his strategy and responded coldly, refusing to lower her head with her body language. This approach was maintained throughout the entire special exam. Suzune deliberately withheld critical information, forcing Ryuuen to take action in an attempt to draw out the true mastermind.

With the exam concluded, Suzune made sure that no one in her group could identify Kushida as the VIP and protect Kiyotaka identity. She successfully deceived nine individuals considered "elites" within her year, including key figures like Katsuragi and Kanzaki, both central figures in their respective classes. Ryuuen being the only one who saw through their lies, not because of skill, but because Kushida betrayed them.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune delivered a perfectly theatrical performance, kneeling and displaying a face of collapse and defeat before Ryuuen in the teachers' lounge. This body language completely convinced the leader of Class C that victory was assured, preventing him from taking any last-minute countermeasures.

   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10): She concealed her own surprise and disturbance at Hirata's violent physical reaction by adjusting her bangs. She maintained absolute composure and impassivity throughout the confrontation to project firmness and authority.

Mass Deception -
   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): She managed to deceive a group of targeted students (Ishizaki, Komiya, Kondō and the influence of Class C), causing the official lawsuit that affected the positions of the entire school to collapse.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She managed to deceive the vast majority of the Dragon group (a group of more than 5 people, including prominent figures such as Katsuragi and Kanzaki) by keeping them in the dark about Kushida's true identity as a VIP.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): Suzune not only deceiving her rivals, but also manipulating the perception of her own classmates to keep Kiyotaka out of the spotlight, Including the upper grades means at least 470 students and teachers, obviously not counting those expelled and those who found out through other means.

Acting Skills -
   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): The island test began, and Suzune was sick (37.5-38°C throughout the week). Despite her condition, she distanced herself from the class and acted naturally to hide it, not wanting to become a burden. Her performance was so convincing that she managed to deceive all 39 classmates, the leaders and co-leaders of the other classes, keeping them unaware of her true condition. Only Kiyotaka and Ibuki (after a fight with her) were able to notice her fever throughout the entire exam.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She played the role of the authoritarian and protective leader in front of the class with complete naturalness, pretending that Kiyotaka was simply following her orders or that he was unaware of the reasons behind certain events in order to maintain the plausibility of her lies.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): To secure her perfect victory, Suzune waited until the last moment to submit her exam questions, anticipating that Ryuuen would do the same just to spite her.

She walked with him to the faculty room and submitted her questions in front of Sae. Then, Suzune and Sae began their performance.

Sae pretended to warn her indirectly that something had happened, while Suzune, in front of Ryuuen, acted as if she was piecing things together. Finally, she fell to her knees in despair, as if she had just realized Kushida’s betrayal.

Thus, she deceived Ryuuen (and Kiyotaka who accompanied her) into believing his plan had worked. Satisfied, Ryuuen left, unaware that he had been caught in Suzune’s trap from the very beginning.

Personal ranking for the best Acting Skills feats:
Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) >= Sick on beach (Y1V3) > Zodiac Exam Strategy (Y1V4) >  Sudou Trial  (Y1V2)

Bluffing -
   * Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): The entire strategy hinged on creating the illusion that they had definitive proof (the video recording of the fight) when in reality they possessed nothing. Maintaining this position of superiority over the Class C criminals forced the withdrawal of their complaint.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She maintains an inflexible and confident stance during the questions to Sae Chabashira about the regulations in order to project authority to the class and dominate the initial debate.

Strategic Deception -
   * Fake study group. (Y1V1): Sudou, Ike, and Yamauchi have an academic level comparable to that of an elementary school student trying to solve a high school exam, yet they are required to achieve a passing grade within two weeks for the midterm exams. Suzune seriously doubts that they can succeed, but still considers helping them, as she unconsciously deduces that their expulsion could result in demerits for the class. However, she is unwilling to invest time and resources in students she deems useless. From her perspective, it is more efficient to focus on other matters and allow the dead weight to be eliminated so they stop dragging down the class’s overall average.

With this objective in mind, she decides to test them to determine whether they are truly worth the effort. To achieve this, she manipulated Kiyotaka into helping her form a study group to evaluate them. After a short period, she confirmed their academic uselessness and chose to abandon them, deliberately dismantling the study group to ensure their expulsion and prevent them from continuing to drag the class down with their poor grades.

   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Sae revealed Sudo's problem, and after analyzing the situation, Suzune decided to help him once again. However, she realized that if she kept bailing him out without consequence, Sudo would continue causing trouble in the future. For that reason, she pretended not to help him on the surface, aiming for him to understand the true reason he was incriminated and to reflect on his actions so he wouldn't repeat them.

Suzune adopted this attitude because she knew the idiot trio and Kushida would wonder why she wasn’t helping this time if she had done so before a question that would inevitably fall on Kiyotaka. To guide him, she left small hints, trusting that he was intelligent enough to notice them and answer in her place, which he did.

When she saw them falling into despair, she decided to approach Kiyotaka to share what she had discovered so he could relay it to the others.

   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): Suzune had managed to postpone the trial to the following day, now with the possibility of expulsion on the table. Regardless of whether they won or lost, the conflict had escalated to a point where both sides could suffer negative consequences. Faced with this situation, Suzune concludes that the best course of action is to have the opposing side withdraw their complaint in order to avoid demerits and more severe repercussions.

Then Suzune tricks them by using Ichinose and Kiyotaka to get them to withdraw their complaint with fake cameras.

In the end, the strategy worked perfectly. Ishizaki, Komiya, and Kondō withdrew their complaint in defeat, with Ichinose and Kiyotaka watching them closely to prevent any last-minute escape attempts. The entire staging combined verbal and non-verbal deception supported by the tactical placement of props (the camera bought by Kanzaki) to force the voluntary withdrawal of a lawsuit against the school.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She responded to Ryuuen's provocations with an attitude of absolute confidence and unwavering rejection, feigning total control of the situation so as not to show weakness.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune deliberately offers her a path to achieve her objective: Suzune proposes a wager in which she puts her own expulsion on the line. Kushida, having no other viable route, focuses entirely on defeating Suzune through this method.

Suzune is fully aware that both of them acknowledge her clear academic superiority, and therefore anticipates that Kushida will resort to cheating to secure her victory. Based on Kushida’s previous alliance with Ryuuen, Suzune predicts that she will employ the same strategy again. As a countermeasure, Suzune acts in advance by speaking with Chabashira and having her pretend to receive Kushida’s exam papers. This ensures that Suzune’s class gains a higher chance of victory in the special exam due to the false information Kushida submits, while also giving Kushida a false sense of security that negatively affects her final performance.

The outcome is Kushida’s defeat and her forced submission to Suzune, while simultaneously inflicting damage on Ryuuen’s class.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): Each class had to submit ten events along with their rules. The events needed to be well-known enough for all students to understand, such as rock-paper-scissors, poker, or chess. For written exams or similar events, the school would create the questions to ensure fairness. Additionally, the rules had to prevent the possibility of a tie.

The ten events had to be unique, relatively short, and could not be repeated within the same class. Once registered, they could not be withdrawn.

Although each class had to register ten events, only five would be considered official, while the other five served as decoys.

Personal ranking for the best Strategic Deception feats:
Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > ESE Planning (Y1V11) > False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2) > Sudou Trial (Y1V2) => Fake study group. (Y1V1)

Black Mail -
   * Food Trap (Y1V1): Suzune requires an intermediary: Kiyotaka. However, Kiyotaka lacks the motivation to get involved and has no reason to do her any favors. Aware of this, Suzune invites him to lunch, pays for his meal, and uses the uncertainty of the situation to pressure him into acting.

   * Food Strategy 2 (Y1V11): At 6 p.m., Suzune texted Kiyotaka, inviting him to her house to discuss a topic she didn’t specify. Kiyotaka preferred for her to come to his place, but Suzune used the excuse that he had been at the center of many problems to pressure him into going to her house.

When Kiyotaka arrived, he found her preparing dinner. Suzune forced him to sit, applying silent pressure. After about 15 minutes, she served the meal.

She had prepared a healthy dish with daikon radish as the base.

Though Kiyotaka hesitated, remembering past experiences, Suzune convinced him to eat. After he took a bite, she revealed her true intention: to show him the analyzed data of the class. As commander, this was basic information Kiyotaka needed to know, so he didn’t object. And the second objective was for Kiyotaka to support her in analyzing the events of Class A

   * Confession recording strategy (Y1V5): After the sports festival and confirming Kushida’s actions and motives, Suzune turned her attention to Ryuuen. She spoke as if everything had happened by mere coincidence and manipulated him into confessing. She had been recording their conversation. Suzune then used that recording as blackmail, intending to avoid kneeling and close the case.

But Ryuuen had already foreseen this. He played his own recording, leaving her with no options.

Personal ranking for the best Black Mail feats:
Confession recording strategy (Y1V5) > Food Trap (Y1V1) >= Food Strategy 2 (Y1V11)

Gaslighting -
   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10): Suzune convinced 36 people that Yamauchi was the best choice to vote for in the "Class Poll," even when half of them had already planned and were certain about voting for Kiyotaka as a sacrificial pawn.

Guilt Tripping -
She knows how to subtly appeal to the responsibility and pride of her classmates so that they take on the weight of the class.

Threats/Extortion -
   * Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1):  Suzune seeks his attention for something, but when she realizes he is ignoring her by “pretend sleep strategy” she threatens him with various punishments: filling his chair with thumbtacks, pouring water on his head every time he enters the bathroom, and even stabbing him with a compass needle. Faced with these threats, Kiyotaka gives in and complains, reminding her that she had already stabbed him before. However, Suzune demands proof—proof that he, despite his fear, is unable to provide. Even though he is aware of the cameras in the classroom and Suzune is holding a compass in her hand, Kiyotaka cannot back up his claim, leaving him at the mercy of his intimidating classmate.

Another: When Kiyotaka started dozing off in class, Suzune stabbed him with a compass needle to keep him awake. The sudden pain snapped him out of his drowsiness immediately. Moreover, if he ever felt sleepy again, the fear of Suzune repeating the action would prevent him from falling asleep. Her method proved effective, though Kiyotaka later complained about it after class.

   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): She implicitly intimidated the students in Class C by threatening to expose the alleged recording to the school administration, which would result in severe punishment for them for lying and wasting the institution's time.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Using Manabu to force Kushida to accept his bet in order to control her.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): Koenji will not be participating in the new exam as usual. Suzune, predicting this, sought Koenji’s confirmation, but he merely laughed, stating he would only attend this one meeting. Seizing the opportunity, Suzune skillfully drew him into the conversation, manipulating him into proclaiming, through his own arrogance, that he could win any event placed before him. This effectively cornered him—if he failed, it would only increase distrust toward him, making him a prime target for future special exams, similar to the "Class Poll" in the previous volume.

Koenji noticed Suzune’s subtle threats but dismissed them. Nevertheless, Suzune fully expected results from him.

Personal ranking for the best Threats/Extortion feats:
ESE Planning (Y1V11) >= Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1)

Fear Inducement -
   * Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1): (Author: It's not psychological and physical torture to turn him into her obedient war horse, it's love, believe me. It's just Tsundere)

   * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): Suzune deliberately engineered a situation where, to achieve her goal, she needed to instill enough fear to force Ishizaki, Komiya, and Kondo to withdraw their complaint. She built her plan around three key elements.

First, psychology. She used a fake camera to make them believe they had been recorded and that the school already possessed evidence of their deception, implying possible expulsion if they continued. She exploited standard but unfamiliar school procedures to her advantage.

Second, the environment. She summoned them to the special building at 3:40 p.m., right after classes ended. At that time, with direct sunlight hitting the building, temperatures between 25-30 °C, and the air conditioning turned off, the heat became oppressive, causing thermal stress and cognitive impairment that hindered rational thinking.

Finally, social pressure. Ichinose—the most popular student of the year and the leader of the class they had previously targeted—applied direct pressure. The combination of fear of expulsion, suffocating heat, and social confrontation allowed Suzune to achieve her objective of breaking their resolve and forcing them to betray the leader who held them under control.

Personal ranking for the best Fear Inducement feats:
False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2) >> Kiyotaka's demon (Y1V1)

Love Bombing -
Jajajajaajajj. No

Negotiation -
   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): She successfully negotiated with Kushida to join the project and, at the end of the test, decisively intervened to negotiate the purchase of the point that Sudo was missing using her own private points in front of Professor Sae.

   * Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): She established an alliance/non-aggression pact with Ichinose using the connections she had already built in previous volumes, ensuring a common front and sharing information about the suspicious behavior of Class C.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): At the end of the exam, after deducing Kiyotaka's move, she negotiated a lasting agreement with him: to act as his public shield/cover in exchange for his strategic cooperation in leading the class to Class A.

   * Ryuuen Zodiac Exam proposition (Y1V4): During the Dragon Group meeting, the discussion lasted far longer than the allotted time. This was because Ryuuen proposed an alliance between three classes to crush Class A, claiming he had control over the three VIPs from Class C.

While Suzune didn’t think the idea itself was bad, the real issue was the person proposing it. To her, Ryuuen was completely untrustworthy, and accepting such a deal even under contract would be like digging her own grave. To pressure her, Ryuuen extended the offer to Ichinose and Class B right in front of Suzune. If Ichinose accepted, it would be a complete defeat for Class D.

At that moment, Suzune firmly spoke up, aiming to dissuade Ichinose with a clear indirect warning: if she betrayed Class D now, she would be seen as a traitor by all the other classes as well. A person who turns on their allies in the blink of an eye for the sake of points can never be trusted.

After the discussion ended, Suzune remained in the room with Ichinose, Kanzaki, and Kiyotaka. They discussed their alliance and ultimately decided to handle things individually.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Hirata introduced a voting system called 'Raise Your Hand,' where students could express their preferred order of participation. However, Suzune interrupted and proposed the 'Skill-Based' system, in which their athletic abilities would be assessed to maximize efficiency, which was the only viable option if they truly wanted to surpass the other classes.

Suzune advocated for prioritizing the most athletic students in the Recommended Participation events, allowing them to choose which competitions to enter. Similarly, in the Universal Participation events, they needed to form the best possible combinations to ensure victory and fully utilize their potential. Simply put: the fastest students should be paired with the slower ones.

This approach meant that to prevent direct clashes between top athletes like Hirata and Sudou, adjustments were necessary. However, this strategy also harshly excluded the weaker students.

Suzune had already anticipated the lack of appreciation for her idea and presented a compromise: students who didn’t need high exam scores could use their private points to compensate for the points lost by lower-performing students. This plan, while slightly reducing their chances of victory, mitigated the risk of defeat and increased overall acceptance.

Personal ranking for the best Negotiation feats:
'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > Ryuuen Zodiac Exam proposition (Y1V4) > Panties Problem (Y1V3) > Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1)

Concealment -
   * Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): She concealed the truth about the lack of real evidence at the school and installed the camera in a discreet location within the building so that it would appear to be a genuine surveillance camera and not raise immediate suspicions.

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She concealed the leader's card by keeping it hidden inside her underwear/jacket at all times, as well as hiding her critical physical condition to prevent other classes from making her an easy target or her own class from losing morale.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): Suzune is fully aware of his abilities and understands that, if she wants his support, she must uphold a clear agreement. First, she must not question or actively investigate his past. Second, she must protect him. Suzune takes on this responsibility seriously, not only deceiving her rivals, but also manipulating the perception of her own classmates to keep Kiyotaka out of the spotlight. For xample in:
   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10): She concealed her true intentions and the fact that her primary objective was to protect Kiyotaka (the class's most valuable asset). She downplayed to the class why Yamauchi chose Ayanokoji so as not to raise suspicions about his true abilities.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): With the exam concluded, Suzune made sure that no one in her group could identify Kushida as the VIP. She successfully deceived nine individuals considered "elites" within her year, including key figures like Katsuragi and Kanzaki, both central figures in their respective classes. Ryuuen being the only one who saw through their lies, not because of skill, but because Kushida betrayed them

   * Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): She kept her true knowledge and intentions hidden for days. Despite being aware of the formation of the massive voting group, she didn't intervene immediately or reveal that she knew of Yamauchi's pact with Sakayanagi, strategically waiting until the last day to disclose the information.

Personal ranking for the best Concealment feats:
Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5) >> Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4)

Information Control -
   * Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): She meticulously regulated what information went in and out: she used the location of the special building, restricted the suspects' attempts to call for help, and kept Sudo isolated in the student council so that he wouldn't leak anything or ruin the trap.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She carefully regulated what data she shared and what she kept hidden, both from Class D (giving precise instructions to Sudo to prevent leaks) and from rival classes.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): She maintains rigorous control of data on the physical performance of the class through private assessment tests to formulate the best pairing combinations.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): She kept the final question bank a closely guarded secret. She divided the task of compiling the questions between Hirata, Yukimura, and herself, ensuring that only she had the complete final set to prevent any leaks to Kushida or Class C.

   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10): A large group formed with the goal of expelling Kiyotaka. To save him, Suzune used the information she had deduced about Yamauchi and redirected the class consensus to make him the new target for expulsion. She withheld information about Yamauchi's behavior, absences, grades, and the conspiracy with Sakayanagi until the exact moment. She controlled the flow of information to maximize the emotional and psychological impact in the classroom.

   * ESE Planning (Y1V11): She knew how to manage the class's internal communication. She created a confidential chat group using Kushida Kikyo as an intermediary/front to avoid suspicion or leaks from spies in the hallways.

Persuasion -
   * Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1): She managed to convince three undisciplined students to join her study group by showing them the harsh reality of their imminent expulsion.

   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She established an alliance/non-aggression pact with Ichinose using the connections she had already built in previous volumes, ensuring a common front and sharing information about the suspicious behavior of Class C.

   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4): She managed to persuade Ichinose to reject or question Ryuuen's toxic alliance, and convinced her own group by analyzing the results that Ryuuen had executed a systematic betrayal based on the pattern of the emails.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5): Shinohara protested against Suzune's strategy. As Suzune had anticipated, many Class D students wanted to secure the top-ranking prizes for themselves. If they were paired with the fastest members of the class, their chances of winning those rewards would be significantly reduced.

To persuade them, Suzune argued that by ensuring the class's victory, everyone would benefit automatically. She also pointed out that the points gained from winning would compensate for not having the opportunity to claim first place. Additionally, to address the disadvantage of finishing in the lower ranks, she encouraged Shinohara to improve through studying. Her final blow came when she made Shinohara realize that, even if given the chance, her lack of athletic ability meant her chances of securing a top spot were slim.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Suzune went after Sudou and convinced him to return, making him her ally in the pursuit of reaching Class A. She achieved this by understanding him, empathizing with him, and being completely transparent about her desires and her own story, allowing him to understand her better because she realized that they were similar and reflected all his mistakes on him and motivated him to improve, making him his loyal dog.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): If Kushida lost, she wouldn’t have to leave—she would only need to stop trying to expel Suzune. However, if she won, Suzune, who had stood in her way, would be gone. With these conditions, Kushida accepted the bet, though she refused to settle for a mere verbal agreement.

Suzune had anticipated this and brought in a reliable witness to validate the bet: Ex-former Student Council President Horikita Manabu. His authority ensured that both parties would be bound to the terms, and he was a completely neutral observer.

Additionally, Manabu’s reputation would suffer if it became known that his sister was someone who broke her promises—something Kushida knew Suzune would never do.

With no way for Suzune to escape expulsion if she lost, Kushida declared that the subject of the competition would be mathematics.

Everything that happened had been predicted by Suzune. Previously, she had restricted Kushida’s options for betrayal to a single path: creating her own exam questions and passing them to Class C. With this bet, Suzune forced Kushida into a corner, making her seek Ryuuen’s help.

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal Strategy (Y1V11): She convinced the class to appoint Kiyotaka as commander during a tense moment. She argued convincingly that the class would provide him with a step-by-step plan, minimizing the perceived risk and securing their acceptance of the proposal.

Personal ranking for the best Persuasion feats:
Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8) > 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5) > Race with Manabu (Y1V5) > Level the 3 idiots Strategy (Y1V1)

Seduction -

Scapegoating -
   * Fake Camera Strategy (Y1V2): She structured the execution of the trap so that, if the school discovered the fake camera deception, the risk and responsibility would legally fall on Class B (who bought the camera and led the confrontation), protecting Class D in the worst-case scenario.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She used Sakayanagi's past actions and Yamauchi's betrayal as the perfect scapegoat to explain Ayanokoji's atypical point accumulation, diverting any investigation into the latter's true strength.

   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10): Suzune took Yamauchi and formally made him the perfect "scapegoat" for the class. She channeled all the tension, guilt, and fear from the Class Poll directly onto Yamauchi, shifting collective attention away from the general uncertainty and toward eliminating a targeted traitor.

Disguise -
   * False Horikita Manabu (Y1V1): For unexplained reasons in the series, she began to imitate her brother, Horikita Manabu, a bad habit that started manifesting in the later years of elementary school. She was completely attentive to every word Manabu said. She studied more, exercised more, avoided doing anything he wouldn’t do, imitated his favorite foods and drinks, copied his favorite colors, and even the type of clothes he would wear. Her path depended entirely on her brother’s will, which limited her potential and development as a human being. As a result, Suzune continued chasing her brother’s shadow.

That shell of trying to become like him in order to gain his attention gradually fused with her own self-perception, to the point where she forgot who she truly was. She became an ice queen—cold and distant—pushing others away and trying to do everything alone, even when it was not what she genuinely wanted.

Sophistry -
   * Hide Card Strategy (Y1V3): She used biting rhetoric in front of Yahiko at the entrance to the Class A cave, arguing that since Class D students were just "brainless defectives," there was no risk in letting them pass. She manipulated logic to disarm the guard.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): When it was time for her meeting, Suzune found Katsuragi waiting. Katsuragi spoke to her with some caution, as she now carried the reputation of having won the island exam. However, Suzune responded without intimidation. Kanzaki indirectly supported her in the verbal confrontation against Katsuragi.

Things became more tense when Ryuuen arrived, attempting to provoke those present. However, Suzune saw through his strategy and responded coldly, refusing to lower her head with her body language.

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal Strategy (Y1V11): Sudou stood up and took the floor. He wanted to know why Kiyotaka had received 42 praise points in the previous exam. His question sparked curiosity among the students, who cast doubtful glances at Kiyotaka.

Suzune turned the question back on him, forcing Sudou to think. He considered the possibility that Kiyotaka was an exceptional student, but Suzune immediately dismissed the idea, belittling him as “just someone who runs fast.” Furthermore, she reasoned that if Kiyotaka were truly extraordinary, Sakayanagi would never have granted him a protection point. This way, she not only safeguarded Kiyotaka’s peaceful life but also steered the class away from any suspicions about his true capabilities.

Personal ranking for the best Persuasion feats:
Kiyotaka's self-proposal Strategy (Y1V11) > Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5)

   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10): Convincing a class of 40 selfish people, who had poorly defined roles and made slow, chaotic, and impulsive decisions with poor communication, frequent unresolved arguments, a notable lack of discipline and consistency, and adopted individualistic attitudes that prioritized ego over collective results, who were sure to expel Kiyotaka, to expel Yamauchi with words alone.
