   * Hide VIP Strategy (Y1V4):
The Zodiac Exam divided the classes into twelve groups, each composed of students from the other three classes. The objective was to identify or protect the assigned VIP within each group. In Suzune’s case, her role was to conceal the VIP, who turned out to be Kushida.

Suzune was placed in the Dragon group, a group deliberately designed to gather the leaders and most outstanding students from each class. This composition made any attempt at manipulation, deception, or misinformation extremely difficult, as each participant possessed strong observational skills, reasoning ability, and strategic caution.

With the exam concluded, Suzune made sure that no one in her group could identify Kushida as the VIP. She successfully deceived nine individuals considered "elites" within her year, including key figures like Katsuragi and Kanzaki, both central figures in their respective classes. Ryuuen being the only one who saw through their lies, not because of skill, but because Kushida betrayed them

   * Sudou Carry Strategy (Y1V4.5):

A volleyball match in the pool took place between Classes B and D, with a free lunch at stake for the winning team. Suzune saw this as an opportunity to gain a slight advantage in the class battle, knowing that if her team won, the opposing side would lose at least 10,000 private points.

Determined to secure victory, she motivated Sudo and made him the core of the team. She positioned him at the front while placing the weakest players (Kiyotaka, Ike and Yamauchi) at the back to minimize risks. With his impressive jumping ability, Sudo could intercept the opponents’ shots and return them with power, or spike the ball if the weak link managed to set it up. Meanwhile, Suzune played a supporting role, ensuring the team’s coordination. Thanks to this strategy, they won the match and enjoyed their free lunch.

   * 'Skill-Based' system Strategy (Y1V5):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
   * The effectiveness of the 'Skill-Based' system depended entirely on tactically pairing the best athletes with the least skilled to average the results. However, the plan's effectiveness collapsed in direct competition because the entry list was leaked to Class C due to internal betrayal (Kushida).

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * Strategic allocation of athletes rather than point-heavy participant swaps
   * Use of existing strengths rather than artificial reinforcement
   * Internal Negotiation and Rapid Consensus of the Class
   * Faced with the initial dispersion of the class and individual intentions of students like Ike and Yamauchi (who wanted to compete more to gain personal points), Suzune rejected their requests to prevent them from dragging the class down.
   * To overcome the resistance of Shinohara and Karuizawa's group—who feared losing private point rewards—Suzune presented an efficient solution:
   * students who didn't need high scores on future exams would compensate for the losses of lower-performing students with their private points. This reduced discussion time and ensured victory in the vote.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   1. Phase 1: Recognition of the Regulations and Preventive Rule Analysis
   1. Following Sae's announcement about the Sports Festival, Suzune immediately takes the initiative, analyzing the rulebook to identify loopholes and mitigate point losses. During the question-and-answer session, she strategically questions the teacher about the protocol for student withdrawals (whether it's the student's decision or a school mandate) and the exact cost of replacements in private points. This action translates her critical thinking into a framework for operational planning before discussing class formation.
   2. Phase 2: Internal Diagnosis and Neutralization of Individualistic Proposals
   1. During the 20-minute internal debate, Suzune takes the lead among Sūdō, Ike, and Yamauchi. Upon hearing that Ike and Yamauchi are seeking to participate in multiple events to gain personal points, she flatly rejects their ideas. She deduces that most students will overestimate their physical capabilities to the detriment of the class, and she also blocks Sūdō's offer to compete in everything, anticipating that exhaustion would drastically reduce their performance in the final stages.
   2. In response to Hirata's proposal to vote on individual preferences, Suzune interrupts the debate and imposes her 'Skill-Based' system. Her plan ranks the class by athletic ability to maximize overall efficiency.
   1. Recommended Participation Events: Reserved primarily for the best athletes in the class.
   2. Open Participation Events: Pairings that match the fastest students with the slowest to raise the average effectiveness.
   3. To overcome the strong resistance from Shinohara and Kei's followers—who protested the loss of individual prizes by being paired with the fastest students—Suzune argues that the class victory compensates for the lost individual points and presents a proposal for financial compensation using the private points of the top-performing students. After neutralizing the objections, she puts the proposal to a vote and secures the consensus of Class D.
   3. Phase 3: Motivating Sudou and Anchor Position in the Relay
   1. Suzune takes charge of the girls' physical assessment tests to determine the participation bracket. Simultaneously, she promotes Sudou to team leader to improve the class's overall athletic coordination. To maintain his commitment and discipline, she stipulates that she will only allow Sudou to call her by her first name if he wins the Sports Festival MVP award, channeling his personal drive toward the collective good.
   2. Aiming to compete on the same stage as her brother Manabu, Suzune devises a plan for the relay race. She gathers Sūdō and Hirata privately to sell them an "unpredictable" tactic: placing Sūdō as the first runner to secure an initial lead and herself as the anchor. When Kushida interrupts the meeting, Suzune appeals to Hirata's sympathy to ensure final approval of the runner order without raising objections from the rest of the class.
   4. Phase 4: Threat Analysis
   1. During physical education class, Suzune notices students from other classes watching Class D's training. Observing that Class C isn't conducting conventional tactical analysis, she studies Ryūen's behavior patterns to try and deduce his sabotage strategy. She also assesses Kōenji's presence in the infirmary; after unsuccessfully trying to convince him privately, she decides not to waste resources on a futile effort and redirects her attention to the rest of the group.
   5. Phase 5: Sudou Recovery
   1. Despite suffering an injury caused by Kinoshita and Class C's coordinated sabotage, Suzune continues competing until she physically collapses. When Sudou loses control after being provoked by Ryūen and leaves the field, Kiyotaka confronts her harshly to break her pride and force her to see her tunnel vision. Suzune pursues Sudou and, instead of using cold logic or authority, becomes completely transparent, revealing her own failures, her desire to reach Class A, and her imitation of Manabu. By empathizing with him and showing her true self for the first time, she manages to convince him to return to the competition, rescuing the only athletic asset capable of keeping Class D alive in the final event.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
   * After Sae finished explaining, the students split up, and most began overestimating their athletic abilities to compete in multiple events and try to earn individual points. Suzune prevented these opinions from jeopardizing their chances of winning.
   * After finding Kōenji in the infirmary, Suzune tried to persuade him to support the class. Quickly assessing that persuasion was futile and having anticipated throughout the month that he would refuse to participate, she maintained the composure of her overall plan, accepting his help as a lost cause without allowing it to derail the structure of her plan for the others.
   * During the competitions, she faced a collision caused by Kinoshita that resulted in an injury, a simultaneous ambush by four riders in the women's cavalry battle, and pressure from the opposing team. Despite the physical damage, her decreased performance, and her plummeting morale, she persevered, competing to the limit of her strength without leaving the field of her own volition.
   * Even after being verbally dissected by Kiyotaka—who exposed her tunnel vision and how Ryūen manipulated her using Kinoshita and the leaked participation chart—Suzune's strategic structure wasn't destroyed. Instead of giving up after hitting rock bottom, she processed her lack of individual talent, abandoned the pretense of copying her brother, and rebuilt her resolve to seek out strong allies who could lead her to Class A.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING
   * Anticipating that pairing slower students with faster ones would lead to the rejection of the less gifted (by being excluded from top positions and individual point prizes), he defended the collective benefit by arguing that the class's victory points would compensate for individual losses. And faced with Shinohara's resistance and Karuizawa's opposition (who brought her followers along), Suzune presented a safeguard and compensation mechanism: she proposed that students who didn't need high grades use their private points to subsidize and compensate for the losses suffered by lower-performing students in the rankings. This reduced the risk of defeat and ensured majority approval in the vote.
   * To prevent Sudou's frustration from ruining the plan, she conditioned his request to call her by her first name on him becoming the MVP of the sports festival. This created an excuse to curb unwanted familiarity while simultaneously injecting massive motivation into the strongest active athlete in the class. Later, when Sudou struck Hirata and left the field, Suzune sought him out, empathized with him through the transparency of her own mistakes, and brought him back into the competition as her ultimate ally.
   * She knew that if she openly proposed being the last runner in the 1,200-meter race, the class would object, assuming she was just seeking attention. To bridge this gap, she privately called Sudou and Hirata, arguing her case to gain Hirata's final support and seal the lineup without raising suspicion.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * Upon hearing Ike and Yamauchi's initial demands (who sought to compete more to earn individual points), Suzune immediately deduced that most students would overestimate their athletic abilities. She foresaw that prioritizing individual gain over the collective would sink the class, leading her to devise the Skill-Based system.
   * Suzune knew beforehand that pairing the slowest students with the fastest would harm the individual opportunities of the weaker students, generating backlash. When Shinohara protested the lost individual rewards, Suzune already had logical responses ready: she demonstrated that the overall victory would compensate for the losses and that, given Shinohara's low ability, he wouldn't have a realistic chance of winning top spots anyway. In the end, she allowed students who didn't need high grades to use their private points to compensate the disadvantaged students, reducing the risk of backlash and ensuring the vote in favor of her plan.
   * She had previously assessed Kōenji's refusal to cooperate and deduced that he would not participate, saving her energy by assuming he was a lost cause. Likewise, she anticipated Sūdō's volatile temper, predicting precisely during the race/cavalry that he would lose control if provoked (which culminated in the blow to Hirata).

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * Seeing that her initial purely meritocratic plan clashed with the unyielding resistance of Karuizawa and his group, Suzune demonstrated tactical adaptability by introducing the compensation amendment with private points. She adapted her ideal plan to a pragmatic one that mitigated the risk of defeating the proposal in the class vote.
   * Suzune wanted to run last so she could start alongside her brother Manabu. To avoid opposition from the class, she adapted her justification to Hirata and Sūdō, presenting the idea as an "unpredictable strategy" where Sūdō would gain an initial advantage. When Kushida intervened, she again adapted her approach, showing vulnerability and emotion to gain Hirata's sympathy and secure the spot.
   * Faced with Kinoshita's deliberate attack and the relentless harassment from the four Class C cavalrywomen in the cavalry battle, Suzune displayed poor operational adaptability on the field. She stubbornly persisted in competing with her injured body until she fell to last place, causing her performance to collapse without adjusting the formation or requesting a replacement.
   * To bring Sudou back after his outburst of anger, Suzune drastically changed her approach. She abandoned the rigid facade she had adopted from her brother and, for the first time in nine years, revealed herself to be transparent, empathetic, and vulnerable. She shared her own mistakes and desires with Sudou, successfully reintegrating him into the competition and giving Class D a chance in the final race.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   * Suzune identifies that students would vote selfishly to gain individual points. She addresses this observation by discarding Hirata's free voting system and implementing the skills-based model.
   * To align the less capable students (who would lose individual points), create a compensation structure where points earned at the class level and Private Point transfers balance the losses.
   * Suzune acknowledges that Sūdō is the physical pillar of the class. She aligns her personal motivations by promising to let him call her by her first name only if he wins MVP, ensuring that his athletic performance is 100% at the service of the team.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   1. Layer 1: The 'Skill-Based' System
   1. Suzune designs a system to counteract the self-destructive tendency of the class where low-achieving students would seek to participate in recommended events to win individual prizes.
   1. In Universal Participation competitions, the rule is to pair the fastest student with the slowest. This maneuver maximizes the statistical probability of overall victories for Class D, reducing losses by a wide margin and protecting weaker students from finishing in the bottom positions.
   2. She neutralizes Shinohara by making him see that, with or without the system, his individual chances of winning anything were almost nil due to his lack of physical skill, recommending that he focus on studying to avoid penalties.
   3. In response to protests over the loss of individual incentives, Suzune introduces a clause where high-achieving students who don't require academic credit will financially compensate struggling students using private points. This transforms a dispute over physical ability into a collective benefit equation, reducing the risk of internal rebellion.
   2. Layer 2: Control of the Sudou Asset
   1. Aware that Sudou is the most valuable athlete in the class but the most emotionally unstable.
   1. She declines his initial offer to compete in all events to avoid straining her physical abilities.
   2. She publicly supports his technical leadership to improve the group's overall coordination.
   3. She makes Sudou's desire to call her by her first name contingent on him winning the Most Valuable Player (MVP) award for the entire festival, using the student's personal ambition as a driving force to propel Class D to victory.

Personal ranking for the best strategy categories in this feat:
Tactical > Alignment > Efficiency > Resilience > Shielding > Execution > Effectiveness > Adaptability > Foresight

   * Confession recording strategy (Y1V5):
Kinoshita had been calling out to Suzune to make her turn around, and at that moment, she intentionally collided with her. This made it seem as if Suzune had hit her when she turned, rather than the other way around. Since Kinoshita had the worse injury, the situation framed her as the victim.

If the incident had become public in front of the entire school, it could have resulted in a temporary suspension, a significant loss of class points, and, furthermore, a direct humiliation for her brother. To prevent that outcome, Suzune was forced to pay Ryuuen one million private points, also accepting personal humiliation as part of the price to contain the consequences.

After the sports festival and confirming Kushida’s actions and motives, Suzune turned her attention to Ryuuen. She spoke as if everything had happened by mere coincidence and manipulated him into confessing. She had been recording their conversation. Suzune then used that recording as blackmail, intending to avoid kneeling and close the case.

But Ryuuen had already foreseen this. He played his own recording, leaving her with no options.

   * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
   * Suzune managed to corner Kushida by forcing her to accept a formal bet backed by Manabu. By limiting Kushida's options for betrayal to a single, predictable path and binding her to a non-aggression pact if she lost math, Suzune laid the groundwork to curb her sabotage and move toward her ultimate goal of "taming" her into a weapon/ally for the class instead of expelling her.
   * The strategy not only protected the class from expulsion, but dealt such a destructive blow to Class C that, without Kiyotaka's external intervention, it would have resulted in the expulsion of several Class C students, raising Class D's points and exacting direct revenge on Ryūen.
   * The effectiveness was so high that Suzune accurately anticipated Ryūen's method and countered the coordinated attack. Through a prior agreement with Professor Sae, any false or altered questions submitted by Kushida were secretly invalidated.
   * She completely deceived Ryūen in the teachers' lounge by staging a scene where she feigned despair and collapsed to her knees. This led her rivals to believe the betrayal plan had worked, ensuring Ryūen wouldn't attempt a last-minute backup plan.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * To prevent Kushida from having free time to plot unsupervised, Suzune overwhelmed her by assigning her as a tutor for two simultaneous study groups and constantly surrounding her with classmates. This restricted her movements by using only the normal classroom study structure.
   * Without spending points or requiring complex logistics, Suzune isolated Kushida from the creation of the official exam questions. This forcibly channeled Kushida down a single, predictable path of betrayal.
   * Suzune used her brother, Manabu, as a neutral and unwavering witness to the gamble. This gave the pact absolute institutional validity without having to risk the class's resources or resort to costly favors.
   * She dialed Kiyotaka's number from memory using her pocket so he could hear the entire confrontation with Kushida. This allowed her to obtain a real-time emergency witness and backup without wasting time on subsequent explanations or attracting Kushida's attention.
   * A single conversation with Sae on the very first day was enough to shut down the exam submission channel. Suzune accurately deduced that Sae would cooperate without demanding anything in return, due to her implicit desire for Class D to advance.
   * A single conversation with Sae on day one was enough to shut down the exam submission channel. This allowed Suzune to confirm Sae's true objective.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   1. Phase 1: Administrative Block
   1. Suzune travels with Sae to establish an unbreakable agreement: she stipulates that Sae will only accept as valid the questions personally delivered by Suzune. Should anyone else (such as Kushida) attempt to submit an exam syllabus, Sae will pretend to accept it to maintain the illusion of betrayal, but will never formally submit it to the school.
   2. Phase 2: Integration and Restriction of Options
   1. Suzune initiates the plan by actively including Kushida in the class leadership meeting and assigning her a dual role in the study groups. By keeping Kushida busy supervising her classmates and explicitly forbidding her from drafting the exam questions (a task reserved only for herself, Hirata, and Yukimura), Suzune reduces Kushida's avenues of betrayal to a single logical option permitted by the exam rules: creating her own separate draft of questions and sending it to Professor Sae to be passed on to Class C.
   3. Phase 3: The Bet
   1. Suzune summons Kushida alone to confront her hostility, but executes a cover maneuver: she dials Kiyotaka's number from memory in her pocket without looking so he can hear the entire conversation live and stay informed. During the face-to-face meeting, she establishes a logical stalemate regarding both of their continued enrollment at the school and proposes a direct wager based on their final exam scores: if Suzune gets a higher score, Kushida will cease her betrayals; if she loses, Suzune will expel herself. To ensure Kushida's acceptance, she grants her the tactical advantage of choosing the subject.
   2. Anticipating that Kushida would demand guarantees against being deceived by a verbal agreement, Suzune immediately introduces former Student Council President Manabu Horikita as an official witness and intermediary. His neutral status and impeccable institutional reputation obligate both parties to honor the pact without possibility of backing out, Kushida knowing that Suzune would never tarnish her brother's reputation by breaking a promise he endorsed.
   4. Phase 4: Ryūen's Targeted Involvement
   1. Suzune manipulates the situation to force Kushida to rely on Ryūen and the answers from Class C to try and surpass her in math. Knowing that Class C has a lower average grade (thanks to months of tutoring from Class D), Suzune puts Ryūen in a difficult position: if he helps Kushida by swapping exams, he'll risk his own students being expelled en masse in pairs due to the disparity in syllabi, using Class C's own cheating methods against them.
   5. Phase 5: Show in the Teachers' Lounge
   1. On the final day of the deadline, Suzune goes to the teachers' lounge to submit the final questions, having predicted when Ryūen would send them. There, Sae hints at an irregularity in the submitted documents, and Suzune acts as if she's piecing together a sudden act of sabotage, falling to her knees in utter despair. This performance completely fools Ryūen, and consequently Kushida, leading them to believe the betrayal worked and causing Ryūen to retreat confidently, leaving Class C completely caught in the crossfire of Class D's counterattack.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
   * Kushida felt a one-sided hatred towards Suzune and was willing to ally herself with Class C or harm Class D in order to expel Suzune and protect her past.
   * Instead of being intimidated or collapsing under the threat of expulsion, Suzune transformed that hostility into a strategic engine. She designed an environment where Kushida's hatred was channeled within a controlled game framework, withstanding the pressure of being the target of constant internal betrayal without deviating from her master plan.
   * The inherent probability that Kushida would attempt to sabotage the creation of the exam questions or leak them to Ryūen.
   * Suzune preemptively countered Kushida's ability to maneuver by overloading her schedule and surrounding her with classmates to restrict her freedom of movement. Forbidding her from working on exam questions with the other tutors limited the potential for betrayal to a single, predictable path.
   * Potential special exam manipulation (Kiyotaka)

Even when Kushida escalated by involving Ryuuen, this reaction had already been anticipated and folded into the plan. The strategy did not merely endure resistance, it weaponized it.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING:
   * Kushida managed to write an alternative question sheet and hand it to the school behind the class's back to replace the official Class D exam.
   * Suzune didn't ignore this possibility; she proactively contacted Professor Sae from day one. Based on her analysis of Sae's behavior after Sūdō's trial in Volume 2, Suzune made Sae promise that she would only accept questions submitted by herself, and that if anyone else (like Kushida) tried to submit an exam, Sae would pretend to accept it but would never formally submit it. This completely closed the door on sabotage.
   * That Kushida would retract the terms of the bet after losing or that she would claim that the verbal agreement was invalid.
   * Suzune anticipated the demand for a formal agreement and brought in Manabu Horikita (former Student Council President) as a neutral and impartial witness. This doubly protected the pact:
   * It guaranteed the authority of the commitment a
   * Used Manabu's reputation to ensure Kushida knew that Suzune could not break her promise without harming her brother, forcing Kushida to be legally and morally bound by the outcome.
   * Whether the confrontation alone with Kushida turned into a conflict of "her word against mine" or whether there was a lack of record of the attempted blackmail.
   * When Kushida demanded that Kiyotaka leave so they could talk privately, Suzune dialed Kiyotaka's number from memory with her hand in her pocket just before they began speaking. This ensured a real-time audio witness without violating Kushida's conditions.
   * That Ryūen or Kushida suspected that Class D knew about their trap and restructured their plan at the last minute.
   * Suzune waited until the last possible moment before the deadline to hand in her questions in the teachers' lounge in Ryūen's presence. To counter any action by her rival and ensure Ryūen didn't change his strategy, Suzune and Sae staged an impromptu performance. Sae gave him a false, indirect warning, and Suzune fell to her knees, feigning utter despair at Kushida's betrayal. This simulation deceived Ryūen, making him believe their plan had worked and causing him to fall right into the trap without attempting any last-minute corrections.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * She knew Kushida would never accept a passive truce and would again use the Paper Shuffle exam structure to sabotage Class D from within. Instead of reacting after the sabotage, Suzune decided to lay the groundwork to force Kushida to betray them in a completely predictable and controlled manner.
   * By cutting Kushida off from access to the official questions and with time running out, Suzune figures out that the only possible way left for Kushida to help Ryūen is to create her own parallel quiz game and hand it out to Class C.
   * Suzune grants Kushida the right to choose the subject for the eight exams, knowing this will give Kushida enough false confidence to fall into the trap. Just as she predicted, Kushida chooses Mathematics.
   * Suzune offers her own expulsion if she loses, while she only demands Kushida's cooperation without hostility if she loses. She anticipates that this disparity will immediately tempt Kushida.
   * She predicts that Ryūen will blindly trust the exam leaked by Kushida and will not academically prepare his class for the real Class D exam. If Ryūen trusts Kushida's answers (which will be discarded by Sae), Class C will take a completely different exam, leading to massive failures and risking multiple expulsions for their partners.
   * She anticipates Sae Chabashira's psychology, deducing from past events that the teacher secretly desires Class D to advance to Class A. With this in mind, she agrees with Sae days in advance that she will only accept the exam delivered directly by Suzune, acting as if she would accept any other external attempt but dismissing it.
   * She has Manabu ready as a witness. Suzune knows that Kushida recognizes Manabu's absolute authority and neutrality, and that she also knows Suzune would never break a promise in front of her brother. This guarantees the legal and psychological validity of the agreement without leaving any legal loopholes.
   * Suzune anticipates that Ryūen will wait until the last second to hand in his questions in order to try to gain an advantage or exert pressure.

Multiple enemy decision trees were mapped in advance, and all converged into the same losing outcome.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * Although Kushida decided to attend the class leadership meeting on her own, Suzune had left room in her plan to integrate her from the very beginning. This allowed her to absorb Kushida's spontaneous action without disrupting the plan and to immediately use her presence to assign her supervisory roles.
   * Faced with the inevitable stalemate caused by Kushida's refusal to cease hostilities unless Suzune was expelled, Suzune adapted her approach. Rather than allowing unseen and uncontrollable sabotage, she created a strategic opening by offering Kushida a high-stakes, direct gamble. By granting her the advantage of choosing the exam subject matter, Suzune absorbed an unforeseen disadvantage for her rival in exchange for channeling the conflict within a controlled, rule-bound framework.
   * Suzune didn't try to stop the alliance between Kushida and Ryūen; instead, she adapted her strategy, anticipating that it would happen. Anticipating that Class C would use cheating methods instead of studying, she adjusted the timing of her questions to the last possible minute to react in real time to Ryūen's presence in the teachers' lounge.
   * Faced with the foreseeable risk that Kushida would break the verbal agreement or claim its invalidity when cornered after losing, Suzune adapted the structure of the bet by incorporating an inflexible and neutral element: the presence of Manabu Horikita. By involving her brother, she created a mechanism of social and reputational adjustment where the mere authority of the former student council president and the weight of his image guaranteed that neither party could back down or alter the outcome in unfavorable situations.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   * Suzune recognized that Kushida's betrayal threatened the class's very existence. She aligned this internal problem with the special exam by targeting Ryūen's Class C as the sole objective. The reasoning was straightforward: she knew Ryūen would use Kushida to betray them again, so she channeled the internal threat into a controlled external arena where the traitor's actions could be predicted and exploited to Class D's advantage.
   * All logistical components aligned sequentially to trap Kushida in a single course of action:
   * She was assigned continuous work supervising two study groups to limit her free time and keep her surrounded by students.
   * She was forbidden from writing the exam questions.
   * Aligned result: Without access to the official questions and with her time restricted, the only logical way left for Kushida to help Ryūen within the rules was to secretly write her own questions and pass the answers to Class C.
   * Suzune linearly connected the individual duel against Kushida with the structural trap set for Class C. By cornering Kushida through the bet with Manabu as a witness, she forced Kushida to desperately seek Ryūen's help to win the math test. In turn, she aligned Ryūen's deceptive style with Class C's academic standing, causing Ryūen to inevitably fall into the trap of feigned exclamations.
   * The final links in the strategy aligned flawlessly at the time of the official exam delivery:
   * Suzune waited until the last possible moment to hand in her questions in the teachers' lounge, knowing Ryūen would do the same to obstruct her.
   * She handed in the questions in front of Sae, triggering a coordinated performance where Sae pretended to accept Suzune's questions as the only valid ones.
   * This simultaneously secured four aligned goals in a single blow:
   * Neutralizing Kushida's betrayal
   * Inflicting massive damage on Class C
   * Getting revenge on Ryūen
   * Securing a direct class point increase for Class D.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   1. Layer 1: Tactical Insulation
   1. Mandatory inclusion and excessive workload.
   1. Suzune invited Kushida to the leaders' meeting and insisted on targeting Class C. To keep Kushida under control and limit her room to maneuver, she assigned her to supervise both study groups. By surrounding her with students at all times and explicitly forbidding her from writing down or touching the exam questions, Suzune closed off almost all of Kushida’s traditional avenues for sabotage. This channeled Kushida toward the only possible avenue for betrayal within the rules: devising her own exam questions and passing the answers to Class C.
   2. Layer 2: Institutional Blockade by Sae
   1. preventive countermeasure.
   1. Habiendo previsto que Kushida intentaría entregar preguntas falsas o modificadas a la escuela para beneficiar a la Clase C, Suzune tomó medidas desde el primer día. Envió un mensaje a la profesora Sae Chabashira haciéndole prometer que únicamente aceptaría las preguntas entregadas personalmente por Suzune. Si cualquier otra persona (como Kushida) intentaba entregar un temario, Sae debía fingir que lo aceptaba pero jamás procesarlo oficialmente. Suzune pudo prever esta colaboración de Sae al deducir (tras el juicio de Sūdō en el Vol. 2) que su profesora deseaba el ascenso de la clase más de lo que aparentaba.
   3. Layer 3: Psychological Trap and Conditioned Response
   1. Handicap Red Card Bet
   1. Suzune cornered Kushida by offering him a one-on-one showdown separate from the special exam. To force Kushida to accept and fall into the trap, Suzune gave him asymmetrical advantages: if Kushida lost, he only had to stop sabotaging the class and cooperate; if Suzune lost, she would expel herself from school. Furthermore, Suzune allowed Kushida to choose the subject. This pushed Kushida to desperately seek Ryūen’s support to ensure his victory, causing Kushida to fall exactly into the trap Suzune had set.
   2. Covert surveillance and an authoritative neutral witness.
   1. Before confronting Kushida, she called Kiyotaka without looking at the keypad, leaving the phone in her pocket so that Kiyotaka could hear the entire confrontation live without Kushida's consent.
   2. To prevent Kushida from backing out or breaking their verbal agreement, he brought Manabu in as a witness. The former Student Council president’s spotless reputation ensured that neither of them could go back on their word, and she also took advantage of the fact that Kushida knew Suzune would never tarnish her brother’s reputation by breaking a promise in front of him.
   4. Layer 4: Checkmate for Class C
   1. Mass deception, reverse psychology, and feigned desperation.
   1. By forcing Kushida to turn to Ryūen, Suzune put Class C at a crossroads. If Ryūen helped Kushida with his sabotage, it would only ensure Class C’s failure in the pairs test due to the class’s low average score.
   2. As the final masterstroke of the trap, Suzune waited until the very last moment to hand in her questions in the teachers’ lounge in Ryūen’s presence. There, together with Sae, they put on an impromptu act. This led Ryūen to believe that Class C’s plan had worked, causing him to leave satisfied without realizing he was completely caught in the trap.
