   * MTC group girls Strategy (Y1V8):
Thanks to Suzune's strategy, all the girls in Class D found a group where they fit in. For example, Mii-chan, who was socially inept and physically weak, was able to team up with Ichinose, an extroverted girl who could help her. Her strategy also thwarted Arisu's plans to win by avoiding expulsions and collateral damage. She not only led her group to victory but also protected her entire class.

   * Expose Yamauchi Strategy (Y1V10):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
   * By publicly exposing Yamauchi’s conspiracy in front of everyone, Suzune destroyed the voting bloc that threatened Ayanokōji in one fell swoop. The revelation caused the group led by Kushida and Yamauchi to crumble, saving Kiyotaka without him having to intervene directly or reveal his abilities.
   * Based on cold, objective data, he determined that Yamauchi was statistically the worst member of the class. When he added to this the crime of betrayal (colluding with Class A against his own classmates), he turned a painful choice into a logically unavoidable decision for the rest of the group.
   * Suzune cornered Kushida, forcing her to implicate Yamauchi in order to maintain her own “facade” as a kind girl in front of the class. She indirectly reminded her of the agreement they had made earlier and forced her to back down, ensuring that Kushida would remain compliant and not sabotage the end-of-year final exam so as not to lose the class’s trust.
   * Suzune used the data comparison to show Sudō just how close he had come to being in Yamauchi’s place because of his past mistakes. This direct confrontation made Sudō realize his mistakes and reaffirmed Suzune’s leadership over him, fostering their long-term integration.
   * By positioning herself as the “judge” and taking on the public responsibility of appointing Yamauchi, Suzune absorbed the class’s resentment. In the end, she offered the class the freedom to vote for her if they disagreed, which reduced the moral pressure on the group and ensured that the vote would be overwhelmingly directed against the traitor.
   * She positions herself as the leader of the ultimate class

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * Suzune didn't act rashly during the first few days of the exam. She deliberately waited until the day before the vote. This calculated wait made two crucial things possible:
   * Gather sufficient evidence and confirm Class A's intentions.
   * Allow the desperation and hidden obstacles to fully take shape, so that his final speech would have the greatest possible psychological impact without giving Yamauchi or Sakayanagi time to mount a counterattack.
   * Suzune drew on a resource she already had and kept up to date: her mental and statistical record of the 39 students’ behavior, notes, and skills. She didn’t have to spend any extra energy or time doing last-minute research; she simply used the database she had already actively built.
   * Instead of wasting energy arguing with Kushida, Suzune used Kushida’s own reputation as leverage. Forcing Kushida to corner Yamauchi in order to protect his public image required minimal effort on Suzune’s part and yielded a twofold benefit.
   * Suzune only needed a brief mental consultation/conversation with Ryūen and her brother Manabu to muster the determination and courage needed to take the final step. She didn't use up any costly favors or class points to set the trap.
   * The most ineffective aspect of the strategy was the inevitable rift with Hirata. Hirata was categorically opposed to singling out anyone for expulsion. Suzune used cold logic to crush Hirata’s arguments, causing him to break down emotionally and vote against her. Although it was a necessary evil to break the deadlock, the temporary loss of Hirata’s mental stability and support came at a high social cost within the class.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   1. Phase 1: Interpretation of the Institutional Context and Initial Analysis of the Exam
   1. Suzune processes the implicit warnings from Professor Sae Chabashira regarding the unusual nature of the exam (“Class Poll”). She immediately understands the mechanics of the praise and censure votes. Tactically, she evaluates the possibility of exploiting a loophole (coordinating votes to cancel them out to zero). Upon being informed by Sae that the school will not allow this loophole and will employ special measures or intervention from other classes, Suzune abandons the idea of collective neutralization and shifts her focus to the need for a direct selection strategy.
   2. Faced with the immediate outbreak of chaos among the students (clashes between Koenji, Sudou, Hirata, Ike, and Yamauchi), Suzune executes a tactical containment move. With a single direct command and through her presence alone, she silences Sudou and puts a stop to the group’s escalating verbal confrontation. This establishes her authority in the classroom, ensuring that the class does not fall apart before she can implement a coordinated plan.
   2. Phase 2: Objective Assessment of Students
   1. To avoid making emotional or arbitrary decisions, Suzune carries out a data collection and analysis phase:
   1. She records and memorizes classroom behavior, tardiness and absence records, and the academic and physical performance of the 39 students in the class.
   2. She applies strictly objective criteria to construct a scale of dispensability, concluding that Haruki Yamauchi is the worst student in the class, followed by Ike and Sudou.
   3. She decides to protect valuable future assets  and rules out the option of putting outstanding students at risk.
   3. Phase 3: Mental Preparation and External Validation
   1. Recognizing the ethical weight of nominating a classmate for expulsion, Suzune seeks to develop the necessary psychological preparation and resolve through strategic interactions:
   1. She seeks the final push and confirms that direct nomination is the only viable method.
   2. She meets with her brother, Manabu, to gather the determination and courage needed before carrying out the
   3. plan in front of the class.
   4. Phase 4: Taking Control of the Classroom
   1. When classes end the day before the election, Suzune kicks off the in-person phase of her strategy:
   1. She requires all students to remain in their seats to discuss the exam.
   2. She assesses Yamauchi and Ike’s feigned calm and thwarts their attempt to escape, leaving them with no way out.
   3. She neutralizes Hirata’s opposition—who refused to accept any expulsion—by enforcing the efficient use of the limited time and relying on Sudou and Koenji’s interventions to silence the interruptions.
   2. Suzune breaks the class taboo by becoming the first student to publicly nominate someone for expulsion: Haruki Yamauchi. When Yamauchi tries to defend himself by comparing himself to his friends:
   1. Against Sudou: Suzune demonstrates that Sudou has improved substantially and that his physical abilities are far superior.
   2. Against Ike: She demonstrates with objective data that Yamauchi is still one step behind.
   3. Against Koenji: She justifies Koenji’s continued participation based on the enormous gap in their physical and academic abilities in Koenji’s favor.
   4. Sudou’s pedagogical integration: She uses Sudou’s background in the debate to make him aware of his past mistakes and push him to improve.
   5. Phase 5: Exposure of the Internal Conspiracy
   1. Suzune reveals that Yamauchi used Kushida as an intermediary to secretly gather votes of no confidence and expel Kiyotaka.
   1. She verbally corners Yamauchi until he is forced to blame Ike, and then forces Ike to point out Kushida’s involvement.
   2. She uses Kushida’s “trusted student” facade against him. By exposing her in front of the class, she forces Kushida to confirm Yamauchi’s guilt in order to avoid damaging her own public image.
   3. She publicly reprimands Kushida for lending her influence, indirectly reminding her of their previous pact and forcing her to remain compliant leading up to the end-of-year exam in an attempt to regain the group’s trust.
   2. To ensure that the class doesn't see Yamauchi as a victim, Suzune explains the external plot:
   1. She reveals that Yamauchi lacked the intellectual capacity to organize the group on his own and that he acted under Sakayanagi’s direct orders (Class A).
   2. She exposes that Yamauchi’s motivation for betraying the class was personal/lewd in nature.
   3. She debunks Yamauchi’s argument regarding his phone and text message history, shattering any pretense of innocence.
   6. Phase 6: Dismantling of Countermeasures
   1. Suzune addresses the question of why the plot was directed specifically at Kiyotaka:
   1. She minimizes the impact on Kiyotaka’s perception by explaining that he was chosen precisely because he didn’t stand out, making him an easy target who offered no resistance.
   2. She frames the situation for the class as a logical choice among three roles: an innocent person (Kiyotaka), a judge (Suzune), and a traitor (Yamauchi).
   3. By shouldering the full weight of the judgment and presenting the facts, she relieves the rest of the class of the moral burden and leaves expulsion as the only rational choice.
   2. Against Sudou:
   1. Thwart Sudou's attempts to defend his friend by logically demonstrating to him that trying to secretly manipulate the votes was the root of the problem.
   3. Removal of the block:
   1. Finding herself cornered, Kushida formally withdraws the no-confidence motion, sealing Yamauchi's isolation.
   4. Hirata's Containment:
   1. When Hirata reacts violently at the table and indicates that he will vote against Suzune, she calmly accepts Hirata's stance without losing her composure, thus bringing the strategy to a successful conclusion.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
   * Sudou tried to fiercely defend his friend Yamauchi. Suzune dismantled his argument in a quick verbal exchange by using Sudou’s own logic: she showed him that defending Yamauchi meant condemning an innocent classmate, forcing Sudou to back down and come to terms with his own past mistakes.
   * Hirata strongly opposed naming a target for expulsion and tried to interrupt Suzune to prevent her from destroying the group’s unity. Suzune did not yield to the pressure or to Hirata’s outburst of frustration. She responded with cold logic, dismantled his arguments one by one, and coolly accepted that Hirata would vote against her as long as it kept the plan on track.
   * When Yamauchi tried to shift the blame onto Sudou or Ike by mentioning their low grades, or onto Kōenji by pointing out that he didn’t contribute anything to the class, Suzune countered every attempt to deflect blame using meticulously compiled data on everyone’s physical and academic abilities. Each of Yamauchi’s arguments was refuted and used against him with relentless logic.
   * More than half the class was already involved in the secret group Kushida had formed to expel Kiyotaka. Suzune’s strategy withstood this massive backlash by exposing the conspiracy publicly, thereby crushing its momentum just one day before the vote.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING
   * Publicly calling out a student carried the risk of earning the class’s resentment or becoming a scapegoat. Suzune addressed this potential flaw by offering herself as a scapegoat: she declared that if anyone was not convinced by the evidence, they could direct their vote of no confidence at her instead. This took the pressure off the class and ensured a logical decision.
   * To prevent her attack from seeming personal or arbitrary, Suzune compiled a mental record—based on her observations during class—of the 39 students’ absences, tardiness, strengths, and weaknesses. With this, she objectively demonstrated that Yamauchi was the worst student in the class, even worse than Ike and Sudou.
   * Anticipating that Kushida would either defend the trap or lie, Suzune cornered Ike into blaming Kushida, and then forced Kushida to publicly implicate Yamauchi. Since Kushida couldn’t break her “good girl” facade, she was forced to withdraw the mass-voting group and deliver the final blow to Yamauchi to save her own reputation.
   * When Yamauchi claimed that he was only “friends” with Sakayanagi and that their phone calls proved nothing, Suzune deduced that Yamauchi lacked the intellectual capacity to devise such a plan on his own and exposed his true motive. This dispelled any image of victimhood that Yamauchi had been trying to project.
   * Suzune deliberately waited until the day before the vote to carry out her plan. This prevented Yamauchi, Sakayanagi, and the group of conspirators from having time to regroup, devise a counterstrategy, or convince the class to change their votes.
   * By controlling the narrative, she prevented uncontrolled voting outcomes.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * Suzune could see exactly how Yamauchi would try to defend himself once he was nominated. She knew in advance that he would try to divert attention by accusing other classmates who were underperforming (Sudō or Ike) or those who didn't contribute to the class (Kōenji).
   * She knew that Kushida was working behind the scenes to gather negative votes against Kiyotaka.
   * She realized that by forcing Ike to tell the truth and exposing Kushida in front of the class, she would be unable to shed her “good girl” facade in order to defend Yamauchi. Forcing Kushida to act submissively allowed her not only to withdraw the group’s votes from Yamauchi but also to secure his reluctant cooperation for the end-of-year exam.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * Originally, the utopian vision was to seek a coordinated solution or for the class to decide together. Noticing the instability within the class and the chaos caused by Hirata, Ike, and Yamauchi, Suzune adjusted her approach: she realized that consensus was unattainable and that she had to personally bear the political cost by directly nominating the target.
   * Before carrying out the plan, she fine-tuned her mental preparation by consulting her brother Manabu and seeking Ryūen’s perspective to confirm that her method of identifying a traitor was the only objective approach available.
   * Hirata tried to interrupt and derail the discussion to prevent any of his classmates from being expelled. Suzune adapted her speech by letting him speak in a controlled manner and drawing on the atmosphere in the room to silence Hirata’s obstructionism without losing her authority in the classroom.
   * When Hirata had an emotional breakdown, slammed his fist on the table, and impulsively declared that he would vote against Suzune to protect the class, Suzune remained calm. Instead of panicking or arguing with Hirata, she calmly accepted his negative vote as a calculable margin of error within her strategy.
   * When Sudō stepped in to defend his friend Yamauchi, Suzune used Sudō’s own logic against him. She reframed the argument to remind him of the mistakes he himself had made in the past, turning an unfavorable interruption into a lesson in personal growth designed to help Sudō reintegrate and become more self-aware in the long run.
   * To avoid being perceived as a dictator imposing an expulsion, Suzune deliberately offered the class the option of using their vote of no confidence against her. By presenting herself as a “judge” option as opposed to the “traitor” (Yamauchi) or an “innocent,” she provided the class with an outlet for their frustration, ensuring that if the class chose logically, the only inevitable target would be Yamauchi.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   * Suzune builds a continuous, uninterrupted logical sequence in which each step naturally sets the stage for the next, leaving no room for chaos in the classroom:
   * Records the habits, notes, and behaviors of the 39 students.
   * Objectively determines that Yamauchi is the worst asset in the class.
   * Consults with Ryūen to confirm the inevitability of the strategy.
   * Meets with Manabu to reach a final mental resolution.
   * Allows the secret groups and Yamauchi’s conspiracy to develop.
   * Intervenes at the moment of greatest impact (the day before the vote).
   * Blocks the exits to prevent Yamauchi and Ike from escaping.
   * Dismantles Hirata’s moralistic resistance by imposing order and a strict timeline.
   * Cites Yamauchi’s poor academic and physical performance to Sudou and Ike.
   * Establishes an insurmountable rift with Koenji.
   * Exposes the secret collection of votes against Kiyotaka.
   * Uses Kushida’s “mask” to force her to incriminate Yamauchi.
   * Indirectly reminds her of their pact, forcing her to withdraw the group.
   * Reveals that the pact with Class A was motivated by lustful intentions.
   * She dismantles Yamauchi’s “victim” image through cold logic.
   * She explains Kiyotaka’s selection as the search for an “easy pawn,” concealing his true worth.
   * She takes a personal risk by inviting the class to vote against her if they aren’t convinced.
The prior recording of data (1) provides the logical foundation to support the public appointment (5). In turn, Yamauchi’s appointment paves the way for revealing the trap set for Kiyotaka (6), which inevitably leads to implicating Kushida (7) and exposing the pact with Sakayanagi (8).
   * The linear strategy not only results in the expulsion of the traitor; in a perfectly coordinated manner, it also achieves:
   * Protect Kiyotaka Ayanokōji.
   * Force Kushida to be compliant in the short term to protect his facade.
   * Make Sudou reflect on his past mistakes to better integrate him in the future.
   * Resolve the dilemma of the special exam by eliminating the weakest link.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   1. Layer 1: Kushida's Mask Trap
   1. Suzune didn't attack Kushida directly to drive her out; instead, she used Kushida's impeccable reputation to corner Yamauchi.
   1. By forcing Ike to confess to Kushida’s involvement, Suzune put Kushida in a position where denying the facts would destroy her image as a “good, trustworthy girl” in front of the class. To maintain her facade, Kushida was forced to cooperate, withdraw the group’s votes, and deliver the final blow to Yamauchi. Furthermore, this served as an implicit reminder of their prior agreement and forced her to play along for the sake of the end-of-year exam.
   2. Layer 2: The Manipulation of Information
   1. Suzune realized early on that a large group of negative votes had formed, but she decided to remain silent and wait until the day before the vote.
   1. Waiting until the last moment prevented Yamauchi and Sakayanagi's group from formulating a counterstrategy or rallying the class's votes. The shock of the sudden revelation of the betrayal eliminated any room for maneuver on the part of the opposition.
   3. Layer 3: Debunking Arguments
   1. By the time Yamauchi tried to divert attention to other students (Sudou, Ike, Koenji), Suzune had already memorized and recorded the 39 students’ classroom behavior, attendance, tardiness, and academic and physical abilities. Every time Yamauchi pointed at someone, Suzune applied cold logic:
   1. Vs. Sudou: She demonstrated that Sudou had improved academically and was far superior to him physically.
   2. Vs. Ike: She demonstrated that Ike was a cut above Yamauchi.
   3. Vs. Koenji: She justified Koenji's continued participation based on his overwhelming individual ability.
   2. Managing two opposing emotional dynamics in the classroom.
   1. She kept Hirata in check by asking him to speak only after she had finished, using the exam’s time limit and Koenji and Sudou’s support to silence his interruptions.
   2. She disarmed Sudou by objectively reminding him of his past mistakes to make him aware of his track record and channel that lesson toward his long-term integration into the class.
   4. Layer 4:  Concealment of the Real Motive
   1. Suzune's primary defensive objective was to protect Kiyotaka (the class's most valuable asset) without revealing his true worth or skill to the rest of the class or to Class A.
   1. She explained Yamauchi’s choice of Kiyotaka not as an attack on anyone in particular, but by arguing that Kiyotaka was chosen simply because he didn’t stand out and was an easy target. In this way, she dispelled suspicion surrounding Kiyotaka while portraying Yamauchi simply as a pawn manipulated by Sakayanagi through romantic and lustful enticements.
   2. To prevent the class from feeling morally guilty about expelling a classmate or from resenting her for acting as the executioner.
   1. Suzune concluded by stating that the class was free to vote for themselves if they weren't convinced, leaving them with the choice between “an innocent (Kiyotaka), a judge (Suzune), and a traitor (Yamauchi).” This relieved the psychological pressure on the other students and ensured a massive vote against Yamauchi without anyone having to get their hands dirty.

Personal ranking for the best strategy categories in this feat:
Execution > Foresight > Effectiveness > Tactical > Alignment > Efficiency > Shielding > Adaptability > Resilience

   * Kiyotaka's self-proposal Strategy (Y1V11):
1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
   * Secured Kiyotaka as commander, the only individual capable of both winning and surviving defeat
   * Prevented the appointment of an incompetent or emotionally driven commander
   * Protected the class from the catastrophic risk of losing a top asset
   * Neutralized suspicion surrounding Kiyotaka’s abnormal praise votes, Protecting him

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * She did not require the use of private points or transfers to convince the class or Kiyotaka.
   * To gain the class's acceptance of Kiyotaka without revealing his true intellect, Suzune sacrificed his reputation by publicly labeling him "someone who just runs fast." This reputational "sacrifice" proved highly effective, as Kiyotaka himself desired to maintain a low profile. Suzune granted him precisely what he wanted (a quiet life) while simultaneously forcing him into a leadership role.
   * By choosing Kiyotaka, Suzune eliminated the highest possible cost of the exam (the loss of a classmate). Because Kiyotaka possessed the Protection Point, even in the worst-case scenario of defeat against Class A, no one in Class D would be expelled.
   * Suzune didn't need weeks of planning or lengthy secret meetings for this plan. She opportunistically seized the exact moment when Sudou confronted Kiyotaka in the classroom.
   * Instead of arguing with skeptical members like Ike or Shinohara, Suzune shifted the burden onto the critics. This instantly silenced doubts without wasting energy on individual debates, achieving consensus through immediate elimination.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   1. Phase 1: Identification of Conditions
   1. Suzune immediately analyzed the regulations for the special exam and understood that the commander's role was the deciding factor for victory or defeat. Given the scarcity of talent in Class D and the potentially fatal risk of expulsion for the loser, she concluded that the chosen person had to meet at least one of two indispensable conditions:
   1. Guarantee absolute victory
   2. Be someone whose hypothetical loss would not ruin the group.
   1. Identifying that Kiyotaka was the only person who satisfied both conditions (thanks to his protection point and inherent ability), she set her sights on forcing him to assume the position, despite Kiyotaka's well-known reluctance to openly act on behalf of the class under normal circumstances.
   2. Phase 2: Diversion of the Pressure
   1. When Sudou confronted Kiyotaka, demanding to know why he had received 42 commendations on the previous exam, doubt began to spread throughout the classroom.
   1. Before the pressure on Kiyotaka could spiral out of control or before he had to respond, Suzune decisively intervened to take control of the conversation.
   2. When Ike demanded to hear directly from Kiyotaka, Suzune flatly refused, arguing that Kiyotaka himself didn't know the reason—a calculated lie to defuse the initial attack and shield Kiyotaka from direct scrutiny.
   3. To dispel any suspicion about Kiyotaka's true abilities, Suzune crafted a logical explanation connecting the events to Sakayanagi's actions.
   4. She revealed that the votes of praise promised to Yamauchi had been secretly transferred to Kiyotaka at Sakayanagi's behest to orchestrate Yamauchi's expulsion for personal reasons.
   5. Suzune intentionally downplayed Kiyotaka's value to his classmates, reducing him to "someone who just runs fast" and arguing that if he were truly a threat or extraordinary, Sakayanagi would never have given him a single point of protection.
   3. Phase 3: Social Cornering
   1. Given Sudou's persistent doubt about whether Suzune was merely protecting her friend.
   1. she explained that she was defending him because she herself had been directly responsible for Yamauchi's expulsion by exposing the truth.
   2. By temporarily clearing Kiyotaka's name but leaving him in a position where his protective stance made him stand out, Suzune transformed the situation into a perfect opportunity:
   1.  Kiyotaka could volunteer to become commander to clear his name, thus preventing any other classmates from being at risk of expulsion.
   3. When Shinohara objected, arguing that appointing Kiyotaka was tantamount to handing the exam to the students, Suzune used the class's own logic to her advantage. She openly asked if any other students were willing to take the real risk of being expelled if they lost.
   4. Faced with the class's silence and widespread fear, Suzune sealed the trap:
   1. Since Kiyotaka had the protection point and had volunteered (forced by circumstances), there was no other logical alternative.
   2. In this way, she successfully executed her strategy of cornering Kiyotaka into leading the class as commander in the special exam.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
   * When Sudou confronts Kiyotaka about the origin of his 42 points of praise and then questions whether Suzune is defending him simply because they are friends, Suzune's strategy remains unwavering. She withstands the pressure, making it clear that she is not defending him out of friendship, but rather out of a sense of moral and logical responsibility for having exposed the Yamauchi case.
   * When the class begins to speculate about whether Kiyotaka possesses hidden extraordinary abilities, Suzune immediately dismantles the hypothesis before it gains traction in the classroom, reducing Kiyotaka to "someone who just runs fast." This narrative withstands the class's scrutiny because it introduces an irrefutable logical paradox: if Kiyotaka were a genuine threat, Sakayanagi would never have given him a protection point.
   * The strategy resisted any attempt by the class to seek alternatives. By establishing from the outset that losing a key asset would permanently destroy Class D, it logically precluded any talented or valuable student from taking the risk of expulsion.
   * The inherent fear of expulsion tied to the commander role

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING
   * When Ike demands that Kiyotaka himself answer, Suzune immediately intervenes, claiming that not even Kiyotaka knows the reason for his points. This calculated lie serves as an instant shield, taking the microphone away from Kiyotaka before he can make a mistake, raise further doubts, or find an excuse to shirk responsibility.
   * When Shinohara vehemently objects, claiming that appointing Kiyotaka as commander is "giving away the exam," Suzune doesn't ignore the complaint or dismiss it aggressively. Instead, she absorbs the objection, validates Shinohara's logic, and uses it as a trap: she openly asks the class if anyone else is willing to take the real risk of expulsion. When no one volunteers, she turns Shinohara's complaint into the final nail in the coffin, sealing Kiyotaka's candidacy.
   * To prevent the class from suspecting Kiyotaka's true strength or Sakayanagi's interest in him, Suzune weaves a perfect explanatory shield: she frames everything as a mere coincidence within Sakayanagi's personal vendetta to eliminate Yamauchi. This protects Kiyotaka's low profile and eliminates the discrepancy of a mediocre student receiving 42 votes.
   * The biggest potential pitfall for Class D in this exam was entering a losing-losing condition: either risking a valuable leader being expelled, or letting the school assign a commander at random. Suzune filled this structural gap by cornering Kiyotaka to fulfill the only two safeguard conditions: someone capable of pursuing victory who, at the same time, would leave the class unharmed if defeated.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * Suzune immediately foresaw that the students' individual performance would be worthless if the commander made a single mistake. She identified the true danger of the exam as lying in the commander's selection and the devastating impact of losing a valuable asset.
   * She reasoned in advance that Kiyotaka was the only perfect candidate because of his newly acquired protection point (which immunized him against expulsion in case of defeat). However, she foresaw that Kiyotaka would never put himself forward of his own volition under normal circumstances.
   * She knew that Sudou's questions about the 42 praise points Kiyotaka had received would trigger collective suspicion and increased social pressure on him. Rather than letting that pressure destroy Kiyotaka or reveal his potential, Suzune anticipated how to use that suspicion to corner Kiyotaka into running for office.
   * She devised a calculated lie, attributing the 42 praise points to Sakayanagi's plan to expel Yamauchi for personal reasons. She reduced Kiyotaka to "someone who just runs fast," ensuring the class wouldn't discover his true abilities while giving him a clean break for his reputation and a reason to step up. She predicted that Kiyotaka gradually revealing his skills would be the key to integrating him into the class.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * When Sudou suddenly stood up to demand explanations about Kiyotaka's points, the situation threatened to escalate. Suzune didn't try to silence the discussion abruptly; instead, she immediately intervened, took control of the conversation, and used the class's curiosity to set her manipulation trap.
   * When Ike demanded to hear directly from Kiyotaka, Suzune immediately changed her stance, firmly stating that Kiyotaka himself was unaware of the reason. This lie deflected Kiyotaka's direct pressure and gave Suzune the platform to construct the narrative about Sakayanagi.
   * When Sudou began to suspect that Kiyotaka possessed exceptional hidden abilities, Suzune adjusted the narrative by publicly downplaying Kiyotaka's abilities ("he's just fast") and arguing that Sakayanagi would never have given a single point of protection to a dangerous opponent.
   * When Shinohara argued that nominating Kiyotaka was throwing the exam away, Suzune turned that rejection to her advantage and used the low expectations surrounding Kiyotaka to challenge the class, suggesting that, faced with the risk of expulsion, the only real alternative was to accept the only volunteer who wouldn't suffer permanent consequences.
   * She reconfigured Kiyotaka's reputational crisis (being suspected of conspiring with Sakayanagi or receiving special treatment) into the perfect solution: running for commander not only saved the class from the risk of losing a key asset, but also allowed Kiyotaka to "clear his name" in the eyes of his peers.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   * Aligning Exam Rules with Class Needs:
   * The commander is the most critical piece, but if the class loses, he is expelled immediately.
   * Class D lacks reserve talent; losing a top asset would be fatal.
   * Suzune deduces that the chosen person must meet one of two strict conditions: guarantee victory OR be immune to the negative impact of defeat (due to possessing a protection point). Kiyotaka is the only intersection in the entire class who meets both conditions.
   * Alignment of Internal Discontent
   * The widespread suspicion fueled by Sudou regarding Kiyotaka's 42 votes of praise threatened to expose his true abilities or provoke internal paranoia.
   * Instead of avoiding the topic, Suzune takes control of the conversation and uses doubt as the direct fuel to justify Kiyotaka's candidacy as commander.
   * Yamauchi conspired with Sakayanagi.
   * Suzune intertwines this fact with a calculated lie, claiming that the flow of praise points towards Kiyotaka was just an arbitrary move by Sakayanagi to achieve Yamauchi's expulsion for personal reasons.
   * This dismantles the theory that Kiyotaka is a "hidden genius," protecting his peaceful lifestyle while logically explaining why he obtained the protection point.
   * Alignment of Collective Pressure
   * The complaint that Kiyotaka as commander amounts to "giving away the exam".
   * Suzune confronts the class, demanding that if they don't want Kiyotaka, someone else must step forward and risk expulsion.
   * The collective cowardice and inaction of Class C forced the class to accept Kiyotaka's voluntary/forced nomination due to a lack of alternatives willing to bear the cost.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   1. Layer 1: Narrative Reconstruction
   1. When Sudou and Ike demanded answers from Kiyotaka about the 42 points of praise he received in the previous exam, Suzune immediately intervened before Kiyotaka could speak or be cornered.
   1. She firmly denied that Kiyotaka knew the reason, using a strategic falsehood to quell direct questioning from the class and take full control of the narrative.
   2. When Sudou began to suspect that Kiyotaka might be an exceptional student, Suzune intentionally downplayed Kiyotaka's abilities, publicly dismissing him as "someone who's just fast." This ensured that the class wouldn't discover his true potential or see him as a threat or a strategic enigma.
   3. She linked Kiyotaka's points directly to Sakayanagi, recalling how she promised points to Yamauchi only to secretly divert them to Kiyotaka.
   4. She used the Protection Point as reverse logic: she argued that if Kiyotaka were truly a dangerous genius, Sakayanagi would never have granted him immunity/protection instead of eliminating him. She reduced everything to a mere coincidence stemming from Sakayanagi's personal vendetta against Yamauchi.
   5. Suzune identified that Kiyotaka was the only person in Class D who met both critical conditions: the actual ability to influence victory and total immunity in case of defeat due to the Protection Point.
   6. She let the class assess the real danger. If any other student assumed the position of commander and they lost, the class would suffer the permanent expulsion of one of its members or a key asset.
   7. When Shinohara protested, arguing that choosing Kiyotaka was tantamount to throwing the exam away, Suzune used the objection to her advantage. She directly questioned Shinohara and the rest of the class about whether any of them were willing to risk their own continued enrollment in the school to take the position. With no volunteers willing to risk expulsion, she made Kiyotaka's self-nomination the only viable and cost-free alternative for the group.

Personal ranking for the best strategy categories in this feat:
Tactical > Foresight > Shielding > Alignment > Execution > Effectiveness > Efficiency > Adaptability > Resilience

   * Class D Basket Strategy (Y1V11):
The special exam began, and the first event selected was one belonging to Class D: basketball. Since events were chosen at random, whenever a class’s own event appeared, it was essential to secure victory by fully utilizing its available talents, while still preserving key resources for future competitions.

For Suzune, events like this represented a critical opportunity. Winning did not only mean gaining points, but also setting the pace of the exam and reinforcing the morale of a class that was still psychologically unstable after recent events.

She commands Kiyotaka in her notes to use Makida Susumu, Minami Setsuya, Ike Kanji, Hondō Ryotarō, and Onodera Kayano.

Makida would be the team’s ace. According to Sudō, as long as Makida practiced enough, he should be skilled enough to pass the exam. Additionally, while Onodera’s true specialty was swimming, her basketball skills didn’t seem too bad. Therefore, Suzune decided that choosing her would be better for the team than picking a boy with no experience in the sport.

The plan was to bring Sudō when things started getting tough, using the power of the commander.

Sudō was a powerhouse in basketball, there was no one in the year who could beat him. Sakayanagi, knowing this, tried to get his teammates to frustrate Sudō, but it didn’t work. Suzune had been training him for months so that, in such situations, he wouldn’t lose control.

Suzune had practically exposed her strategy, predicting how they could stop her and preparing everything in advance to ensure they couldn’t defeat her.

In practice, the match was close. Class C was leading 12-11. Kiyotaka called Sudō while they still had the advantage. As planned, Sakayanagi used a tactic to throw Sudō off, which led to a score of 15-17 in favor of Class A. However, to prove himself in front of Suzune, Sudō regained his composure and turned the game around, just as Suzune had anticipated.

   * Typing/Archery Strategy (Y1V11):
Another event from Class C. In academics, Class A had a significant advantage. Suzune completely avoided that type of event and chose one that wouldn’t be influenced by such skills.

Suzune’s strategy was to use Sotomura for this match, as it was his specialty. Being a 1v1 battle, she gave him the entire field to face the Class A opponent. Additionally, she tried to limit the commander’s ability to intervene in the event as much as possible. The strategy was to let “The Professor” handle it on his own, without giving Sakayanagi too many opportunities to interfere.

In the end, Sotomura’s talent shone through over Class A, just as Suzune had hoped.

The same thing happened with Miyake in the archery event, a 1v1 duel in Akito's specialty to secure the victory.

   * Anti-Class A Tests Strategies (Y1V11) :
The exam largely depended on luck, but Suzune had predicted that at least two written tests would appear.

Suzune had outlined two different strategies in the notebooks she had prepared, in case two written tests were chosen. The first strategy was to aim for victories in both tests by evenly distributing the capable students between them. The second option was to deliberately lose one of them and concentrate all resources on the other to secure a win.

Kiyotaka chose the second option, which resulted in a victory for Class A.

In the next writing test event, Kiyotaka decided to follow Suzune's strategy and used the heavy hitters from Class C to ensure a win. With the information Suzune provided, he used: Hirata Yōsuke, Yukimura Teruhiko, Ishikura Kayoko, Wang Mei-Yu, Azuma Sana, Kushida Kikyō, and Nishimura Ryūko.

Yukimura secured first place, and Kiyotaka assisted Nishimura with a question. However, Class A still won due to their superior average.

   * FMA Strategy (Y1V11):
Another event from Class A.

Suzune's strategy for this event was to use Kōenji Rokusuke and Matsushita Chiaki. She recommended putting headphones on Matsushita, as she knew that Kōenji wouldn’t listen to anyone.

The reason Suzune chose Kōenji was that the event didn't rely on the class with the highest overall score. The victory would go to the student from the class who finished in first place. There was a chance that Kōenji could meet the expectations and win. But, in case he didn’t take the event seriously, Matsushita would be there as backup. Matsushita had a quick mind for math, which is why the plan had always been to use her in the math exam or in events like Flash Mental Arithmetic.

In the end, they were defeated, and Kōenji showed that he could have won if he had wanted to.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Y1V11.5-now):
Suzune’s primary objective is to reach Class A, whether to earn her brother’s recognition, as at the beginning of the story, or to prove her own worth, as in her current stage.

The problem is that this goal is extraordinarily difficult.

In their first month, the class started with 0 points, while the other classes held overwhelming advantages (Class C: 490, Class B: 650, Class A: 940). Their homeroom teacher not only failed to support them but actively sabotaged them, showing complete indifference.

Not to mention that the school system itself was designed to label them as inferior.

Class points are directly linked to private points—the money students use to live each month. With very few or none, as at the beginning, the class was forced to rely on low-quality free goods, which negatively affected their health and overall performance. Furthermore, in many special exams, having private points could determine victory or defeat.

Clear examples include the Sports Festival, where changing positions within a team required 100,000 private points, and the Class Poll, where saving someone required 20 million. Two exams in which their options were severely limited by their lack of capital.

Rival classes only worsened the difficulty. Class C, the closest threat, was ruled by a dictator—Ryūen—turning them into a unified and aggressive force. They excelled physically, and their leader was clever and ruthless enough to sacrifice even his own allies for marginal gains.

Class B was more friendly, but no less formidable. Under Ichinose’s charismatic leadership, they possessed absolute teamwork. While they were not extreme in either physical or intellectual ability, every member was above average, and their cohesion made them a difficult wall to overcome.

Finally, Class A. After resolving their internal power struggle, it fell under the control of Sakayanagi—a genius among geniuses—leading students considered elite even by elite standards.

With the system stacked against her and increasingly difficult rivals at every level, Horikita’s challenge seemed impossible… and yet, none of these obstacles were her true problem.

Suzune’s Class D was considered the worst Class D in the history of ANHS. It lacked clear leadership, had poorly defined roles, and suffered from slow, chaotic, or impulsive decision-making. There was an almost total absence of planning and concrete objectives, along with poor communication, frequent unresolved arguments, and a low level of commitment to a shared goal.

Many members acted purely out of obligation, showing a significant lack of discipline and consistency, and adopted individualistic attitudes that prioritized ego over collective results. Additionally, there was a notable disparity in skills and knowledge, leading to excessive reliance on one or two students, alongside a clear lack of technical and strategic preparation and an inability to adapt to problems or unexpected changes.

This was worsened by inefficient use of available resources, a tense, toxic, and demotivating environment, and the habit of blaming others for mistakes instead of taking responsibility. The lack of trust among members, combined with resistance to criticism and learning, resulted in unmet objectives or mediocre results, the constant repetition of the same mistakes, an inability to learn from defeats, and ultimately the gradual disintegration of the class under adversity.

They were the worst of the worst—something that inspired no confidence in Suzune. However, upon realizing that she could not reach Class A alone, she understood that she had to act to change the situation.

Her objective could be summarized in four clear points:
   * First, prevent the loss of class points due to misconduct or special exams.
   * Second, raise the class’s average level academically, physically, and mentally.
   * Third, build a cohesive unit that would not easily collapse.
   * Fourth, confront internal and external threats to prevent expulsions.

On this foundation, Suzune designed a strategy to accumulate points gradually and steadily while constantly strengthening the class as a whole, dealing with traitors, and ensuring that no one—or at least as few people as possible—was lost along the way.

This is a strange strategy because it wasn't that complex at first. But it evolved over time due to the changes in Suzune as a person throughout the light novel.

This strategy was conceived as soon as she learned about the S-System. The first thing Suzune did upon learning about the school system on May 1st of her first year was calculate how many points were deducted for misconduct such as talking or arriving late to class. This gave her an idea of her class’s weaknesses and allowed her to establish initial objectives that would evolve over time. These three objectives were:
   * Reduce tardiness
   * Minimize conversations and disturbances in class
   * Ensure everyone passed the midterm exam
Suzune knew she could do nothing about the first two points, so she focused on preventing expulsions during the midterm exam. To achieve this, she proposed a trial study group to see whether the failing students could be saved. After all, those with the lowest grades were not only the ones causing the most trouble but were also likely to continue reducing points in the future.

When they failed the trial, she initially decided it would be better to get rid of them now rather than let them continue harming the class. However, her conversation with Kiyotaka made her change her mind. This was her first shift in the objectives that would later define her strategy. Every human has value and strengths, and losing them would only be an expense that would make her path to the top more difficult.

Now determined not to lose anyone, she instilled the fundamentals the “idiot trio” needed in order to pass.

The next problem occurred exactly as she predicted—Sudō got into trouble, and now they had to win a trial to prevent his suspension and avoid losing class points. Suzune handled it herself, teaching him a lesson so he wouldn’t fall into such traps again.

In the process, she connected with Ichinose and formed a non-aggression alliance with her. This not only eliminated one rival but also ensured that Class A would be kept busy dealing with Class B. This reduced her enemies to only Ryūen and Class C, who had set the trap for Sudō.

Another gain from the Sudō incident was her deep understanding of the kind of person Ayanokōji Kiyotaka was. After observing him closely, Suzune had no doubt that Kiyotaka was a talented student—but a strange one. To her, Kiyotaka was a contradictory enigma, someone difficult to predict. Through careful observation, she concluded that he could never remain static and that it was impossible to classify him as a single type of person. He constantly adapted, never fitting into one definition.

This was a remarkably accurate assessment, considering that Kiyotaka was deliberately shaping his own personality beneath a cold, manipulative exterior. To Suzune, this made him strange—and dangerous.

For this reason, Kiyotaka was not someone she could fully trust. Suzune had already understood his mindset of viewing others as mere tools to be used. She knew they were using each other out of convenience. If she ceased to be useful, or if Kiyotaka devised a larger plan, he could turn against her. Still, he remained a valuable asset amid so much incompetence.

With the arrival of the first special exam, another layer of the S-System was revealed—a system that allowed them to either lose heavily or gain an advantage to approach the upper classes.

Class D did not have enough points to compete properly in the exam. Winning the first special exam—or at least preserving as many points as possible—was essential not only to maintain a realistic chance of reaching Class A but also to survive and avoid relying solely on low-quality free goods.

Despite sharing the same general objective, the class was deeply divided between boys and girls, as well as by their selfish priorities and needs. Kōenji, in particular, had no interest in the exam and planned to withdraw, which would result in a direct loss of points. Additionally, there was pressure from the strategies being prepared by other class leaders to secure victory.

As if the situation were not difficult enough, Suzune suffered a fever exceeding 38°C, causing severe physical discomfort—chills, trembling, excessive sweating, headaches, and dehydration—all while she was expected to shoulder the class’s strategic responsibilities.

After hearing the rules on the first day, Suzune’s strategy was to conceal the leader’s identity while accumulating points at the machine, spending as few points as possible.

Unfortunately, due to internal conspiracy, she failed and was saved by Kiyotaka. Despite the failure, she gained something more valuable: Kiyotaka’s support in reaching Class A. In doing so, she gained a degree of control over the unpredictable and powerful student by covering for him and maintaining his peaceful life. From that point on, Kiyotaka became the final safety net—someone she could rely on if everything went wrong.

That Kiyotaka saved her so many times throughout the play was nothing to scoff at. After all, she was the one who gave him the role of support and last barrier so that her strategy would be effective and the defeat would be less painful if she failed at something along the way.

Of course, Suzune had no idea of ​​Kiyotaka's true abilities, but she recognized his talent and that he was a key player, so officially making him her ally was a great move.

Along with her alliance with Ichinose, Suzune gradually began to form a vision of reaching Class A, especially after learning that she could not fight alone. With Kiyotaka protecting her from the shadows, she moved on to the next exam.

The next exam took place on the cruise ship—the Zodiac Exam. Divided into twelve groups, she attempted to reach a favorable outcome by acquiring as many private points as possible, but her influence was so limited that she could only affect her own group. Even so, she managed to prevent Class B and Class C from forming an alliance thanks to her non-aggression pact with Ichinose, and she successfully concealed the identity of the VIP throughout the exam. However, an internal leak ultimately cost them the exam.

At this point, everything was going wrong. Even if they were no longer losing many points, they were not gaining them at the same pace as other classes. Thanks to her secret weapon—Kiyotaka—they were still afloat, but the fact that Suzune could not keep up overall and that the class remained fragmented was still a serious issue.

In the next exam, the Sports Festival, Suzune, instead of working alone or proposing her ideas to only a few individuals, spoke in front of the entire class for the first time. She presented her ideas and ensured the class had a way to compete against others rather than charging in aimlessly. For the first time, Suzune united the class under a single banner—but there were mistakes.

Despite her newfound understanding, she failed due to betrayal by a classmate—Kushida. Even knowing that Kushida harbored resentment toward her and suspecting that she had already betrayed the class in the Zodiac Exam by leaking her identity as the VIP, Suzune could do nothing but fall into a trap orchestrated with Ryūen.

During the island special exam, Suzune learned that she should not face challenges alone and selfishly. On the cruise, she realized that she could be surpassed. In the Sports Festival, she worked with her class to create strategies, reduce risks, and prepare for consequences. Now, at last, she understood that she could not see the full picture. She had been so focused on herself that she had forgotten what was best for Class D.

This happened because her brother was on the same field as her. Suzune longed to be seen by him; she ran to catch up, but when she turned around, she realized there was no one behind her. Even so, she did not care, believing that as long as she had talent, her brother would eventually acknowledge her. That was why she participated in numerous competitions and achieved good results—so that one day he would notice her. That was also why she wanted to be the relay anchor: she clung to the fleeting hope that if she did, he would call out to her or cheer for her.

Whether for the class or for herself, deep down, these concerns were secondary for Suzune.

Realizing this, she understood that she lacked the individual talent to reach Class A on her own. She recognized that the methods she had been using were flawed. Since she could not fight alone, she needed strong allies to help her achieve her goals.

She only managed to escape the situation because her secret weapon, Kiyotaka, saved her once again—but Suzune was determined not to be trampled again. This was when her strategy truly began.

She started with Sudō. After the Sports Festival, he had grown close enough to become her partner. Sudō was violent, foul-mouthed, academically at an elementary school level, and had a terrible temper—but he was also the best athlete in the entire class. Suzune began holding private sessions with him to educate him academically and mentally, shaping him into a potentially ideal student for the future. In doing so, she gained a new piece she could use in future exams to achieve her objective. Not to mention that she motivated him to continue his club activities, which provided another passive source of class points.

Not only with Sudou, but before each midterm and final exam, a study group was formed with the lowest-achieving students in the class (Ike, Yamauchi, and Sudou) to ensure they passed the exam and raised their averages, thus passively boosting the class's grades.

This study group would grow over time, eventually including all those at risk of expulsion, but that only happened in Year 2.

While training Sudō, a new special exam began—the Paper Shuffle. Based on her previous experiences, Suzune devised a plan to defeat Class C, deceive Ryūen, neutralize Kushida without expelling her so her abilities could be used for the class’s benefit, increase Class D’s points, and save every member of Class D. As a result, Class D was promoted to Class C through her efforts.

During and after the exam, she led study groups that soon became the norm to raise the class’s academic level so they would not fall behind other classes and to gradually increase the points earned each month. She leveraged her alliance with Class B to fill the gap left by the lack of academically capable students in Class D, gaining new allies within her own class, such as Yukimura. As for Kushida, she did not expel her despite her repeated betrayals due to her exceptional abilities, using her as a bridge to the class.

In this exam, by recognizing her own past deficiencies and cooperating with the class with the general benefit as her objective, Suzune consolidated her position as a central figure within the class.

Thanks to the trust she gained among the students, Suzune was now openly listened to by everyone. With Hirata, Karuizawa, and Kushida at her side, capable students like Yukimura and Sudō supporting her directly, and Kiyotaka supporting her from the shadows, the class was practically hers.

This was reflected in how she coordinated the class to avoid being provoked by Ryūen’s search for “X” or by the  “Sakayanagi” rumors spread across the classes. In this way, she prevented doubts from forming among classmates and stopped conflicts before they arose.

Her new level of control became even more apparent in the next special exam—the Mixed Training Camp (MTC).

During the MTC, she once again used her alliance with Class B, ensuring that the largest female groups were formed by combining members of Classes B and C. Each class shared its weakest students with the other’s main group. A representative example is Mii-chan, who struggled severely with communication. All of this was done using Kushida’s “good girl” mask as her spokesperson to reach everyone. In this way, Suzune ensured no one was expelled, gained points for the class, and avoided demerits.

However, Class C (formerly Class D) remained stubborn. Students considered the worst of the worst, even if they trusted her to prevent expulsions, were still a brutal disadvantage.

This was proven in the next special exam—the Class Poll. The possibility of expulsion without a real way to prevent it overwhelmed Class C, plunging it into collective anxiety. There was no clear objective or defined criteria, making every student a potential expulsion candidate.

Because the vote was anonymous, fear of retaliation disappeared completely. Students could vote for anyone without direct consequences, creating widespread paranoia. Without “allies” to protect them, everyone felt exposed. This led to extreme vulnerability and a form of selective social anxiety: some acted excessively kind to avoid being seen as a threat, while others withdrew entirely to avoid attention.

This psychological environment generated resentment even before a single vote was cast, as everyone understood the power their classmates held to destroy their future with a single decision. In response, informal support groups emerged, where members promised mutual votes to ensure their own safety while selecting a common target for negative votes.

Indirectly guided by Sakayanagi of Class A through the traitor Yamauchi—who succumbed to pressure—Kushida’s social manipulation was used to form a large group targeting Kiyotaka, an exceptional student who deliberately presented himself as ordinary. Without him, Class D would have been effectively finished, both strategically and structurally.

Seeing all of this, Suzune acted to prevent Kiyotaka’s expulsion—the class’s most valuable strategic asset—identified and expelled Yamauchi, a negative student, traitor, and external pawn, exposed and limited Kushida’s manipulation, stabilized Class D’s psychological collapse, and redirected hostility from random targets toward a justified conclusion.

Suzune used to be a cold, selfish girl who did not care about anyone and refused to work as a team—or at least, that is how she was at the beginning. Over time, she changed: she learned the value of teamwork, that she was not a genius who could handle everything alone, and that she needed to connect with and appreciate those around her. Because of this shift in mindset, choosing someone for expulsion had become extremely difficult for her. However, she had found her answer.

Her answer was to voluntarily assume the role of moral executioner in an emotionally unstable class, fully aware that:
   * She would earn Yamauchi’s open hatred
   * She could become the next target if she failed
   * Hirata, Sudō, and part of the class would emotionally oppose her
   * The exam involved an irreversible expulsion
   * She would feel guilt for sacrificing a classmate

Suzune was willing to shatter everything she had built to save Class C in order not to lose a valuable asset.

However, her honesty in the face of adversity earned her the respect of all students, and with Hirata stepping back under pressure, she officially became the leader of Class C (formerly Class D).

Her new influence was reflected in the Event Selection Exam, where the class followed every one of her orders and organized themselves in a way that gave them a chance against Class A—the strongest class in the entire grade. The only reasons they lost were the randomness of the events, Kōenji’s unpredictability and inactivity, and Acting Director Tsukishiro’s sabotage of Kiyotaka. Even so, Suzune maximized Class C’s competitive potential, neutralized systemic disadvantages, strategically countered Class A’s events, and protected the class from expulsion while preserving Kiyotaka’s peaceful life by dispelling doubts about why he had obtained a protection point in the Class Poll. She also used Kiyotaka to motivate Hirata to return stronger than before.

Even when they eventually fell back to Class D, under Suzune’s leadership the class stopped self-destructing. She exposed and caused Yamauchi’s expulsion, controlled Kushida’s influence, understood Hirata’s desires, maintained a cooperative relationship with Class B, and avoided unnecessary expulsions—creating a solid class for future competition and a foundation for the start of the second year.

In her first year alone, Suzune transformed Class D from a chaotic and self-destructive group into a stable and organized class capable of steady progress under clear leadership.

While Kiyotaka focused on supporting and improving the class leaders (Suzune, Hirata, Kei), Suzune took it upon herself to improve everyone else and turn them into capable allies. This is most evident in their second year. (This feat follows in Year 2 and is more Y2 feat.)......

Summary
