Planning Categories
   * Fake study group (Y1V1):
Planning Formulation -  Suzune analyzes the class's behavior patterns following the revelation of the S System. She identifies that the massive loss of Class Points is primarily due to indiscipline and poor academic performance. Observing that study groups within the class (such as the one led by Hirata) are rejected by the lowest-performing students (Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi), Suzune hypothesizes that these three elements represent an imminent threat of expulsion that will drag down the entire Class D.

Step 1: Bait Kiyotaka

Knowing that she does not possess the communication channel or social closeness to directly summon the trio of disabled people, Suzune designs a mechanism of forced intermediation. She invites Kiyotaka to lunch at the cafeteria, offering to buy him "whatever he wants." She anticipates that Kiyotaka will try to choose the most expensive menu item to annoy or test her; she accepts this in advance as a necessary operating cost. By having Kiyotaka consume the luxury lunch, she creates an instant "social/financial debt." She rejects Kiyotaka's attempts to pay her back in cash and turns the lunch into a tactical blackmail contract: the only way to repay the debt is by serving as a social bridge between her and the three idiots.

During the same lunch conversation, Suzune logically guides Kiyotaka to the conclusion that the only solution is to create a study group. When Kiyotaka tries to refuse, claiming it's none of his business, Suzune uses the lunch debt as leverage for direct extortion and adds the forgiveness of his previous entrapment at Café Palette as a final incentive. She gives him her phone number and establishes a strict control channel, periodically reminding him of his mission under threat of social repercussions.

Step 2: Operational Adaptability

Kiyotaka manages to reunite the trio, but adds two variables to the equation not considered in the original formulation: Kushida and Okitani. Instead of rejecting Kushida's presence (who is very popular and has a gentler teaching style), Suzune integrates her into the group dynamic. She observes the impact of Kushida's presence on the trio's attention and motivation to see if social influence can compensate for a lack of intellect. Suzune begins the study group session not only to teach, but as an exercise in pedagogical resistance.
   * She assigns them exercises on systems of equations. She tries to guide them patiently and explain things in simple terms (elementary school level) when she sees that they lack even the most basic foundations.
   * She observes that at the slightest difficulty, Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi don't try to reason things out, but instead give up immediately.
   * She allows Kushida to try teaching them with her own method. When she notices that even with Kushida's empathetic teaching they still can't process the information, she confirms that the problem isn't the teacher, but rather the students' inherent apathy.

        Step 3: Voluntary Dismantling

Upon confirming that the trio lacks the will to improve and will represent a continuous drain on energy and future points, Suzune activates the group's self-destruction phase:
   * She launches a direct verbal attack on Sudō's pride and ego at his most vulnerable point.
   * Just as she anticipated, Sudō explodes in anger and leaves the room. Ike and Yamauchi follow him out of group loyalty, causing the session's final collapse.
   * Suzune makes no effort whatsoever to stop them. The study group's public "failure" is, in reality, the successful execution of her filter.
Suzune concludes that trying to save unmotivated students is a terrible long-term investment of resources. If she saves them on the midterm, the problem will simply repeat itself on the final and every makeup exam. Furthermore, observing Kiyotaka's cold indifference to the departure of his "friends," Suzune extracts a critical analytical piece of information about his personality: Kiyotaka has no real emotional ties with the trio, confirming that he is a usable pragmatic asset.

Short Term Planning - Suzune realized she couldn't directly invite Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi due to social friction. Instead of wasting energy trying herself, she identified Ayanokōji as the perfect intermediary. Suzune prepared a progressive syllabus, starting with first-degree equations and progressing to elementary school-level concepts. She intentionally maintained her composure while measuring three crucial factors:
   * Basic cognitive ability.
   * Willingness to make an effort / Tolerance for frustration.
   * Receptiveness to others' teaching (even when Kushida tried to explain using gentler methods).
Upon confirming that the trio lacked discipline and focus, Suzune didn't lose control of her emotions. She used a calculated provocation aimed at Sudō's pride to trigger his departure and that of the others, disbanding the group without having to bear the cost of being the one to resign arbitrarily.

Long Term Planning -
   * Suzune's ultimate goal was to accumulate Class Points and avoid losses. Her long-term logic at this early stage of the story was purely pragmatic: saving students who represented a recurring burden was a poor investment of resources. If it required a disproportionate effort to get them to pass a midterm exam, they would continue to cause point losses in every future special exam. Therefore, the study group served as a filter to determine whether it was worthwhile to keep them or let them be expelled to rid the class of structural burdens.
   * Immediately after the group's split with Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi, Suzune offered Ayanokōji private tutoring. This demonstrates that the entire process served to eliminate false targets and isolate the only individual she considered to have real potential to be a valuable partner in her long-term plan.
   * During the study group's resolution, Suzune observed Ayanokōji's complete apathy and indifference toward the fate of his "friends." This quick reading allowed her to sketch Ayanokōji's profile from Volume 1: a highly capable individual but devoid of conventional empathy, which laid the groundwork for how she would interact and negotiate with him in the following months.

Planning Intricacies -
   1. Layer 1: The Social Trap
   1. Suzune predicted that freely inviting Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi would result in immediate rejection. Therefore, she identified Kiyotaka as the only viable social bridge to them.
   2. She anticipated that Ayanokōji would choose the most expensive menu item to spite her, deliberately accepted the cost, and transformed this economic/social debt into a form of extortion (a moral debt).
   3. She neutralized Kiyotaka's attempts to escape using social logic, also forgiving him for the Café Palette incident in exchange for his cooperation, leaving him no room for refusal.
   2. Layer 2: The Diagnostic Test
   1. She used the class's visible distress and the trio's lack of participation in Hirata's groups as perfect cover.
   2. She established the facade of a "responsible leader" focused on saving Class D's points so as not to arouse suspicion in Kiyotaka or the other participants about her true intentions.
   3. She designed the initial exercises (basic systems of equations) not only as a teaching tool, but also as an analytical filter.
   4. She evaluated two key factors in the participants:
   1. their actual academic level a
   2. their receptiveness/attitudinal discipline.
   3. Layer 3: Targeted Provocation and Dismantling
   1. When the trio demonstrated utter incompetence and a lack of will (even rejecting Kushida's teaching methods), Suzune executed the breaking point.
   2. She used carefully chosen words to strike Sudō's pride and ego at their most vulnerable spot, causing him to leave immediately and prompting Ike and Yamauchi to flee.
   3. She maintained absolute composure throughout the argument, allowing the group to collapse without making the slightest effort to stop them.
   4. Freed from the burden of the three least promising students, she immediately redirected her focus.
   5. She offered private tutoring to Ayanokōji, whom, after observing him, she determined to be the only individual with real potential worth "saving" and integrating into her circle.

Planning Aim -
   * She was able to confirm through field tests that attempting to save Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi at that moment would create an endless cycle of future problems. The tactical conclusion that it was preferable to drop the "dead weight" to focus on more efficient resources was fulfilled with complete analytical precision.
   * She managed to get Ayanokōji to carry out the summons exactly as she had planned, without him being able to get out of the commitment.
   * The unexpected arrival of Kushida or Okitani to the group did not alter their central objective; on the contrary, Suzune used Kushida's presence (and the trio's inability to learn even with her) as the perfect trigger to justify the attack on Sudō's ego.
   * The hidden goal of the plan culminated in the re-evaluation of Ayanokōji. Observing Kiyotaka's cold indifference to the departure of his "friends," Suzune confirmed two things with absolute certainty: that he was not an ordinary student who had simply joined the group, and that he possessed a superior strategic value that justified focusing her tutoring efforts on him.

Planning Process -
   * Suzune immediately recognized that she lacked the social connections to summon Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi. Instead of forcing a direct invitation that would have been rejected, she solved the problem by coercing Kiyotaka through implicit debt and social leverage, transforming him into her direct intermediary.
   * When Kiyotaka appeared not only with the trio but also with Kushida and Okitani, the plan's structure was disrupted. Far from being destabilized, Suzune absorbed Kushida's presence to expedite the competence assessment, allowing her to attempt to teach using her own methods to gauge whether the problem lay with her instructional style or with the trio's structural inadequacy.
   * Upon realizing that the trio's level didn't even reach the basics, he adjusted his teaching methods on the fly to the elementary school level. When he realized that their lack of motivation invalidated any pedagogical effort, he resolved the "problem" by strategically provoking Sudō's exit through a direct attack on his ego, thus ending the test without having to awkwardly adjourn the session.

Planning Preparation Process -
   1. Conditioning Kiyotaka: She financed an expensive lunch, arguing social etiquette to logically corner Kiyotaka before submitting the request, ensuring compliance through constant follow-up via messaging.
   2. Tutoring Facade: She set up the study session under the pretext of helping the trio pass the midterm exam, hiding the real premise of the test.
   3. Provocation and Discard: She calculated Sudō's response to the criticism to force a natural split within the group. She orchestrated this outburst to conclude the field test without leaving any suspicion that she herself had planned the dissolution.
   4. She designed progressive exercises (from systems of equations to basic arithmetic) to test two variables: cognitive ability and attitudinal disposition.
   5. She established beforehand that if the subjects showed inability and disinterest, the logical conclusion would not be to persist, but rather to minimize losses.
   6. She allowed Kushida to intervene. This served as a control group: if the students also failed to respond to Kushida's empathetic approach, it confirmed that the problem lay with the students' attitudes, not Suzune's strictness.
   7. Once she had gathered the necessary data, she used a verbal jab aimed at Sudō's pride to dissolve the meeting without assuming the direct social cost of canceling the group herself.
The entire tactical chain was designed to answer a single strategic question: Is it worth investing class resources in saving these three individuals? Concluding that they represented a recurring and inefficient expense, the plan culminated perfectly with the offer of mentorship to Kiyotaka, reallocating their resources toward an asset with real potential.

Planning Coverage -
   * Management of Psychological Variables
   * She anticipated that Kiyotaka would try to annoy her by ordering the most expensive item on the menu. Suzune not only anticipated this, but deliberately allowed it to happen in order to ethically put him in debt.
   * She neutralized Kiyotaka's attempts to avoid the situation by using sociological arguments about Class D's status (class points) to force him to acknowledge the need for the study group.
   * She knew Kiyotaka had a close relationship with Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi. Instead of directly offering herself as the leader to the trio, she used Kiyotaka as an unwitting bridge to recruit them.
   * She refused to refund the money when Kiyotaka tried to pay her, subtly threatening him with guilt over their previous meeting at Café Palette, and sealed the deal by giving him her phone number.
   * Scenario Mapping
   * Scenario A: If the students demonstrated basic ability and a willingness to improve, the class retained its academic standing and avoided the loss of points due to expulsions.
   * Scenario B: If the trio proved to be academically incompetent and relentlessly lazy, the group's collapse was triggered by an attack on Sudō's ego. This served to confirm that the expenditure of energy was not worthwhile and that it was more efficient to trim the "ballast" early on.
   * Hazard Coverage
   * Suzune lost nothing when the group broke up; on the contrary, she obtained the complete diagnostic information she was looking for. She deliberately provoked Sudō's outburst of anger by striking at his most vulnerable ego point, knowing that Ike and Yamauchi would follow suit, allowing the group to disintegrate organically without her being seen as the direct culprit for the departure, but rather as the strict tutor whose "student incompetence" had crossed the line.

Planning Versatility -
   * Absorption of Unexpected Variables
   * Kiyotaka arrived at the study group session not only with the trio of idiots, but also with Kushida Kikyo and Okitani.
   * Instead of getting frustrated or canceling the test because of the intrusion of the class's most popular student (Kushida), Suzune included Kushida in the assessment.
   * She let Kushida try to teach the trio using her own friendly and didactic method. When the trio still failed to grasp Kushida's explanation, Suzune used this complete failure as the final straw to deliver the decisive verbal blow: if they couldn't understand the elementary level even with Kushida's gentle method, the problem wasn't the teaching method, but the students' utter inadequacy.
   * Resource Versatility
   * She transformed a common economic good (an expensive lunch) into a tool of psychological coercion and an unwritten social contract.
   * As she dismissed the trio of idiots, Suzune immediately redirected her tutoring resources toward Kiyotaka, offering him private lessons directly.
   * She used Kiyotaka's apathy and indifference to the group's disintegration to draw a crucial conclusion about his character: she discovered that Kiyotaka truly didn't care about his "friends," thus confirming her internal perception of him as a cold, distant, and potentially exploitable asset.

Planning Risk Calculation -
   * Suzune correctly identified that Class D would repeatedly lose class points if academically deficient students remained in the system. Her calculation evaluated two options:
   * Option A: Temporarily save them by teaching them from scratch (high energy cost, low long-term performance).
   * Option B: Subject them to a stress test to assess their discipline. If they show no adaptability or willingness, allow their early elimination to prevent them from further dragging down the overall score in the future.
   * Aware that she lacked the social pull to directly convince Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi, she calculated the risk of approaching Kiyotaka. To neutralize his predictable refusal, she set up a social debt trap:
   * She bought him the most expensive lunch on the menu, anticipating Kiyotaka's reaction of ordering the most costly item just to spite her.
   * Once the meal was eaten, she used the debt as direct leverage, cutting off any logical escape route.
   * As a strategic incentive, she forgave him for the previous deception at Café Palette, leaving Kiyotaka with no grounds to refuse the task of acting as a social bridge.

Planning Contingency -
   * When Kiyotaka brought Kushida and Okitani into the session—an unforeseen discrepancy in the initial structure—Suzune didn't abandon the plan. Instead of letting Kushida's presence disrupt the dynamic, she integrated her to see if a gentler teaching methodology would work with the trio.
   * When she confirmed that the trio lacked even the most basic concepts and the will to learn (even with Kushida's intervention), Suzune executed her closing contingency:
   * She launched a direct and stinging attack on Sudō's pride and ego.
   * Knowing exactly how Sudō's temper would react, she provoked his immediate departure, which triggered a chain reaction of exodus by Ike and Yamauchi.
   * She made no effort to stop them because the group's collapse completed the information gathering she sought: to determine that they weren't worth saving..
   * After the session was broken up, Suzune immediately redirected her contingency plan: she offered Kiyotaka private tutoring. Recognizing that Kiyotaka possessed a far more analytical mind and a detached attitude from his "friends," she concluded that he was the only asset in that group truly worth cultivating and protecting.

Planning Invincibility -
   * Eliminating Gaps in Recruitment
   * Suzune anticipated Ayanokōji's passive resistance to getting involved in class affairs. Instead of openly asking for his help (which would have resulted in immediate rejection), she devised a seamless scenario of social and economic blackmail:
   * She offered to buy him any lunch he wanted. Anticipating that Ayanokōji would deliberately choose the most expensive dish to spite her, she let him, maximizing the moral debt.
   * When Ayanokōji tried to pay her to settle the obligation, Suzune refused the money, leaving the "debt of gratitude/honor" intact and transforming it into the primary leverage point.
   * As an added measure of payment, she forgave him for tricking her into going to Café Palette, neutralizing any moral counterargument from Ayanokōji.
   * Contingency Plan for Unexpected Variables
   * When Ayanokōji attended the study group bringing Kushida and Okitani with him, Suzune did not panic or allow the strategy to be derailed.
   * Instead of rejecting Kushida, Suzune embraced her presence to expedite the trio's diagnosis.
   * She allowed Kushida to attempt to teach them using her gentle, friendly approach. When even Kushida's method proved ineffective, Suzune received definitive confirmation that the trio's problem wasn't methodological or emotional, but rather an irremediable lack of attitude and intellect.
   * Inviolable Outcome Design
   * The plan had a structure where any outcome represented a strategic victory for Suzune:
   * Scenario A: The trio learns the basics, passes the exams, and Class D doesn't lose any Class Points due to expulsions.
   * Scenario B: The trio proves incompetent, Suzune orchestrates their collapse with a calculated attack on Sudō's ego, and justifies cutting back on their training to focus on individual preparation or higher-value students (Ayanokōji).

Planning Accuracy -
   1. Cause-and-Effect Connection of Micro-Tactics
   1. Each step of the plan was intertwined with impeccable sequential logic:
   1. Suzune knew she lacked the social connection with Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi to summon them directly. The bridge was Ayanokōji. Binding Ayanokōji automatically ensured the trio's presence without Suzune expending any direct social capital.
   2. She began the session with simple exercises (systems of equations) not only for teaching purposes, but also as an analytical benchmarking tool. She calculated precisely the minimum level required so as not to be a burden on the midterm exam.
   2. Damage Control
   1. The breakup of the study group wasn't an impulsive outburst on Suzune's part, but rather the execution of her diagnosis. Upon realizing that the trio lacked even basic elementary school concepts and showed a complete lack of motivation, she used coldly calculated words to strike at Sudō's pride. This led to the trio's dramatic departure without Suzune appearing as the one who simply "gave up" without even trying.
   2. A meticulous detail considered in the final phase was testing Ayanokōji's true character. After the trio's departure, Suzune offered him mentorship. Observing his complete indifference to the expulsion or fate of his own "friends," Suzune was able to map Ayanokōji's true psychology: a pragmatic, cold, and detached individual, confirming that he was a valuable asset with whom she could operate based on pure logic and mutual benefit.

Personal ranking for Suzune Fake study group (Y1V1) categories:

   * Sudou Trial (Y1V2):
Planning Formulation -  Sae revealed Sudo's problem, and after analyzing the situation, Suzune decided to help him once again. However, she realized that if she kept bailing him out without consequence, Sudo would continue causing trouble in the future. For that reason, she pretended not to help him on the surface, aiming for him to understand the true reason he was incriminated and to reflect on his actions so he wouldn't repeat them. Making Sudo think is her main goal.

Step 1: Maintaining a consistent facade to the point of desperation

Suzune adopted a seemingly indifferent attitude because she knew that the idiot trio and Kushida would question why she was not helping this time, given that she had done so in the past. That question would inevitably be directed at Kiyotaka, so she subtly guided him by leaving small hints, trusting that he was perceptive enough to notice them and respond on her behalf, which he did.

Although it appeared that Suzune genuinely reconsidered her stance after Kiyotaka mentioned earning points through clubs, she had already identified Sakura as the key witness well before that conversation. She intentionally remained silent, waiting for the idiot trio and Kushida’s efforts to fail on their own.

Once she saw them reaching despair, Suzune decided it was the optimal moment to act. She approached Kiyotaka to share her discovery so he could relay it to the others. However, her plan partially misfired when she arrived at his room and found him not alone, resulting in everyone learning of her involvement.

Step 2: Investigation

Suzune led Kiyotaka to the special building, the site where the incident had taken place. As she carefully examined her surroundings, she noticed the special building had no security cameras to provide evidence. In addition to meeting Ichinose and her situation

Step 3: Recruit Sakura

Suzune recognized her lack of close connections and empathetic reputation within the class. As a result, she delegated the task of securing Sakura’s cooperation to Kushida and Kiyotaka, while she focused on preparing the logical and procedural groundwork for the trial.

Step 4: Dialectical Counterattack in the Trial

She began by interrogating members of Class C, carefully searching for inconsistencies in their testimonies. During this process, she questioned Ishizaki’s presence, noting that as a non–club member, he had no legitimate reason to be at the scene.

Drawing on her knowledge of martial arts, Suzune explained how physical confrontations typically unfold, demonstrating that the fight could not have occurred unless the opposing party had intended to engage from the outset.

Step 5: Use Sakura

With this foundation established, Suzune called Sakura as a witness. Due to Sakura’s poor communication skills, she hesitated, which Sakagami immediately exploited to undermine her credibility.

Before Sakura could be fully discredited, Suzune intervened decisively, counterattacking Sakagami’s argument and buying Sakura enough time to regain her composure and present her evidence.

Sakura’s testimony cornered Sakagami, compelling him to propose a compromise. Kiyotaka pretended to accept it, and Suzune instantly recognized his intent. Within seconds, she devised a way to reverse the situation entirely.

Suzune reprimanded Sudou, then fixed the teachers with an intimidating gaze before firmly reaffirming Sudou’s innocence and outright rejecting the compromise. This created a stalemate, prompting the student council president to grant a grace period to continue the trial.

Step 6: Fake camera Strategy

To secure victory, Suzune used Ichinose and Kiyotaka to corner Ishizaki, Komiya, and Kondo. They argued that the school was already aware of their lies and that the trial itself was merely a test to observe their reactions.

This narrative applied intense psychological pressure, suggesting that continued resistance would lead to expulsion. The claim was reinforced by the alleged existence of security cameras, lending credibility to the deception.

Caught in their own lie and fearing consequences, the accusers withdrew their complaint, allowing Suzune to achieve her objective.

Throughout the final phase, Suzune remained in the student council room, keeping Sudou under control to prevent last-minute mistakes and ensuring he did not jeopardize the outcome.

Suzune successfully minimized damage, strengthened her alliance with Class B, resolved the incident in Sudou’s favor, and most importantly forced Sudou to confront the need to change his attitude to avoid future trouble, while preparing contingencies in case things had gone wrong.

Short Term Planning - Suzune's short-term planning to resolve the trial in its final stage is a masterpiece of social engineering, environmental exploitation, and damage mitigation. In a matter of minutes, Suzune devised a highly detailed sequence of actions that did not rely on chance, unlike the Fake Camera Strategy.

Long Term Planning - Although the immediate problem was avoiding Sudō's expulsion/suspension so as not to lose class points, Suzune's real plan was long-term: to transform the most dangerous and impulsive link in the class into a disciplined asset.
   * Suzune intentionally pretended not to help Sudō on the surface at the beginning of the volume. She knew that if she automatically saved him (like in the previous exam with the trio of idiots), Sudō would develop a dependency and continue to get into trouble in the future.
   * Her main objective in the plan was to force Sudō to think and reflect on the consequences of his actions. She wanted him to experience the despair of helplessness so that he would appreciate the collective effort needed to protect him.
   * While Ichinose and Kiyotaka executed the camera trap, Suzune stayed behind with Sudō in the student council room, not only to prevent him from making a last-minute mistake, but also to reinforce the behavioral lesson.
This long-term planning paid off throughout the entire story. Sudō went from being a violent student on the verge of expulsion to becoming the best athlete in the class, Suzune's personal tutor, and a key player in the subsequent special exams.

Planning Intricacies -
   1. Layer 1: Internal Psychological Re-education
   1. Pretending not to help Sudō on the surface to force him to reflect on his actions and identify why he was framed, preventing him from repeating these outbursts in the future.
   2. Layer 2: Social Expectation Management
   1. Knowing that a lack of superficial action would raise suspicions in Kushida and the trio of idiots, Suzune left clues for Ayanokōji to answer in her place while she secretly investigated.

   1. Keep the discovery of the witness secret until the efforts of her colleagues failed and they became desperate, introducing the resource at the moment of greatest impact.

   1. Interrogate Class C at the trial, dismantle their narrative using martial arts knowledge, and reject Sakagami's compromise to force a stalemate that would gain a grace period.
   5. Layer 5: The Fake Cameras:
   1. Lure Class C to the special building by Kushida at 3:40 PM, using direct sunlight and sweltering heat (25-30°C without air conditioning) to disorient their judgment and pressure them with a fake camera.
   6. Layer 6: Institutional Shielding:
   1. Delegate the purchase of the camera to Kanzaki and the direct confrontation to Ichinose. If the school discovered the deception, the blame would fall solely on Class B; if it worked, it would strengthen the alliance without harming Class D.

Planning Aim -
   * Suzune not only devised a way to corner the aggressors, but she built a trap that combined multiple external and internal factors:

   * She devised the logistics so that Kanzaki would buy the fake camera and Ichinose would be the most active figure. This way, if the school detected the deception, the punishment would fall on Class B, leaving Class D unscathed.
   * If the plan succeeded (as it did), Class D would save Sudō without repercussions, Class C would drop the charges, and Class B would strengthen its relationship of trust with Class D by feeling valued in the operation.
   * While Ichinose and Kiyotaka carried out the deception in the special building, Suzune remained in the student council room with Sudō. This final maneuver ensured that Sudō wouldn't make any last-minute mistakes and, crucially, allowed her to fulfill her main Planning Aim: to make Sudō understand the need to change his destructive attitude.

      * Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6):
Planning Formulation - Suzune analyzes Professor Sae’s instructions and deduces several key points:

      * The exam will not affect individual grades.
      * The failure criteria are not defined.
      * Pairs will be assigned after the test.
      * Expulsions will occur based on the results.

From this, she concludes that students with higher grades will be paired with those with lower performance. This means the class needs a strategy to avoid expulsions while surpassing the other classes.

Step 1: Choosing the Opponent

After understanding the pairing system, Suzune moves on to the next phase: manipulating which class to face. She forces Hirata, Karuizawa, Kushida, Sudo, and Kiyotaka to reach the conclusion that the only class they can realistically defeat is Class C. However, her true goal is to face Ryuuen and create an opportunity for Kushida to betray them.

Step 2: Gaining the Class’s Trust

Knowing that her classmates wouldn’t listen to her easily, Suzune uses Hirata to introduce her into the conversation naturally. Before discussing the exam, she apologizes for her poor performance in the sports festival and admits her mistakes, surprising the class and gaining their empathy.

Then, she explains the exam rules with Hirata’s help and ensures that everyone understands them.

Step 3: Grading Strategy

Suzune designs a plan to stabilize the class’s performance by organizing students into four groups:
      * The 10 lowest-scoring students → Must write their name on the exam and score a 0.
      * The 10 highest-scoring students → Must score at least 85 points.
      * 10 upper-middle-level students → Must score a maximum of 80 points.
      * 10 lower-middle-level students → Only need 1 point.
This balances the results and ensures beneficial pairings.

Step 4: Dealing with the Rebel (Kōenji)

To make sure everyone follows the plan, Suzune publicly confronts Kōenji, who could ruin the strategy if he refuses to cooperate. Although he responds ambiguously, Suzune trusts that he will follow the strategy to avoid his own expulsion.

Step 5: Creating Study Groups

Suzune organizes daily study sessions:
      * 4:00 PM - 6:00 PM: Supervised by Suzune.
      * 8:00 PM - 10:00 PM: Supervised by Hirata.
Each session has designated tutors, such as Kushida and Yukimura. Additionally, Suzune uses Kiyotaka to ensure that students actually study and don’t waste time.

To prevent information leaks by class C spy and optimize learning, Suzune forms an alliance with Class B to protect class D from class C spies, with the excuse of applying the Feynman technique (learning by teaching) that benefited both classes and improved their relationship for future cooperation. In this way solving the problem of the lack of teachers and protecting the class  at the same time

Step 6: Controlling Kushida, the Traitor

Suzune knows that Kushida is a threat to the class, but instead of expelling her, she decides to manipulate her into working in their favor. To achieve this:
      * She keeps her busy with study groups.
      * She forbids her from participating in exam question preparation.
      * She leaves only one way for her to betray them: writing her own questions and passing them to Class C.
      * Suzune also alerts Chabashira to only accept her questions and reject any others.

Step 7: The Bet Against Kushida

Kushida has no options to act and Suzune proposes a bet to ensure her don't do anything drastic:

      * If Suzune scores higher than Kushida on the final exam, Kushida must stop betraying the class.
      * If Kushida wins, Suzune will leave the school.

Kushida accepts and chooses math as the subject for the bet. As a guarantee, Suzune brings in the former student council president, Horikita Manabu, as a witness, ensuring that both will keep their word.

Step 8: Deceiving Ryuuen

Ryuuen, the leader of Class C, tries to sabotage Class D by obtaining the exam answers. Suzune, predicted this, waits until the last moment to submit her questions and pretends to be desperate when she "discovers" Kushida’s betrayal.

This makes Ryuuen overconfident, preventing him from taking further action, unaware that Suzune had planned this from the beginning. Plus, acting like she's lost gives Kushida a false sense of security so she'll slack off on her exams.

With this plan, Suzune successfully:

      * Defeats Class C.
      * Deceives Ryuuen and gets her revenge.
      * Neutralizes Kushida without expelling her to leverage their skills for the class.
      * Elevates Class D Class Points
      *  Save all members of class D

Not to mention all the countermeasures she took and the problems she solved along the way, such as lack of guardians, denied information leaks and countermeasures to the future such as improving the class average and achieving a non-hostile relationship with Kushida.

Only the final exam with Kushida remained. Suzune was sure and confident that she would get 100 points no matter what questions Class C asked, that's why she did nothing to stop Kushida from cheating. Something that almost expelled her because there was a trick question in which she fell and got 98. An error that shows that it still has to grow.

Suzune went to confirm her bet with Kushida, deducing that both had calculated the score they needed to achieve. Suzune secured a score of 98 points, while Kushida admitted that she had barely reached 80, thereby granting Suzune the victory. In an attempt to build a more amicable relationship, Suzune extended her hand, but Kushida stepped away instead of accepting it.

Long Term Planning -

Planning Intricacies -
      1. Layer 1: Pairing
      1. Suzune analyzes the rules of the special exam to neutralize the risk of expulsions in Class D. She strategically pairs the highest-achieving students with the weakest (such as integrating the "three idiots" with key tutors) to raise the overall average and put in place a safety net against expulsions.
      1. She establishes two tutoring groups headed by herself, Hirata, and Yukimura, covering all sides of the class to ensure active learning.
      2. Layer 2:  Study groups with Ichinose
      1. With the arrival of exams, the library was almost full, with only about 10% of the seats still available. First-year students couldn’t sit wherever they wanted, as was common in many schools, where space was implicitly divided between upper and lower-year students. Seats by the windows with a nice view and those near the free drinks in the cafeteria were considered prime spots reserved for senior students. In this territorial division, the only area accessible to first-years was the noisy section near the entrance, where they could easily be spied on by Class C members.
      1. she used the Ichinose study group as a visual barrier and proposed a joint study group.
      2. She also ensured that the tutors were from Class B, using the Feynman technique, which involves explaining what has been learned to someone else. By teaching others, knowledge is reinforced, and understanding deepens.
      3. Layer 3: Restricting Options and Channeling the Enemy
      1. Suzune understands that Kushida will inevitably betray the class. Instead of trying to block her completely (which would lead to unpredictable sabotage), Suzune manipulates the environment to force Kushida to betray in one specific way:
      1. She assigns Kushida the supervision of the two study groups in the class, keeping her under close watch surrounded by students.
      2. She explicitly forbids her from participating in the preparation of the exam questions (a task reserved for her, Hirata, and Yukimura).
      3. Since she doesn't have access to the official exam and is busy, the only way for Kushida to help Ryūen within the exam rules is to write her own alternative questions and submit them to Class C.
      4. Layer 4: Trap with Sae
      1. Anticipating that Kushida would write a fake exam and try to submit it to the school on behalf of Class D:
      1. From the first day of the preparation period, Suzune sends a message to Sae demanding that she only accept questions submitted personally by her.
      2. Suzune deduces Sae's inner desire to see Class D advance (based on her behavior since Sudō's trial in Y1V2) and makes her a silent accomplice.
      3. She asks Sae that, if anyone else (Kushida) tries to submit an exam draft, she should appear to formally accept it but secretly invalidate it, keeping Suzune's draft as the only official one.
      5. Layer 5: The Must-Bet
      1. To stop Kushida's continued betrayals in the long run, Suzune formulates a personal challenge intertwined with the general examination:
      1. She offers Kushida the choice of the subject of the duel. This asymmetrical concession makes the wager irresistible to Kushida.
      2. She brings in the former Student Council President as a neutral and binding witness. This psychologically binds Kushida: she knows that Suzune would never break a promise in front of her brother because of the impact on her reputation.
      3. By accepting the bet in Mathematics, Kushida finds herself cornered into asking Ryūen for help to obtain the real answers to the exam created by Class C.
      6. Layer 6: Indirect Checkmate to Class C
      1. The deepest layer of the plan connects Kushida's betrayal with the tactical destruction of Class C:
      1. Thanks to months of tutoring provided by Suzune to the worst students in Class D, her class's average grade surpassed that of Class C.
      2. Ryūen is confident that Kushida will give him the official Class D exam in exchange for giving him the Class C math exam.
      3. Since Sae canceled Kushida's questions and presented Suzune's real questions, Class C would be studying for a fake exam.
      4. If Class C answers the wrong questions while Class D takes the exam correctly, the point gap would result in the mass failure and expulsion of Class C students in pairs.
      7. Layer 7: Theatricalization
      1. On the exam submission deadline, Suzune carries out the final phase of psychological confirmation:
      1. She walks alongside Ryūen to the teachers' lounge to hand in the exams simultaneously.
      2. Sae pretends to give her an indirect warning that "someone had already handed in questions." Suzune feigns putting two and two together in real time, falling to her knees in feigned despair in front of Ryūen.
      3. Ryūen and Kushida are completely convinced that the sabotage worked. Believing themselves victorious, they make no last-minute changes and don't suspect the trap, sealing their own defeat.
      4. During the pre-duel confrontation, Suzune calls Kiyotaka from her pocket without looking at the phone, allowing him to overhear the entire negotiation without Kushida noticing.

Planning Aim -
      * Suzune identified Kushida's motive and set up a scenario where Kushida's only chance of winning was to accept a math bet overseen by Manabu. By previously restricting Kushida's access to class questions through two study groups and an explicit prohibition against writing questions, Suzune narrowed Kushida's options down to one path: creating her own questions and passing them to Ryūen.
      * By securing the agreement with Chabashira-sensei from day one, Suzune guaranteed with 100% accuracy that any attempt by Kushida to substitute the Class D exam would be dismissed without Kushida even noticing.

Planning Process -
      * To solve the problem of the risk of expulsion due to the pairing system, Suzune designed an organizational structure that mitigated the blind spots of Class D:
      * She realized that the task of providing support couldn't fall solely on her. She organized three study groups, led by herself, Hirata, and Yukimura, to reach all the struggling students.
      * She devised the exam strategy by targeting Class C, calculating that the overall academic average of Class D already exceeded that of Class C.
      * The main problem identified within the plan was Kushida's presence as a double agent willing to collaborate with Ryūen to force Suzune's expulsion. Suzune resolved this vulnerability by manipulating Kushida's timing and abilities:
      * She assigned her the task of supervising two study groups simultaneously. This kept her constantly occupied and surrounded by classmates, physically restricting her ability to act covertly.
      * She explicitly forbade her from participating in the creation of the exam questions (a task reserved for Hirata, Yukimura, and Suzune).
      * By denying her access to the official class questions and overloading her with tutoring, Suzune forced Kushida into only one path of permissible betrayal according to the rules: creating her own set of questions and handing them over to Ryūen.
      * Knowing that Kushida would attempt to deliver her own exam sheet to the school on behalf of Class D, Suzune resolved the delivery channel problem through a prior maneuver with the class tutor:
      * From the first day of the exam, Suzune spoke with Sae and made her promise to only accept questions handed to her. If anyone else (Kushida) tried to hand in a set of questions, Sae was to pretend to accept it but ignore it during the official submission.
      * To organize this, Suzune resolved the uncertainty surrounding Sae's neutrality by correctly deducing that Sae desired Class D's advancement more than she let on.
      * To consolidate the neutralization of Kushida, Suzune organized a controlled confrontation that forced the traitor into a defined arena of competition:
      * She offered Kushida the freedom to choose her exam subject from among the eight available options, giving her a psychological advantage and encouraging her to accept the bet.
      * She introduced Manabu Horikita as a neutral and unflappable witness. This not only gave the bet legal validity but also used his brother's reputation as a safeguard: Kushida knew that Suzune wouldn't break a promise in front of Manabu, thus eliminating any suspicion of cheating on Suzune's part.
      * During the confrontation with Kushida, she dialed Kiyotaka's number from memory, with her hand in her pocket, ensuring that he heard the entire interaction in real time, thus keeping him as a witness/safety net without breaking the tension of a face-to-face encounter.
      * To resolve Ryūen's interference, Suzune coordinated a visual deception performance at the time of the final delivery:
      * She waited until the last minute to hand in her questions in the teachers' lounge, knowing Ryūen would act similarly.
      * In front of Ryūen, Sae and Suzune staged a false alarm. Sae pretended to warn him that "something had happened to the questions," and Suzune fell to her knees on the floor, feigning despair and pretending to have just discovered Kushida's betrayal.
      * Ryūen left completely convinced that his sabotage had worked and that Class D would receive the altered exam. Confident, he made no last-minute changes, leaving Class C in a critically vulnerable position with the real exam prepared by Suzune.

Planning Preparation Process -
      1. Internal Structuring of Class D
      1. The Paper Shuffle pitted classes against each other in pairs by having students create and take exams they themselves had designed. Suzune aligned Class D's preparation by focusing on minimizing the risk of expulsion for the lowest-performing students (such as the trio of Sudō, Ike, and Yamauchi).
      1. Suzune designed an internal matching system that paired students with the highest grades with those at risk of failing.
      1. Instead of allowing students to choose based on social affinity, he structured the pairings so that the margin of error for weaker students would be offset by the high scores of their peers.
      2. This tactic ensured that Class D had a solid foundation before facing the attack from Class C.
      2. She created two main study groups led by the academic pillars of the class: Hirata and Yukimura.
      1. Optimize learning time by dividing students according to their specific weaknesses.
      2. This division served as the perfect framework for integrating Kushida without raising initial suspicion, channeling her social energy into group management while restricting her access to critical exam content.
      3. Suzune made a deal with Ichinose for joint study groups to be kept secret from the students of Class C.
      1. This also improved the effectiveness of the study groups.
      2. She avoided espionage by Class C
      2. Control and Isolation of the Traitor
      1. Suzune identified Kushida as a critical threat to the class's existence. Knowing that Kushida would seek her expulsion by collaborating with the enemy, Suzune chose not to pursue her expulsion, but instead to control and limit her methods of sabotage, forcing her to operate within predictable parameters.
      1. Suzune assigned Kushida to work actively in both study groups (Hirata's and Yukimura's).
      1. By keeping Kushida constantly occupied and surrounded by colleagues, he restricted her room for maneuver and prevented her from acting covertly.
      2. She established a strict rule: only she, Hirata, and Yukimura had the authority to write the questions for the Class D exam. Kushida was explicitly forbidden from participating in the drafting.
      1. Reducing Kushida's betrayal options to a single logically predictable path: independently creating a fake quiz game and trying to deliver it to the school or passing it on to Ryūen.
      3. Containment Net
      1. Knowing that Kushida would try to submit unauthorized questions to the school to harm Class D, Suzune anticipated this from day one by building an institutional lock.
      1. Suzune contacted Sae Chabashira from the beginning of the preparation period to establish an agreement:
      1. Chabashira was only to accept as valid the questions submitted directly by Suzune.
      2. If anyone else attempted to submit a draft, Chabashira was to pretend to receive it but never process it.
      4. The Straight Bet
      1. With Kushida's sabotage options cornered, Suzune moved into the offensive phase to neutralize Kushida's hostility in the long term.
      1. Dial Kiyotaka's number from your pocket to have a real-time audio witness.
      1. She kept Kiyotaka informed in real time without revealing her presence to Kushida, ensuring a backup witness.
      2. Suzune issued a direct challenge: if Suzune achieved a higher grade on the final exam, Kushida would have to cease all sabotage against the class. If Suzune lost, she would voluntarily resign from the school.
      1. To ensure Kushida would take the bait, Suzune gave her the advantage of choosing the exam subject.
      2. This apparent advantage lulled Kushida into a false sense of security, and she immediately turned to Ryūen for the Class C exam answers, falling right into the trap Suzune had set.
      3. To prevent Kushida from breaking the promise in case of losing, Suzune involved her brother Manabu as a witness to the agreement.
      1. Manabu's presence acted as an ironclad guarantee for Kushida; she knew that Suzune would never break an agreement signed before her brother because of the reputational weight it carried, which made the trap completely believable to Kushida.
      5. Counter-Attack on Class C
      1. The final phase of the plan aligned Class D's academic victory with a direct blow to Ryūen's strategy.
      1. Suzune analyzed the historical performance and habits of Class C. Knowing that they had a lower academic average and were not seen studying in the library, she deduced that Ryūen would resort to cheating and swapping exams.
      2. On the last day of exam submissions, Suzune waited until the last moment to hand over the official version of the questions to Chabashira in Ryūen's presence.
      1. Suzune and Sae executed a rehearsed simulation. Sae issued a feigned warning, and Suzune acted as if she had just discovered Kushida's "betrayal," falling to her knees in feigned despair.
      2. Ryūen believed his infiltration plan had worked perfectly. Assuming victory, Ryūen withdrew without making any last-minute adjustments, leaving Class C completely unprotected against the real exam prepared by Class D.

Planning Coverage -
      * Before addressing the individual trap against Kushida or the confrontation with Ryūen, the first layer of cover for the plan focused on the structure of the Paper Shuffle exam:
      * Suzune analyzed the cross-exam pairing rules between classes. She knew that pairing the weakest students with the strongest was vital to ensuring a high average and avoiding expulsion below the minimum threshold.
      * To maximize the class's pass rate, she organized two main study groups led by herself, Hirata, and Yukimura. This arrangement mitigated the risk of weaker students not receiving individualized attention.
      * Suzune strictly restricted who was allowed to write the set of questions that Class D would send to Class C. She explicitly forbade Kushida from participating in question creation, limiting that task to trusted tutors (Hirata, Yukimura, and herself). This addressed a critical potential for sabotage: preventing a traitor from writing ridiculously easy or off-topic questions to benefit the rival class.
      * The library where they studied forced them to be in a position where the students of Class C could easily spot them. Suzune made a deal with Ichinose for joint study groups that would remain hidden from the students of Class C. This also improved the effectiveness of the study groups.
      * Suzune did not design her plan in a vacuum, but based it on Ryūen's documented behavior in previous volumes:
      * At the leadership meeting, Suzune insisted on directing Class D's attacks against Class C, arguing that they were the most academically approachable class. However, the true purpose of this decision was to provoke Ryūen into action. Suzune knew that Ryūen would respond by seeking a way to sabotage the class and would use Kushida as his pawn.
      * Observing that Class C didn't use the library or hold formal study groups, Suzune deduced that Ryūen wouldn't focus on honest study, but rather on obtaining the exam answers from Class D through leaks.
      * The cover story of Suzune's plan put Class C in direct checkmate. If Class C blindly trusted the fake questions Kushida would give them (believing them to be the official ones) and studied only those answers, its students would receive catastrophic grades when Suzune's real exam was administered. This allowed her to cause a mass expulsion of Class C without violating any school rules.
      * Kushida's containment represents the core of Suzune's tactical cover, reducing her avenues of sabotage to a single, predictable, and controllable path:
      * To neutralize Kushida's ability to act independently, she was assigned as a tutor to both study groups simultaneously. This kept her under constant social surveillance and exhausted her operationally, preventing her from maneuvering on her own.
      * By prohibiting her access to the official exams created by the tutors, the only remaining way for Kushida to help Ryūen was to create her own set of questions and give it to Sae Chabashira, claiming it was the official Class D exam.
      * Suzune anticipated this move and interfered with the official channels from day one. She sent a message to Sae making her promise that she would only accept the question sheet delivered directly by Suzune. If anyone else (Kushida) attempted to deliver an exam, Sae was to pretend to receive it but never process it. Suzune based this on her previous analysis that Chabashira deeply desired Class D to be promoted to Class A (deduced from Sudō's trial).
      * To definitively neutralize Kushida's hostility, Suzune designed the individual Mathematics exam bet with a closed level of legal and institutional cover:
      * Suzune voluntarily offered Kushida the advantage of choosing the exam subject from among the eight available options. Since Suzune generally had better grades, giving Kushida control of the subject boosted her confidence to accept the bet.
      * If Kushida lost, she wouldn't be expelled; she would simply have to stop sabotaging the class and actively cooperate to achieve Class A. If Suzune lost, she would voluntarily leave the school. This asymmetry mitigated the risk of losing Kushida's social skills to the class.
      * To prevent Kushida from breaking the verbal agreement or attempting to alter the conditions after losing, Suzune brought Manabu as a neutral witness. This mitigated two risks:
      * Kushida couldn't back down under the authority of the former Student Council President.
      * Kushida knew that Suzune would never break a promise made in front of her brother because of the impact on the family's reputation.
      * When confronting Kushida alone to propose the deal, Suzune dialed Kiyotaka from her pocket without looking at the keypad. This ensured that Kiyotaka heard the entire conversation in real time, mitigating the risk of Kushida distorting the facts or later claiming she was threatened or cornered without any witnesses present.
      * The completion of the planning covered the confirmation factor that Ryūen and Kushida would need to consider their sabotage successful:
      * Suzune waited until the last minute of the deadline to submit her official questions to the teachers' lounge, anticipating that Ryūen would be there either watching or submitting his own questions.
      * In front of Ryūen (and Kiyotaka), Chabashira acted out a veiled warning that "someone else had already submitted questions," and Suzune pretended to realize Kushida's sabotage at that moment, falling to her knees in despair.
      * This performance masked the risk of Ryūen or Kushida suspecting a trap at the last minute. Convinced that Suzune had been defeated and emotionally broken, Ryūen withdrew without attempting to verify the legal validity of the submitted form.

Planning Versatility -
      * Suzune turns a simple administrative task—submitting the class's prepared questions to the teacher—into a tool for traps and psychological manipulation. She uses the exam papers not only to win against Class C, but also as bait to corner Kushida and Ryūen.
      * When confronting Kushida to settle the bet, he dials Kiyotaka's number from memory, blindly, with his hand in his pocket. This turns his phone into a live microphone, tactically using an everyday object to make Ayanokōji a passive auditory witness without alerting his rival.
      * Restriction and Filtering of Selected Information:
      * To neutralize internal sabotage, she explicitly forbids Kushida from touching or writing the exam questions, limiting that task solely to trusted tutors (Hirata, Yukimura, and herself).
      * She conceals from the entire class (including Kiyotaka and Kushida herself) the secret pact she made with Sae Chabashira on "Day 1."
      * She uses the historical average grades of Class C and Class D, knowing that Class D had improved its performance after months of tutoring the "three idiots." She accurately deduces that Class C wasn't studying in the library and that Ryūen would resort to cheating instead of conventional study.
      * Suzune reads her tutor's psychology, deducing that Chabashira cares more about Class D's promotion than she lets on (based on Sudō's trial in V2) and turns her into a strategic ally. She makes her promise to only accept the question sheet delivered by Suzune in person, neutralizing any attempt at fraudulent submission by Kushida.
      * Instead of seeking Kushida's expulsion, she uses the figure of her brother—the former Student Council President—as a validation tool.
      * Kushida knows that Suzune would never break a promise in front of her brother due to the reputational damage it would cause.
      * She demonstrates enormous conceptual versatility in redefining the traitor. Instead of eliminating her (which would weaken the class), she decides to "tame" her and assign her dual tutoring roles to keep her busy, surrounded by people, and without room to maneuver.
      * She anticipates Ryūen's blackmail methods and designs the environment so that Ryūen believes he is manipulating the situation, when in reality he is walking straight into a trap that would have cost him expulsions from Class C.

Planning Risk Calculation -
      * She accepts a bet where her own continued enrollment in school is at stake (if she loses, she expels herself).
      * She grants Kushida the advantage of choosing the subject from among the eight available. Suzune calculates that, under purely academic conditions, her level in Mathematics is almost unbeatable for Kushida.
      * By cornering Kushida into seeking the exam answers through Ryūen, Suzune leaves no room for doubt that Kushida will obtain the actual Class C exam. By ensuring through Chabashira that the Class D questions could not be altered by Kushida, the risk of Kushida gaining an unfair advantage was reduced to absolutely zero.
      * In the initial phase of the Paper Shuffle, the biggest risk for Class D was that the students with the worst grades (Sudō, Ike, Yamauchi) would be paired with each other or against deadly exams from other classes.
      * Suzune organized the tutoring system to raise the overall academic level of the class. This ensured that, regardless of the pairings provided by the system, no student would be left unprepared for a particularly difficult exam.
      * Suzune calculated that Ryūen would seek the path of deception instead of studying, due to the low academic level of Class C.
      * If Ryūen helped Kushida by giving her false or manipulated answers to beat Suzune, Class C would be studying with a syllabus that didn't match the actual exam Suzune had given to Chabashira.
      * This meant that Class C would fail en masse due to the syllabus discrepancy, risking the expulsion of multiple pairs in Class C.
      * Suzune took the risk and acted defeated in the teachers' lounge at the last minute of the exam distribution in front of Ryūen. This dramatic act ensured that Ryūen wouldn't change her questions at the last minute, sealing Class C's fate without exposing Class D to any real danger.
      * To prevent Kushida from going back on her promise not to sabotage the class if they lost, the risk calculation included Manabu. Manabu's moral authority ensured that the cost of breaking the deal for Kushida would be the complete destruction of her social standing, something Kushida values ​​above all else.

Planning Contingency -
      * Suzune did not leave this to chance; she used the knowledge gained from Sudō's trial (Y1V2) about Sae's true personality and her underlying desire for Class D to progress, ensuring unwavering institutional support.
      * By limiting Kushida's avenues of betrayal to a single predictable path (asking her own questions and passing them on to Ryūen), Suzune eliminated chaotic variables and guided her enemy to act exactly as she needed.
      * Manabu not only acted as an unquestionable neutral authority, but his reputation also served as a psychological lock: Kushida knew that Suzune would never break a promise in front of her brother so as not to tarnish his image, and at the same time Kushida was bound with no possibility of backing down or lying about the terms.

Planning Invincibility -
      * To ensure that Kushida accepted the challenge without suspecting a trap, Suzune gave her an overwhelming advantage: allowing her to choose the exam subject from among the eight available courses.
      * Suzune knew that her grades were generally superior. By giving Kushida the choice, she forced her to seek Ryūen's help in obtaining the leaked math answers. This tied Kushida's bet to the trap Suzune had already set with Chabashira.
      * Suzune thoroughly analyzed the Class C options for passing the exam:
      * Suzune analyzed Class C's background and confirmed that there were no study groups in the library. Furthermore, thanks to months of tutoring the trio of weak students, Class D had a higher average, putting Class C at a disadvantage in a fair competition.
      * It was an open path for both classes, but the average advantage of Class D remained.
      * Leaking/Cheating: This was Ryūen's preferred method.
      * If Ryūen helped Kushida by giving her the answers to the Class C exam in exchange for the questions for the Class D exam, she would fall into a devastating trap. When Kushida's questions were rejected by Sae, Class D would take a different exam, one they had actually studied for, while Class C would receive the real syllabus prepared by Suzune, resulting in a massive failure rate and multiple expulsions in pairs. Ryūen would be cornered with no way to win through fraudulent means.
      * To prevent Ryūen from suspecting that her plan with Kushida had been intercepted, Suzune waited until the last moment to deliver the question set in the teachers' lounge.
      * In Ryūen and Sae's presence, Suzune and the teacher performed a rehearsed act. Sae pretended to warn her that something was wrong, and Suzune feigned a dramatic collapse, falling to her knees as if she had just discovered Kushida's betrayal. This completely convinced Ryūen and Kushida that their plan had worked, preventing them from attempting any last-minute maneuvers before the deadline.
      * The plan was not limited to the trap; it encompassed the comprehensive preparation of the class:
      * Suzune consulted with Ichinose to understand the leadership dynamics and management of the class unit.
      * At the leaders' meeting, she insisted on designating Class C as the target under the argument that they were the most easily executable rival, when her real goal was to channel Ryūen's aggressions towards the territory she already controlled.
      * She arranged the tutoring sessions with Hirata and Yukimura, ensuring that the underperforming students were paired and covered by the stronger ones, neutralizing the risk of internal failure while executing the external trap.

Planning Accuracy -
      * Suzune didn't see tutoring simply as "support classes," but as a tool for geographical and operational control. By dividing Class D into two main study groups headed by tutors (Hirata, Yukimura, and herself) and assigning Kushida to both groups, she ensured a dual purpose: raising the average grades of vulnerable students to narrow the gap with Class C and, simultaneously, filling Kushida's schedule.
      * The pairing structure for Class D was designed to neutralize the risk of paired expulsion. By strengthening the low-achieving students in the preceding months, Class D drastically reduced the margin of vulnerability, forcing Class C to resort to cheating rather than fair academic competition.
      * Before launching the attack against Kushida, Suzune identified a crack in her own resolve: she was troubled by the idea of ​​a classmate plotting against the class out of a personal grudge. She sought out Ichinose to understand how to lead and treat everyone fairly. This "emotional/philosophical" component connected directly with the tactical component: not seeking Kushida's expulsion, but rather her taming and transformation into an asset/weapon for Class D.
      * Suzune mathematically reduced Kushida's infinite forms of betrayal to a single possible path: creating her own questions, passing them to Ryūen, and obtaining from Ryūen the answers to the exam prepared by Class C in order to win her personal bet.
      * The final detail in the teachers' lounge demonstrates impeccable dramatic precision. She waited until the last minute to hand out the questions for Class D. Together with Sae, they staged a performance where the teacher hinted at a problem with the questions and Suzune feigned a breakdown of despair. This resonated with Ryūen's psychology; he believed their plan had worked and that the questions Kushida had switched were the official ones. Ryūen left satisfied, causing Kushida to also disengage from the scheme, believing herself victorious.
