      * MTC Suzune plan (Y1V8):
Chabashira announced a new special exam. After listening to the explanation and read the document, Suzune reached her own conclusions. (Short Term Planning)

The first thing she did upon understanding the situation was to listen to Hirata and his lifeline proposal. She also sent a message to Kiyotaka to ask if he had anything in mind, but he denied it. (Planning Formulation)

Since boys and girls would be separated by gender, the girls who usually relied on Hirata would feel lost. Aware of this, Suzune accepted the role of leader for the girls in this exam.

She understands her position in the class: even though she is respected, the girls will not approach her even if they have a problem. For this reason, Suzune asked Kushida to be her sub-leader. Kushida has great social influence and is well-liked by everyone, so having her by her side would be a great advantage. Additionally, this would also serve as another step in Suzune’s plan to earn her trust. (Planning Coverage) (Planning Risk Calculation)

Suzune passed the exam with ease, leading her group ensuring their safety at all times. Additionally, she made sure that no group with a member from Class C was put in danger, further solidifying her strategy and control over the situation. Thanks to her alliance with Ichinose, who facilitated Suzune work (Planning Aim)

      * ESE Planning (Y1V11):
Planning Formulation - Suzune listens to the exam rules and quickly realizes that five out of the ten events are mere decoys. She concludes that some events are simple "bluffs" designed to confuse the opponent, as the events are selected randomly. During the Q&A session, Suzune uncovers hidden details about the exam system, such as only five of the ten registered events being official, with the rest serving to distract the opponent. She also understands how the events and participants are selected.

Step 1: The Commander's Goal

Suzune deduces that it is essential for Kiyotaka to become the class commander, as he is the best option to lead and protect the class from potential expulsion due to his protect point. To achieve this, Suzune manipulates the situation with Sudou and Ike, redirecting attention towards Kiyotaka and manipulating the 38 students in Class C to follow her plan.

Step 2: Preparation for the Competition and Information Control

After naming Kiyotaka as commander, Suzune organizes a meeting with the class to plan their strategy for the events. She explains that they must be prepared for any scenario and assigns tasks to ensure the students think of events where they can excel, taking complete leadership without Hirata's involvement.

To prevent leaks, Suzune organizes a communication group using mobile phones and assigns Kushida as an intermediary. She ensures that information does not fall into the wrong hands.

Step 3: Selecting the Rival

Suzune initially plans for her class to face Class D, as they have no leader and would be an easy challenge. However, due to the class vote, they end up facing Class A, a much greater challenge that ruins her initial plans. Despite this, Suzune quickly adapts and adjusts to the new situation.

Step 4: Event Analysis and countermeasures

Suzune studies Class A's event proposals and selects those most suitable for Class C, aiming to secure victories in events where her class has a higher chance of winning, and avoiding academic events where Class A has a clear advantage. She also creates strategies for key events, using the individuality of her students to her advantage.

Step 6: Resolving Hirata's and Koenji Situation

Suzune decides to exploit Koenji's arrogance to her benefit. She provokes him into committing to win any event, hoping that if he loses, the class will see him as a potential threat in future votes. However, this does not work as expected.

And Suzune decides not to involve Hirata too much due to his unstable emotional state. Although she tries to motivate him, she considers him a risk, so she leaves the responsibility to Kiyotaka, who ultimately provides the necessary support.

Step 7: Assignment of Tasks and Roles

Suzune ensures that every class member has a task assigned and that the events they possibly participate in align with their strengths.

In subsequent meetings, Suzune continues to adjust the plans with the class. She ensures that everyone is aware of the strategy and potential event variations, so they can adapt to any changes.

Step 8: Development of the Plan During the Events

As the events progress, Kiyotaka applies Suzune's strategies, using his classmates effectively. Despite some setbacks, such as defeats in certain events, Suzune ensures that Kiyotaka has all the information he needs to face Class A effectively.

With this plan, Suzune maximized the talents and abilities of the members of Class C, almost countering Class A. If Kiyotaka had not been defeated by Tsukishiro's intervention or if Koenji had not become lazy, Class C's victory would have been certain.

Short Term Planning -
      * When Sudō questioned why Kiyotaka had received 42 points of praise on the previous exam, Suzune intervened with a calculated lie, claiming that Kiyotaka didn't know the reason. She reduced the whole thing to a scheme by Sakayanagi to expel Yamauchi, diverting attention and protecting Kiyotaka's peaceful life by labeling him "just someone who runs fast."
      * She capitalized on the collective guilt over Yamauchi's expulsion and the class's rejection of the proposal to expose an average student to position Kiyotaka as commander. She argued that the class could plan everything in advance and give Kiyotaka precise instructions so he only had to follow the established strategy, minimizing the risk to the class.
      * Suzune immediately deduced that 5 of the 10 events to be recorded were simple bluffs or decoys to mislead the opponent, uncovering hidden rules through strategic questions to Sae.
      * She assigned all her classmates the task of proposing events where they could excel individually or as a team. This helped her not only to submit official proposals but also to thoroughly analyze each student's area of ​​expertise.
      * Anticipating that Class A would try to limit Class C's participation, she planned formats such as mandatory rotations every 10 minutes (e.g., in volleyball) to force more students to get involved and keep everyone prepared.
      * She cornered Kōenji through his own arrogance at the class meeting, manipulating him into claiming he could win any event. This left him exposed in front of the class, forcing an implicit compromise to avoid being targeted in future expulsion exams.
      * Upon noticing that Hirata was in a state of depression following Yamauchi's expulsion, Suzune coldly and logically decided not to consider him as an initial key asset to prevent the strategy from collapsing, leaving his indirect management in the hands of Kiyotaka.
      * She rejected the idea of ​​putting guards at the door to prevent spies and created a group chat using Kushida as a discreet intermediary to share information without drawing attention in the hallways.
      * Suzune structured the Class C events (English, basketball, archery, swimming, tennis, table tennis, typing, soccer, piano, and rock-paper-scissors) to minimize the influence of Class A's superior academic ability and corner her opponents into technical niches that left them no room to maneuver.

Long Term Planning -
      * Suzune had been training Sudō for months not only in academic and athletic aspects, but also to maintain emotional control in situations of high pressure or enemy provocation.
      * The development of the analysis notebook with comprehensive class data was not improvised. Suzune had been building a detailed database of each student's weaknesses and hidden talents since the formation of study groups in previous volumes. This proved useful to her later on. During dinner at her apartment, Suzune presented Kiyotaka with the processed analysis of the class, showing him with exact metrics who should compete in which area (for example, identifying Matsushita's hidden abilities in mathematics or Sotomura's geeky skills).

Planning Intricacies -
      1. Layer 1: Commander Instrumentalization
      1. Faced with the class's suspicions about Kiyotaka's 42 points of praise, Suzune took control of the classroom before the tension escalated. She conveniently lied, claiming that Kiyotaka "didn't know the reason" and attributing it all to a ploy by Sakayanagi to get Yamauchi expelled. She diminished Kiyotaka's abilities in front of the class by labeling him "someone who's just fast."
      1. She used this scenario to pressure the class and force Kiyotaka to run for Commander. In doing so, she ensured a leader with a protection point who absorbed the risk of expulsion, protected Kiyotaka's peaceful life by directing attention to Sakayanagi, and established the framework that the Commander would only have to "follow the instructions planned by the class."
      2. Layer 2: Audit, Skills Mapping and Data Collection
      1. She distributed copies of the SAE regulations to ensure general understanding and instructed each student to present the disciplines where they had individual or group advantages.
      1. This wasn't just to select the 10 official events, but to conduct a thorough mapping of the strengths and weaknesses of the entire class. Suzune personally compiled and analyzed this data (which she later presented to Kiyotaka during dinner at his apartment) to create accurate performance records for each student.
      3. Layer 3: Counterintelligence and Secure Communication Protocol
      1. Given Yukimura's concern about possible spies in the hallways, he rejected the use of guards, considering it a waste of resources and identifying the hallway as a public space.
      1. She channeled communications through mobile phones, creating a private chat group and involving Kushida as an intermediary. This served both to maintain the discretion of the information and to keep Kushida monitored/occupied.
      4. Layer 4: Event Engineering
      1. Suzune deduced that 5 of the 10 events presented were mere bluffs designed to disorient the opponent.
      1. To prevent the class from becoming complacent and to counteract large Class A events, he designed specific rules (e.g., games with a 30-minute time limit and mandatory rotation every 10 minutes in basketball or volleyball), forcing the active participation of more than half of the class.
      2. She avoided purely academic events in her choices and selected highly specialized disciplines where they had unbeatable "students/experts" (Sotomura in Typing, Miyake in Archery, Sudō in Basketball) to minimize the bias of the top academic average of Class A.
      3. She registered "Rock, Paper, Scissors" as a last resort event in case circumstances turned unfavorable.
      5. Layer 5: Management and Control of Unstable Assets
      1. Since the exam required mandatory partner assignments or individual participation, Suzune publicly cornered Kōenji at the meeting. She used his arrogance to make him declare that he "could win any event in front of him," creating an implicit threat: if Kōenji failed to perform or sabotaged the exam, the class's discontent would make him the primary target for expulsion in subsequent exams (such as the Class Poll).
      2. Noticing Hirata's emotional breakdown, she tactically isolated him in her calculations so as not to depend on him as a pillar in the active strategy, preventing a breakdown in the middle of the exam from ruining the planning.
      6. Layer 6: Tactical Assignment Double Safety Net
      1. Faced with the possibility that the machine would select two written exams, he wrote two guides for Kiyotaka:
      1. Option A: Distribute the most capable students equally to ensure victory in both exams.
      2. Option B: Strategically sacrifice one exam by concentrating all heavy resources on the second to guarantee a victory (an option successfully implemented by Kiyotaka).
      2. She assigned Kōenji because of his high ability, but aware of his unpredictability, she placed Matsushita Chiaki with headphones as a backup because of his mental speed in mathematics.
      3. She had trained Sudō's composure for months beforehand. When Sakayanagi tried to use provocations to unsettle him during the basketball event, Sudō regained control and led his team to a 24-16 victory.
      4. She learned and mastered chess from scratch in just one week, reaching an estimated tactical level between 1100-1600 ELO (surpassing Hashimoto, a player with months of preparation trained by Sakayanagi), forcing Sakayanagi herself to intervene directly to stop her progress.

Planning Aim -
      * Suzune's first major objective was to deflect the attention and social pressure that had been placed on Kiyotaka after he received 42 points in the previous exam (Class Poll). She manipulated the class with a calculated lie (attributing everything to a plan by Sakayanagi to expel Yamauchi) and forced the situation so that the class had no choice but to accept Kiyotaka as commander. With this she achieved three goals in one:
      * She protected Kiyotaka's peace of mind by allaying suspicions about his true abilities.
      * She prevented a mediocre student from risking expulsion in case of defeat.
      * She convinced the class by arguing that the commander would only have to follow a pre-designed and detailed plan created by them.
      * Suzune quickly deduced from Sae's explanation that 5 of the 10 recorded events must be "decoys/bluffs" to mislead the enemy before the random draw.
      * She managed to define events that neutralized the disadvantages of Class C compared to the academic excellence of Class A:
      * Physical/Specialized Events: Basketball (with Sudō), Archery (Miyake), and Typing (Sotomura).
      * Class A Events: FMA (with Matsushita and Kōenji) and Chess (where she trained herself in a single week with Kiyotaka at his request).
      * The strategy drafted by Suzune for the 7 events was so precise that Class C managed to go 4-3 in actual wins against Class A (the strongest class of the year), proving that the operational objective of the planning was mathematically winning and superior under normal conditions
      * The plan failed to achieve total victory solely due to two unpredictable and uncontrollable variables beyond Aim's direct control:
      * Kōenji's intentional lack of cooperation during the Flash Mental Arithmetic exam.
      * The external interference of Acting Director Tsukishiro, which altered the final result of the chess match between Kiyotaka and Sakayanagi.

Planning Process -
      * Given Hirata's deep depression following Yamauchi's expulsion, Suzune coldly assessed his condition and decided not to consider him a key asset in the initial phase to avoid overburdening him, leaving the emotional support to Kiyotaka after acknowledging her own social limitations. Once Hirata recovered, she immediately reintegrated him into the process.
      * When Yukimura raised the risk of Class A spying on them in the hallways, Suzune refused to spend resources posting guards. Instead, she reorganized the communication channel using a group chat with Kushida as a discreet intermediary to prevent information leaks.
      * During the proposal meeting, she manipulated Kōenji's arrogance into publicly declaring that he could win any event. This created a social trap: if Kōenji failed or sabotaged the exam, the class would have full justification to make him the next target for expulsion.
      * Suzune compiled information from the entire class and organized comprehensive instruction notebooks for the commander (Kiyotaka) to execute in real time during the exam:
      * Basketball Strategy (Event 1): She initially fielded a reserve team (Makida, Ike, Hondō, Onodera) to keep the score close and saved Sudō as a trump card mid-game through the commander's intervention. She anticipated Class A's attempts at frustration and had trained Sudō's composure for months prior to the exam.
      * Typing and Archery Strategy (Events 2 & 6): She directly assigned individual specialists (Sotomura and Miyake), limiting the opposing commander's ability to intervene and preventing Sakayanagi from influencing the outcome.
      * Branching Strategy for Written Exams (Events 3 & 4): She prepared two alternative routes in case there were two written exams:
      * Route 1: Distribute talents evenly to try to win both.
      * Route 2: Deliberately sacrifice one and concentrate the best students on the other to ensure a guaranteed victory (the option chosen by Kiyotaka in the written exam).
      * Strategy for FMA (Event 5): She positioned Matsushita Chiaki with headphones as a backup in case Kōenji refused to actively compete, demonstrating organizational foresight in the face of staffing shortages.

Planning Preparation Process -
      1. Environmental Control
      1. During the briefing with Sae Chabashira, Suzune immediately deduced the nature of the 5 decoy events and extracted critical details by asking questions (participant limits, rotation, rival draws, and the Commander's role).
      2. Neutralizing Suspicion and Creating a Political Shield for Kiyotaka
      1. When Sudō questioned Kiyotaka's 42 points of praise, Suzune immediately seized control of the narrative, interrupting the conversation.
      2. She calculatedly lied, claiming that Kiyotaka "didn't know the reason" and attributed it all to a ploy by Sakayanagi to expel Yamauchi.
      3. She minimized Kiyotaka's value to the class, reducing him to "someone who just runs fast," and asserting that Sakayanagi would never have given him a protection point if he were a genius.
      4. She used this narrative to convince the class to appoint Kiyotaka as Commander. This protected the mediocre students from expulsion and ensured that the Commander role was in the hands of the most suitable person, all under the premise that he would "only follow the instructions drawn up by the class."
      2. Data Collection
      1. Instead of imposing random events, Suzune distributed pamphlets with the rules and instructed each student to present the disciplines where they had individual or collective advantages.
      2. She used this data collection not only to design the 10 events, but to audit in detail the hidden or secondary abilities of the entire Class D. For example, she identified that Onodera had acceptable skills in basketball even though his strength was swimming, and she detected Matsushita's mental speed in mathematics.
      3. She refused to post guards at the door so as not to draw attention in a public hallway. Instead, she created a group chat using Kushida as an intermediary to discreetly filter and exchange information without raising suspicion of leaks to Class A.
      3. Logical Alignment of the List of 10 Events
      1. Suzune carefully selected the 10 events for Class D: English, Basketball, Archery, Swimming, Tennis, Table Tennis, Typing, Soccer, Piano, and Rock-Paper-Scissors.
      1. She avoided purely theoretical or academic events where Class A had an overwhelming advantage, focusing on events where individual or specialized talent could counteract the overall average of Class A.
      2. She registered "Rock, Paper, Scissors" as a last resort, neutralizing it, dependent on chance in case the odds were against her.
      3. Although she considered the piano (where she estimated a 60% chance of winning), she accepted Kiyotaka's suggestion to train in chess. In just one week of intense preparation, she raised her level from zero to surpass Hashimoto (the star chess student of Class A, trained by Sakayanagi for years).
      4. Strategic Role Assignment and Contingency Coverage
      1. In her preparation, Suzune assigned specific profiles to each event, anticipating Sakayanagi's responses and eliminating single points of failure:
      1. Basketball:
      1. She lined up Minami, Ike, Hondō, and Onodera.
      2. Suzune knew Sakayanagi would try to frustrate Sudō from the start. However, she had trained Sudō for months to maintain emotional control under provocation. Her predetermined strategy was to keep Sudō in reserve and introduce him with the Commander's power during the critical phase of the match, turning the score around (24-16).
      2. Typing and Archery:
      1. For the Typing test (1v1), she assigned Sotomura ("The Professor") because it was his absolute specialty. She designed the rules to give him the entire field and restrict the opposing Commander's ability to intervene as much as possible, effectively nullifying Sakayanagi.
      2. She applied the same logic in Archery, supporting Miyake's sheer ability.
      3. Class A Written Tests
      1. Option A: Distribute the best students evenly between both tests to try to win both.
      2. Option B: Deliberately sacrifice one written test and concentrate all the top performers on the other to ensure one guaranteed victory.
      4. FMA Strategy
      1. She knew that Kōenji's voluntary cooperation couldn't be relied upon. Therefore, she aligned him with Matsushita, recommending that the latter use headphones to block out the noise and focus. Matsushita would act as a safety net in case Kōenji decided not to compete or sabotaged the event.
      5. Preventive Management
      1. At the initial meeting, she publicly manipulated him into declaring to the class that he could win any event. This cornered him: if he failed or refused to perform, it would increase the collective distrust against him, making him the perfect target for future expulsions from exams like the Class Poll.
      2. She acknowledged that Hirata was emotionally collapsing after Yamauchi's expulsion. Rather than forcing him and turning him into a "ticking time bomb," Suzune decided not to count him as a key asset in the initial planning stages, letting Kiyotaka manage his recovery without compromising the exam schedule.

Planning Coverage -
      * She questioned the teacher to uncover implicit rules (random selection of 7 official events out of the 10 presented, participation limits per student, victory/defeat conditions in class points and tie-breaking rules).
      * She sensed an inconsistency in the exam rules from the first day, which later became the loophole Tsukishiro used to intervene, demonstrating an analytical cover that bordered on predicting external sabotage.
      * She designed Kiyotaka's defense not only to protect his peaceful life, but also to logically corner him into the position of Exam Commander. She argued to the class that using Kiyotaka as commander was optimal because it didn't risk expulsion for a brilliant student and because the class would predefine all the strategies, which he would then simply have to execute to the letter.
      * She assigned all students the task of proposing events based on their individual/group skills. This served a dual purpose:
      * Identifying competitive events against Class A
      * Investigating in depth the hidden specializations of each classmate.
      * Given Ike's concern that few would participate, Suzune explained how to structure the rules to force the participation of more than half the class and avoid disinterest.
      * In the Mental Arithmetic event, she assigned Kōenji based on his ability, but placed Matsushita Chiaki with backup headphones, knowing beforehand of Matsushita's unrevealed mathematical prowess.
      * In Basketball, she structured the lineup so that the starters could withstand the pressure and used the Commander's power to bring Sudō in at the critical moment of greatest pressure.

Planning Versatility -
      * Knowing that Kōenji wouldn't cooperate in regular meetings, Suzune cornered him publicly during the only meeting he attended. She manipulated him through his own arrogance into declaring that he could win any discipline, trapping him in his own logic: if he failed, the class would mark him as the next expulsion target in a future "Class Poll."
      * Seeing Hirata deeply depressed after Yamauchi's expulsion, Suzune coldly assessed him as a "ticking time bomb" and readjusted the plan to avoid relying on him as a primary asset. She refrained from directly pressuring him to prevent exacerbating his isolation and delegated his containment to Kiyotaka. Once Hirata recovered, she immediately reinstated him to the lineup for the written test event.
      * When Yukimura warned her about potential Class A spies in the halls, Suzune refused to spend resources posting guards. Instead, she used cell phones, creating a private chat group and placing Kushida as the intermediary to discreetly relay information.
      * In Typing, he selected Sotomura ("The Professor") because it was his area of ​​otaku specialty, isolating the event to a 1v1 without giving Sakayanagi room to interfere as commander.
      * In Archery, she entrusted the event entirely to Miyake due to his previous experience.
      * In Basketball, she combined players like Makida and Onodera (whose main specialty was swimming, but who performed well in basketball) to maintain the game until Sudō's strategic entry.
      * She prepared two parallel strategy notebooks in case there were two written exams:
      * Strategy A: Distribute capable students evenly between both exams to compete for both victories.
      * Strategy B: Intentionally sacrifice one exam and concentrate all academic resources on the other to secure a decisive point.
      * When Kiyotaka suggested they play chess, Suzune learned the game from scratch in just one week. During the game, she not only maintained tactical control of the board, managing Hashimoto's aggressive and invasive play, but she also read her opponent's verbal psychological warfare and anticipated his mind games, completely cornering him before Sakayanagi intervened.

Planning Risk Calculation -
      * Appointing a mediocre student as Commander meant taking an extremely high risk of expulsion for the class if they lost. Appointing a strong asset was also risky.
      * Suzune assessed the social pressure on Kiyotaka after he received 42 points in praise on the previous exam. Instead of letting the class corner him, she created a "calculated lie," attributing everything to a plot by Sakayanagi to expel Yamauchi. By publicly downplaying Kiyotaka's abilities ("just someone who runs fast"), she lessened the class's suspicion about his true skills and simultaneously maneuvered the situation to force him into the role of Commander. In doing so, she protected the mediocre students from expulsion and ensured Kiyotaka was in the safest strategic position without compromising his status.
      * When Yukimura suggested using Katsuragi as a spy in Class A, Suzune quickly assessed Katsuragi's personality and determined that the strategy would be ineffective and difficult to execute accurately, discarding it to avoid wasting resources.
      * Assessing the risk of Class A or spies passing through the hallway outside the classroom, he refused to post guards (considering it a waste of resources) and instead set up a discreet channel through a chat group using Kushida as an intermediary.
      * When evaluating the academic events for Class A, she determined that competing directly against them on GPA was a lost cause. Therefore, she selected events or settings where the weight of victory rested on individual specialists or skills not influenced by overall GPA.
      * She assessed that sending Sudō from the beginning would expose him to provocation and frustration from Class A tactics. She calculated to use a base team and kept Sudō as a resource for the Commander's intervention when the situation became complicated.
      * She assessed that, being a 1v1 battle, she should give Sotomura ("The Professor") the full field of play without much intervention from the Commander, allowing him to exploit a unique ability that surpassed Class A. The same applied to Miyake.
      * Suzune calculated the risk of facing Hashimoto's offensive style. She assessed that Hashimoto would attempt mind games and slow-invasion maneuvers; by remaining calm and avoiding reflexive piece sacrifices, she managed to completely outplay a player with months of training.

Planning Contingency -
      * She knew Kōenji had the potential to win, but recognized that he was unpredictable and might refuse to compete or not take the event seriously. As a backup, she included Matsushita in the event wearing headphones, knowing he had a quick mind for mathematics, thus ensuring an alternative if Kōenji failed.
      * She identified that Hirata was deeply depressed after Yamauchi's expulsion, making him a risk to the class. Determining that she couldn't rely on him as a key asset, she devised a strategy without him as a central figure. She also anticipated that Sakayanagi might use large-scale events to force Hirata's participation, so she had previously asked for his assistance in such situations and delegated the management of his emotional state to Kiyotaka.
      * Anticipating that less motivated students (like Ike) would look for excuses not to train, she envisioned scenarios of modified rules (e.g., mandatory rotations in volleyball) to force the participation of more than half the class and prevent the lack of preparation from taking them by surprise.
      * Anticipating that the machine might select written exams (where Class A had an advantage), Suzune drafted and prepared two opposing strategies in her notebooks for the Commander to execute based on the selected sequence of events.
      * She included the "Rock, Paper, Scissors" event among the 10 registered as a last resort in case the exam conditions became completely unfavorable, relying on a neutral event with no academic advantage for Class A.
      * To prevent Kiyotaka's inexperience from ruining the test if he chose to remain passive, she designed the plan so that the class could plan everything in advance and give him precise instructions on how to intervene in each event, guaranteeing the predictability of the outcome without depending on his real-time decision-making.

Planning Invincibility -
      * Knowing that Kōenji is a chaotic and uncooperative individual, Suzune publicly cornered him during the meeting, forcing him to arrogantly declare that he would win any event in front of her. This ensured that if Kōenji intentionally failed, he would become the primary target for expulsion from the class (as in the Class Poll), psychologically cornering him into competing or, at the very least, not sabotaging the event. During the Flash Mental Arithmetic event, she placed him next to Matsushita Chiaki as a backup, wearing headphones in case he decided to ignore the instructions.
      * She anticipated that Sakayanagi would try to emotionally destabilize Sudō during the basketball game. Suzune had already trained him for months to maintain his composure under pressure. Furthermore, she devised the rule to use the commander's authority to introduce Sudō only when the game entered its decisive phase, maximizing his impact without exposing him prematurely.
      * Given Yukimura's concern about spies in the halls, Suzune refused to spend resources on physical guards and established an encrypted communication channel/chat group, using Kushida as a front to divert attention and keep the ten events selected by Class C secret.
      * In anticipation of the Class A exams, Suzune prepared two notebooks of opposing strategies for the Commander.
      * Suzune managed to construct such a solid plan that she secured a genuine 4-3 advantage against Class A (the physically and academically strongest class in the year). The only cracks that prevented an outright victory stemmed from factors impossible for a student to foresee: Kōenji's complete lack of cooperation at her event despite threats, and the direct and unlawful interference of Acting Principal Tsukishiro.

Planning Accuracy -
      * Basketball: Designed specifically around Makida's ability (as a fake ace) and Onodera's versatility, reserving Sudō for the Commander's final twist. The result was exactly as predicted: a final score of 24-16 in favor of Class C.
      * Typing: She identified Sotomura (The Professor) as an absolute specialist in a 1v1 confrontation. The precision of the detail was in structuring the rules to limit the enemy commander's ability to intervene as much as possible, letting Sotomura's sheer skill secure the point.
      * Archery: Same level of accuracy when isolating Miyake in a direct 1v1 where the academic advantage of Class A was completely nullified.
      * Flash Mental Arithmetic: She assigned Matsushita Chiaki after assessing that she possessed a quick mathematical mind (keeping her as a backup for Kōenji), demonstrating a thorough knowledge of her class's hidden talents.
      * Suzune capitalized on the social pressure on Kiyotaka (due to his 42 commendation points) to manipulate the class narrative. She fabricated the explanation that it was all a ploy by Sakayanagi to expel Yamauchi, protecting Kiyotaka's low profile and logically forcing his nomination as Class Commander so that no other student would be at risk of expulsion.
      * She prepared all the information for the class and gave it to Kiyotaka in summary form during a dinner at her apartment, giving him precise instructions but allowing him freedom of execution within the planned limits.
      * To counter the advantage of Class A, Suzune took on the responsibility of learning chess in just one week.

Linear Thinking -
      * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Y1V11.5-now): Suzune doesn't try to "fix" the class all at once. Her progress follows a clear timeline where each phase depends on the previous one:
      1. Identify the initial condition (Class D = zero points, chaos).
      2. Avoid immediate losses (expulsions, penalties).
      3. Raise the minimum academic standard to survive.
      4. Stabilize leadership.
      5. Only then, seek sustained growth.

      * Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6): Suzune demonstrates linear thinking in the plan's macro structure, which follows a clear order. Nothing is done out of sequence; she doesn't attempt to choose an opponent before understanding the system or implement groups before legitimizing her leadership. Her system follows this order:
      1. Understand the exam
      2. Infer the pairing system
      3. Choose the opponent
      4. Prepare the class
      5. Execute
      6. Neutralize threats

      * Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10):
      1. Understand the structure of the Class Poll.
      2. Evaluate whether there is a "no expulsions" option.
      3. Confirm that the system will force a specific outcome.
      4. Observe the formation of groups without intervening.
      5. Identify the true traitor.
      6. Prepare yourself emotionally to point the finger at someone.
      7. Expose them publicly with evidence.
      8. Close off all avenues of counterattack.

      * ESE Planning (Y1V11): There are no impulsive leaps. Each action occurs when the context allows it:
      1. Listen to rules without emotional intervention.
      2. Deduce the basic structure of the exam.
      3. Confirm hypotheses by asking Sae questions.
      4. Detect hidden rules.
      5. Secure information (photo of the board → Kiyotaka).
      6. Solve the commander's problem.
      7. Organize the group.
      8. Plan events → allocate resources → execute.

      * Everything revolves around elevating the collective, not individual brilliance.

      * Paper Shuffle plan (Y1V6): Once Suzune concludes that "Couples will be decided by ranking," she never abandons that conclusion. The entire plan—distribution of scores, groups of ten, control of extremes—is developed from that single premise.

Even when she loses votes (as the opponent), she doesn't restructure the core of the plan, she merely adapts.

      * PM3: Hirata, who usually relies on consensus and stability, accepted the breakup quietly without trying to publicly mend the relationship.
      * GC1: The breakup was not caused by internal romantic conflict, but by a planned realignment.
      * PM4: Kiyotaka is a Hirata friend
      * PM5:  Kei consistently acts on instructions or shows subtle alignment with decisions that ultimately benefit Kiyotaka's hidden objectives.
      * GC2: Kiyotaka holds direct leverage and personal trust over both Hirata and Kei, allowing him to manipulate their social dynamic without causing class-wide chaos.
      * PM6: Kiyotaka consistently avoids open leadership roles, preferring to operate through influential proxies in Class D.
      * PM7: Severing Kei’s dependency on Hirata directly shifts her operational reliance entirely onto Kiyotaka.
      * GC3: Kiyotaka actively restructures interpersonal relationships in the class to build his own confidential network of operatives.
      * GC4: The breakup between Hirata and Kei was orchestrated or facilitated by Kiyotaka.

Traps Categories
Setting Traps -
         * False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2): To achieve this, Suzune uses Ichinose and Kiyotaka to corner Ishizaki, Komiya, and Kondo, arguing that the school was already aware of their lies and that the trial was merely a test to observe how they would react. This strategy was designed to apply psychological pressure and force them to withdraw their complaint out of fear of expulsion.

All of this was supported by the existence of alleged security cameras, which gave credibility to the deception. In doing so, Suzune caught them in their own lie and achieved her objective. The feat is literally the construction of a physical and psychological trap. Suzune designed a closed environment (the special building at 3:40 PM, at 25-30°C without air conditioning) combined with false information (the simulated camera) to conceptually trap the Class C students with no option for escape.

         * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She set a misinformation trap for Ryuuen by subtly suggesting that Ayanokouji was the manipulative figure behind her triumphs, diverting suspicion from the VIP's identity and Suzune's true defensive intentions.

         * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6):  Suzune, drawing from her past experiences (sports festival) understands that Kushida will attempt something similar in the new special exam (Paper Shuffle). Consequently, she begins to restrict Kushida’s freedom of movement by assigning her major responsibilities during the study sessions, where she can be directly monitored by Suzune herself (and Kiyotaka) in the first session, and by Hirata in the second.

With Kushida’s options severely limited, Suzune deliberately offers her a path to achieve her objective: Suzune proposes a wager in which she puts her own expulsion on the line. Kushida, having no other viable route, focuses entirely on defeating Suzune through this method.

Suzune is fully aware that both of them acknowledge her clear academic superiority, and therefore anticipates that Kushida will resort to cheating to secure her victory. Based on Kushida’s previous alliance with Ryuuen, Suzune predicts that she will employ the same strategy again. As a countermeasure, Suzune acts in advance by speaking with Chabashira and having her pretend to receive Kushida’s exam papers. This ensures that Suzune’s class gains a higher chance of victory in the special exam due to the false information Kushida submits, while also giving Kushida a false sense of security that negatively affects her final performance.

The outcome is Kushida’s defeat and her forced submission to Suzune, while simultaneously inflicting damage on Ryuuen’s class.

         * Expose Yamauchi (Y1V10): She trapped Yamauchi and Kushida in a social and verbal trap in front of everyone. By giving them the theoretical option of voting for her as "judge," she left Yamauchi with no room to justify himself and forced Kushida to withdraw his support for the dissident group to save his own reputation.

         * ESE planning (Y1V11): At the basketball event, she anticipated and trapped Class A by foreseeing the tactics they would use to try to stop Sudou, preparing the solution in advance to neutralize the enemy commander's interference.

Personal ranking for Suzune Setting Traps by feats:
Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6) > False Cameras Strategy (Y1V2) > Food Trap (Y1V1)

Trap Evasion -
         * Sudou Trial (Y1V2): Ryuuen wants to test how the school reacts to physical incidents between classes and all kinds of cheating, so he uses Ishizaki, Komiya and Kondo to set a trap for Sudou, taking advantage of his poor self-control. This leads to the school’s intervention through a trial against Sudou, which could result in a temporary suspension and a loss of class points. Suzune manages to devise a plan to avoid a negative outcome for the class: she saves Sudou, prevents the loss of class points, solidifies an alliance with Class B, and identifies the mastermind of Class C using minimal resources.

         * Kiyotaka False Persona (Y1V1-Now): Kiyotaka wishes to live a peaceful and ordinary life. To achieve this, he uses method acting and assumes the role of a “normal student”, someone who struggles to make friends, is weak around beautiful women, and possesses virtually no abilities that stand out above average.  (His second persona)

Despite the numerous tests Kiyotaka carries out over several months to reinforce his act, Suzune is able to realize that he is indeed capable of social development, but simply chooses not to; that he possesses exceptional self-control, and that he is a student with noteworthy abilities. This does not remain at a superficial level. By the second month, Suzune had already identified Kiyotaka’s “true nature” (His third persona), someone who views people as tools and does not genuinely care about his “friends,” remaining in the shadows to preserve his peace of mind. But she didn't stop there; she was able to see through that final mask to the real Kiyotaka, who was confusing, and contradictory. This made her question his past and the reasons behind his actions.

         * Trap Kushida Strategy (Y1V6): Suzune completely neutralized the expulsion plot hatched between Kushida and Ryuuen. By rendering invalid any questionnaires sent to Sae by third parties, she thwarted her enemies' attempt to trap her before it could harm her.

         * Expose Yamauchi. (Y1V10): She completely neutralized the trap orchestrated externally by Sakayanagi (Class A) and executed by Yamauchi. The trap aimed to covertly expel the class's most valuable asset (Ayanokoji) by accumulating votes of no confidence, but Suzune detected the maneuver and dodged the blow, redirecting it toward the triador and expelling him.

Personal ranking for Suzune Trap Evasion by feats:
Kiyotaka False Persona (Y1V1-V2) > Sudou Trial (Y1V2) > Ryuuen's interrogation (Y1V4) > Invitation to the Student Council (Y1V7.5) > Fortune Teller (Y1V4.5) > Counter Operation friendship (Y1V1)
